<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001600 -->
# R12b Profile C: two completed analytical attempts, no closure

**UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Grant 07d388b77ab325df. Original Goal OPEN.**

No full PSD certificate and no actual complete-word counterexample to Profile C were obtained. Two concrete attempts were completed: an asymmetric scalar projection-envelope trajectory with an explicit proposed saturated recursion, and an explicit dense cyclic interpolation matrix adapted to the prescribed covariance weights. The first proposed trajectory is infeasible for every fixed unequal pair of caps at all sufficiently large enclosing depths. The second matrix satisfies every required coefficient constraint, but its FULL remainder has a strictly negative central diagonal at every enclosing depth in the task. A flow argument also excludes acyclic coefficient graphs at sufficiently large depths. These statements have their stated scopes only; none excludes arbitrary cyclic matrices or disproves the fixed Profile C functional.

## 1. Locked problem, sources, and status

Read the complete OPERATOR.md, original PROBLEM.md, R12_AB_COUNTEREXAMPLE_HANDOFF_20261009.md, R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md, R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md, and brownian_dominance_certificate_20261009_r12/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. Also read the complete R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md, including Section 5. Its old positive-cone exclusion is not used here outside its hypotheses. The mathematical-academic-writing skill and its mathematical-integrity reference were read for notation and evidence discipline. The initialized marker is present. No private worker memory was read.

Checked source SHA-256 values:

- TASK.md: baf0f77863d9b4d9b1cdf9bc2c69b8617c8f452a9f1a5a19dcb9d47b5a883e60.
- R12_PROFILE_C_ADDENDUM_20261009.md: 9001df1d3b8ecdbc198d8be2e85657610263fc93fdf8fd50f3e295f058e58030.
- R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md: 0fcebe8f462bc029b6a9cf45a1d83abf10854b3f4de386b4bc37d5a01e8d95f7.
- brownian_dominance_certificate_20261009_r12/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md: 77b64f3633cccd091e262ef49833539bec4bf49aaac9fdb03dd201df94a2d3f1.

The original question concerns lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1, where the exact all-integer Zhang--Jiang denominator is B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. It concerns all predetermined nonnegative schedules and all finite-dimensional convex L-smooth functions with an attained minimum, at the actual last iterate and original selected minimizer/radius. W=2R. A certified strict full-class subsequence improvement would refute that limit. The certificate weight Bcert below is not B_n.

Fix ONE enclosing integer m>=1536, and throughout every child use

    c0=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=c0(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).

Write s_j=sum H_j, k_j=a c^j, n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=s_m, and T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. The FIXED Profile C is

    Bcert=1+(32/17)S,
    rho_i=1-T_i/(8S),           0<=i<=n,
    u_i=rho_i h_i,              i<n,
    U=sum_(i<n)u_i,
    u_n=Bcert-U.

In particular rho_0=1 and rho_n=7/8. Set ell_i=h_i for i<n and ell_n=u_n/rho_n. There is no physical terminal step ell_n. All u_i are positive: rho_i>=7/8, and U<=S<Bcert.

Let b_i=u_i for i<n and b_n=-U. Let Bmat have diagonal 1 and entries (Bmat)_ij=h_j for j<i. The covariance and exact target remainder are

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j[min(rho_i,rho_j)-rho_i rho_j],
    R(C)=K+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)
         -(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T).                       (1.1)

For a zero-row-sum C with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums b, the source identity on actual normalized smooth-convex trajectories is

    D_u=sum_(i,j) C_ji Q_ij + g^T R(C)g,             (1.2)

where

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
    Q_star,i=-2(f_i-f_star)+2<g_i,x_i-x_star>-||g_i||^2,
    D_u=||x_0-x_star||^2-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2Bcert(f_n-f_star)
        -||x_0-x_star-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.

The Q rows are nonnegative. Thus an actual D_u<0 would exclude every admissible full-PSD C. In contrast, a negative free-vector direction of R for one constructed C excludes only that C. This distinction applies to Section 4 below.

## 2. Shared exact hierarchy formulas

Put alpha=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]. In the parameter range here, 0<alpha<1. Indeed c(c-2)>1 at c>c0, while beta<1. Summing the word gives

    s_j+1=alpha c^j+(1-alpha)2^j.                    (2.1)

The symmetric scalar starts from the prior draft are the exact scalar sequence

    X_1=beta,
    X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),  j>=2.

Only its algebraic recurrence is needed in Section 3. For

    d(c)=(c-1)/(c+1),
    P=alpha c d(c), Q=2(1-alpha)/3, R0=beta-P-Q,

its exact solution is

    X_j=P c^(j-1)+Q 2^(j-1)+R0(-1)^(j-1).          (2.2)

To check the leading coefficient, (s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)) has c^(j-1) coefficient alpha(c-1); P(1+1/c)=alpha(c-1). Its 2^(j-1) coefficient is 1-alpha, giving Q(1+1/2)=1-alpha. The last coefficient enforces X_1=beta.

The enclosing-m values of c lie in the fixed compact interval [c0,c0(1+1/6144)], and alpha is bounded away from zero. Equations (2.1)--(2.2) give, uniformly for those parameters,

    X_j/s_j=d(c)+O((2/c)^j),
    (s_j+beta-1-a)/s_j=1+O(c^(-j)).                 (2.3)

The implicit constants can be chosen independently of enclosing m: all coefficients in (2.1)--(2.2) are bounded on that compact interval, alpha has a positive lower bound there, and s_j is bounded below by a constant times c^j for all sufficiently large j. Consequently (2.3) can be used at j=m-1 and j=m-2 in the SAME enclosing word. No child's parameters are refreshed.

## 3. Approach 1: an explicit unequal-cap critical construction fails

### 3.1 Fixed actual function and the proposed recursion

Fix A>0 independent of m. Consider the one-dimensional function

    f_A(x)=x^2/2,             -A<=x<=1,
           x-1/2,             x>=1,
           -A x-A^2/2,        x<=-A.

Its derivative is clip(x,[-A,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz. The function is convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth, with unique original minimizer 0. At every x,

    -2 f_A(x)+2x f_A'(x)-f_A'(x)^2=0.               (3.1)

Thus a correctly realized trajectory would have Q_star,i=0 at every query. The construction below never changes this function or its minimizer.

We tried to generalize the old complete critical trajectory by defining positive and negative starting magnitudes

    X_1^+=beta, X_1^-=A beta,
    L_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1),
    X_j^+=L_j-X_(j-1)^-,
    X_j^-=A L_j-X_(j-1)^+,           j>=2.           (3.2)

The proposed positive task H_j at X_j^+ would execute its entire left child and bridge with gradient +1 and land at -X_(j-1)^-. The proposed negative task H_j at -X_j^- would execute its entire left child and bridge with gradient -A and land at +X_(j-1)^+. The right child would recurse with opposite orientation. At the base, the starts lie inside the quadratic interval and the actual one-step outputs are -a beta and +a A beta, respectively. There is no declaration that the higher-level saturated execution is valid.

The REQUIRED saturation inequalities follow at the bridge query, because the left-child total step mass is s_(j-1):

    positive orientation: X_j^+>=s_(j-1)+1
                          iff X_(j-1)^-<=k_(j-1),
    negative orientation: X_j^->=A(s_(j-1)+1)
                          iff X_(j-1)^+<=A k_(j-1). (3.3)

If these inequalities hold, the same inequalities at earlier left-child queries follow since their physical prefix mass is at most s_(j-1). If they fail, the proposed bridge query lies outside the region for the claimed saturated gradient, so the proposed entire saturated itinerary cannot be actual. This does not determine the true bridge query after an earlier departure. This is an actual projection constraint, not an interpolation table supplied without execution.

### 3.2 Exact solution and the incompatible top-level conditions

Adding and subtracting (3.2) gives

    X_j^++X_j^-=(1+A)X_j,
    X_j^+-X_j^-=(1-A)(s_j+beta-1-a).               (3.4)

For the second identity, its base value is (1-A)beta and

    sum_(l=2)^j L_l
      =alpha(c^j-c)+(1-alpha)(2^j-2),

which together with (2.1) yields (3.4). Hence these are exact formulas for the proposed starts, even when they are not feasible starts for the intended itinerary.

Uniformly in the literal enclosing parameters, (2.3)--(3.4) yield, at j=m-1 and j=m-2,

    X_j^+/s_j -> p=(c0-A)/(c0+1),
    X_j^-/s_j -> q=(A c0-1)/(c0+1),
    k_j/s_j -> c0-2=r,       r=sqrt(2)-1.           (3.5)

For a proposed positive root at depth m, the root's left-child/bridge requires X_(m-1)^-<=k_(m-1), so its limiting requirement is q<=r. Its negative right child at depth m-1 requires X_(m-2)^+<=A k_(m-2), so its limiting requirement is p<=A r. Since c0=2+r and c0 r=1, these two requirements simplify exactly to

    q<=r  iff A<=1,
    p<=A r iff A>=1.                               (3.6)

For every fixed A>1, the first difference in (3.6) is strictly positive in the wrong direction, so the positive root bridge is infeasible for every sufficiently large enclosing m. For every fixed 0<A<1, the second difference is strictly positive in the wrong direction, so the negative right-child bridge is infeasible for every sufficiently large enclosing m. This uses uniform errors at the two actual top depths and does not assume that a fixed internal-depth limit describes the enclosing word.

Thus no fixed unequal cap A permits this particular complete alternating saturated recursion for all large m. If the proposed starts become nonpositive in some parameter range, that is an additional failure and cannot repair (3.6). At A=1 the proposed recursion reduces to the old symmetric critical trajectory, which the supplied Profile C addendum already calibrates positively at both parities. We do not count replaying it as a Profile C obstruction.

### 3.3 Exact scope of the failed actual-function attempt

The actual function f_A is legal. The attempted higher-level trajectory table (3.2) is not legal for any fixed A!=1 at all large depths. Therefore it supplies no endpoint, no D_u sign, and no counterexample to Profile C. The exclusion does not cover A depending on m, trajectories with interior phases, different dwell patterns, other scalar functions, or higher-dimensional projection envelopes. No such alternative trajectory is claimed in this packet.

The parent independently supplied the same unequal-cap feasibility guard while this calculation was in progress. Equations (3.2)--(3.6) reconstruct its needed recurrence and enclosing-m justification here. This is not independent verifier acceptance.

## 4. Approach 2: actual dense cyclic coefficients and their full-matrix failure

### 4.1 A diagonal identity and why causal flows cannot work

For ANY admissible C and a physical query i, define its backward and forward outgoing masses

    P_i=sum_(j<i) C_ij, F_i=sum_(j>i) C_ij.

Zero row sums and the actual Bmat give

    (C Bmat)_ii=-P_i+(h_i-1)F_i.

Since K_ii=u_i(h_i-u_i), the COMPLETE remainder diagonal is

    R_ii=u_i(2-u_i)+2P_i-2(h_i-1)F_i.              (4.1)

The covariance is already included in (4.1). In particular, a necessary condition for full PSD at a large physical bridge is

    P_i-(h_i-1)F_i >= u_i(u_i-2)/2.                 (4.2)

No sign is imposed separately on R-K.

Here is a checked obstruction to the causal subcase, supplied as a diagnostic within this coefficient approach. Regard C_ij, i!=j, as directed flow from i to j. Its net incoming minus outgoing mass is b_i. If its positive-edge graph is acyclic, choose a topological ordering. At the cut just after a node i, no positive edge enters the prefix. The total outgoing flow from the prefix is minus its sum of b-values, which is at most U because n is the only node with negative b and b_n=-U. Every outgoing edge of i crosses that cut. Therefore

    P_i+F_i<=U.                                    (4.3)

This also follows from source-to-sink path decomposition; (4.3) does not assume that the topological order agrees with chronological query order. Since every physical h_i>1, equations (4.1)--(4.3) imply

    R_ii<=u_i(2-u_i)+2U.                           (4.4)

At the actual middle query k=2^(m-1)-1, put s=s_(m-1) and hmid=1+k_(m-1). Then S=2s+hmid, T_k=s. Formula (2.1) gives, with the original enclosing parameters,

    s/S -> r,
    hmid/S -> r^2,
    u_k/S -> (1-r/8)r^2>0,
    U<=S.                                         (4.5)

Consequently the right side of (4.4), divided by S^2, has strictly negative limit superior. Every admissible acyclic C fails full PSD for all sufficiently large enclosing m. This includes dense directed acyclic graphs, not just adjacent or lower-triangular flows. It does not include graphs with circulations. The parent provided the acyclic-flow guard, and the topological-cut proof above checks it.

### 4.2 An explicit law with circulations and exact coefficients

To test a construction outside that excluded class, use the prescribed ell and b to set

    E=sum_(i=0)^n ell_i=S+ell_n,
    q_i=ell_i/E,
    p_i=ell_i-b_i,
    C=p q^T-diag(p).                               (4.6)

These are ACTUAL closed-form coefficients, without a solver or unproved recurrence. In particular,

    p_i=(1-rho_i)h_i>=0,          i<n,
    p_n=ell_n+U>0.

All q_i are strictly positive, sum q_i=1, and sum p_i=E because sum b_i=0. Thus every row of C sums to zero, all off-diagonal C_ij=p_i q_j are nonnegative, and the i-th column sum is

    (sum_j p_j)q_i-p_i=ell_i-p_i=b_i.               (4.7)

For all i>=1, p_i>0, so the subgraph on indices 1,...,n has genuine two-way cycles. This construction is not rejected merely by the acyclic result above.

### 4.3 The zero covariance row at the original query

Since rho_0=1, K_0j=0 for every j. Here p_0=0, so the entire initial row of C vanishes. Because h_0=1+a<2 and u_0=h_0,

    R_00=h_0(2-h_0)>0.                             (4.8)

The zero row of K does NOT imply a zero row of R. For j>0, row sums and Bmat give

    (C Bmat)_j0=(1-h_0)C_j0,
    R_0j=(h_0-1)C_j0=(h_0-1)p_j h_0/E.             (4.9)

Thus the degenerate covariance boundary is included explicitly, without an invalid inverse of K or an assumption that the entire residual row vanishes.

### 4.4 A strictly negative central diagonal of the FULL R

For physical i, set Q_before=sum_(j<i)q_j and Q_after=sum_(j>i)q_j. From (4.6),

    H_ii=(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T)_ii
         =2p_i[(h_i-1)Q_after-Q_before].            (4.10)

At the middle query k, the palindromic physical word and the terminal algebraic weight yield

    Q_before=s/E, Q_after=(s+ell_n)/E,
    p_k=s hmid/(8S)>0,
    H_kk=(2p_k/E)[(hmid-1)(s+ell_n)-s].             (4.11)

For all task depths, hmid>3: m-2>=1 and

    k_(m-1)=beta c^(m-2)>=beta c
            =c0(1-epsilon^2)>2.

For the last strict inequality, c0>12/5 and epsilon^2<1/6 imply c0(1-epsilon^2)>2. Therefore the bracket in (4.11) is

    (hmid-2)s+(hmid-1)ell_n>0,

so H_kk>0. Moreover s/S<1/2, hence rho_k>15/16 and

    u_k>(15/16)hmid>45/16>2.

Including the whole covariance diagonal exactly,

    R_kk=K_kk+u_k(1-hmid)+b_k-H_kk
         =u_k(2-u_k)-H_kk<0.                       (4.12)

Thus the explicitly constructed cyclic C in (4.6) fails full PSD for EVERY m>=1536. This is a decisive test of the FULL matrix: its quadratic form on the coordinate vector e_k is negative. It neither discards covariance terms nor requires separately nonnegative physical diagonal slack. It excludes this concrete law only. The coordinate vector is not asserted to be an actual smooth-convex trajectory, so (4.12) is not a counterexample to D_u>=0 and not an exclusion of every admissible C.

# Handoff: original-normal projection flows

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN. One analytical invocation completed;
STOP, no automatic renewal. Parent closes allocation `f3573e14d9155ee2`.

Read `REPORT.md` for the complete assumptions, coefficient conventions,
derivations, and witnesses. No new accepted fact or full-class bound.

## What is usable as a draft

For exact U certificates `J+sum lambda_ij Q_ij` that are globally PSD in
the original physical gradients, one auxiliary `p=P(x_0/M)`, and zero:

1. Set `mu_ij=a_j lambda_ij`, with physical `a_j=1` and `a_p=1/M`.
   Exact cancellation forces all nontrivial star rows to zero and
   `out_i-in_i=2(1_(i=n)-1_(i=p))`.
2. Common-gradient contact forces
   `sum_j mu_ij(t_j-t_i)=2h_i` for `i<n`, and zero at `n,p`, with
   `t_i=1+sum_(k<i)h_k` and `t_p=M=1+2sum h`.
3. For positive total mass, `p` is absorbing and its total incoming mass
   is two. Writing `w_i=mu_(i,p)`, `z=sum_i w_i g_i/2` gives the exact
   decomposition `R=M||p-z||^2+J_z+sum_(physical i,j)mu_ij Q_ij`.
   Hence every such PSD certificate requires
   `J_z=2<x_0,z>-M||z||^2-U_1(x_n)>=0` on every legal physical history.
4. The full Dirichlet identity is REPORT (3.3). Its signed memory term
   remains unpaid. The Markov interpretation supplies no PSD result.

These are statements about a specified sufficient conic certificate
class. No necessity for arbitrary true U inequalities and no dual
attainment theorem was proved.

## The two explicit laws and their first decisive obstructions

- Terminal fan, REPORT (5.1):
  `mu_(n,i)=mu_(i,p)=2h_i/(M-t_i)` for `i<n`, and remaining source flow
  goes `n->p`. All weights are nonnegative and satisfy both interface
  equations. The full Schur diagonal is `w_i(1-M w_i/4)`, which is
  negative for every `h_i>=2`. It cannot handle the H3 large bridge.
- Recursive binary tree, REPORT (6.1)-(6.4):
  parent of physical node `i` in H_m is `i+2^v2(i+1)`; use the clock
  conductances there and canonical absorption weights
  `w_i=2h_i/M`, `w_n=2(1+S)/M`. H1 passes analytically; H2 has the
  complete negative assignment `g_2=v`, all other physical gradients
  zero, `p=A v/M_2`, giving
  `M_2 R=[A^2+2C(1-A)]||v||^2<-17||v||^2/12`.

Both are formal PSD obstructions, not legal U counterexamples.

## Stronger global exclusion and quantitative interface

The canonical absorption pattern itself, independently of **every**
physical multiplier and graph topology, would force the whole-word
primitive energy `V=||x_0||^2-||x_n+Sg_n||^2` to be nonnegative. It is
false already for the actual same-m H3.

Use `C=1+S_2`, `k=beta c`, `S=S_3=2C+k-1`, `C_3=1+S`,
`M=1+2S`, `r=k/C`, and `delta=k(2C+k)-C^2>0`. The interval body
`[-r,1]`, start `C`, has left-block gradients one and right-block
gradients `-r`; whole-block inequalities justify this analytically.
Its primitive energy is
`V=-delta(C^2+C_3 k)/C^2<0`.

For **any** single-p original-row PSD certificate, let
`alpha=sum_(i=0)^3 mu_(i,p)/2`. REPORT (8.9) gives

\[
 \alpha-\frac{C+k}{M}\ge
 \frac{\delta(C^2+C_3k)}{2MSk(C+k)}>0.
\]

Thus changing all physical circulations while keeping canonical
absorption cannot work. A future candidate in this certificate class
must change absorption too, and then prove the complete residual PSD.
The inequality is only necessary; no candidate accomplishing this was
constructed. Additional auxiliary projections, different certificate
classes, and direct `17/16` radius certificates remain outside the
exclusion. These possibilities were not opened as extra mechanisms.

# R8a handoff — UNVERIFIED, original target OPEN

Grant `f8af8997e480ca3b` used one invocation and one substantive line: a quantitative audit/attempt of area-based terminal selection for the actual disk pursuit map. Mathematical programs, arrays, solvers, formal submissions, new agents/models, and consult calls: zero. Parent owns the ledger and closeout.

## Outcome

No original-radius lower bound at scale `1/B_n` was obtained. The full inherited-parameter `H_m` lower-witness viability test remains unpassed. No affirmative or negative minimax candidate is available for formal review.

The new result is an explicit **all-depth obstruction to an area-only inference** on the actual inherited `H_m`: a uniformly positive invariant-area sector, with original radius bounded between positive absolute constants and actual reflections, has terminal payoff at most `4^(-2^m)` at every one of its points. This bounds only that sector and does not bound the whole disk family or unrestricted `W`.

## Complete proof location and reconstruction data

Read `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md` in full. It checks the global static function, minimizer/radius, actual recursion, Jacobian, both inverse branches, image-domain restrictions, sign changes, and caustics before the quantitative argument.

Use `F_K(x)=max_(g in B(e1,1)) (<x,g>-||g||^2/2)`, `x=(1-u,v)`, `u>=1`. Its gradient is projection, it is globally convex 1-smooth, its minimum set is the negative horizontal ray, and the original closest-minimizer radius squared is `v0^2`. Put `r=sqrt(u^2+v^2)`.

Actual map and payoff:

    u'=u+h(1-u/r), v'=(1-h/r)v,
    Pi=2(r_n-u_n)/v0^2=2/(r_n+u_n)*(v_n/v0)^2.

The parent Jacobian is correct: `det DT_h=1-h/r`; hence the determinant in `(u,log|v|)` is 1 away from the axis and collapsed circle. The inverse branch is uniquely determined by the sign of `u'-h`. The reflected branch also requires `Rb<h`, and both branches must satisfy original `u>=1`. Full image-domain bounds are given in Section 3. Finite first-hit caustic preimages are null; the constructed sector avoids all of them.

For enclosing `m>=1536`, retain the prescribed `eps=1/(4m)`, `beta=1-eps`, `c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps)`, `a=beta/c` in EVERY child. Let `ell_j=1+a*c^j`, so `H_(j+1)=(H_j,ell_j,H_j)`. Set

    zeta=1/64, eta0=zeta^2/8,
    p0=(ell0+delta)*(cos theta,sin theta),
    eta0*ell0/2 <= delta <= eta0*ell0,
    3/4 <= cos theta <= 4/5, sin theta>0.

Here `u0>1` and `4/5<|v0|<3`. Its invariant area is

    (eta0*ell0/2) * integral_[acos(4/5),acos(3/4)] csc(theta) dtheta,

bounded below by a positive constant independent of `m`.

The first leaf maps it exactly to

    p1=(ell0+delta*cos theta, delta*sin theta).

For all subsequent states, a closed bootstrap gives

    ell0 < r_t < ell0*(1+zeta/2).

Thus every later leaf multiplier has magnitude `<zeta`, and every nonleaf bridge reflects with magnitude `<=c^j`. There are no collapsed states. All sign changes and the preserved area are actual.

The complete physical-word bounds are

    Q_j = zeta^(2^(j-1))*c^(2^j-j-1)
        = c^(-j-1)*d^(2^(j-1)), d=zeta*c^2<1/4,
    every formal prefix product <=1,
    J_j=sum_(all physical steps) h_t*(formal prefix product)^2 <3.

The last inequality follows from `J_(j+1)=(1+A_j)J_j+ell_j*Q_j^2`, `A_j=c^(2j)*Q_j^2`, `sum A_j<1/15`. The bound on `J_j` controls the actual horizontal drift and closes the leaf-multiplier bootstrap. It uses no chosen-spine replacement and no numerical calculation.

The actual final estimate, keeping the original `v0`, is

    Pi <= zeta^2/(64*ell0) * c^(-2m-2)*d^(2^m)
       < 4^(-2^m).

Since `D_m<11*3^m`, this gives

    D_m * sup_sector Pi < 11*3^m*4^(-2^m) ->0.

The supplied accepted `B_n/D_m<47/50` makes the same sector `o(1/B_n)`. This last comparison is conditional only on the already supplied accepted fact; the disk-sector estimate itself does not use the R6 quadratic report.

## The remaining selection gap

For an initial log-height band `A<q0<B`, a claimed payoff upper `<kappa` permits a terminal region of the form

    1<u<U+S,
    q < (1/2)*log[d_kappa*(2u+d_kappa)],
    d_kappa=kappa*exp(2B)/2.

That region has infinite invariant area. A finite-area contradiction therefore needs additional quantitative control of the actual image near `q=-infinity` and of the original `q0`. Nullity of the collapsed circles is insufficient. The explicit sector above demonstrates that even noncollapsed, fixed-positive-area, genuinely reflecting trajectories can all have negligible original-radius payoff on every inherited depth.

Other initial sectors are untouched. A new selection argument might still find a good disk trajectory there. No bound for the supremum over all starts, no passage to all words, and no exact-profile comparison has been obtained. The invariant itself must not be logged as minimax progress.

All requested files are saved. This allocation stops here; no automatic renewal and no unused mathematical or formal credit.

## DOC-001606 — handoff_20261010/materials/frontier/pursuit_area_lower_bound_20261009_r8a/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010\materials\frontier\pursuit_area_lower_bound_20261009_r8a\PROOF_ATTEMPT.md

原始字节数：16069；原始 SHA-256：75d275d42c22e34ce1297d11b6d4f2e54b2c3bce23d342fbc06842bd7e78f2c3

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001606 -->
# R8a: a quantitative obstruction to area-only disk selection

**UNVERIFIED direct research draft.** Grant `f8af8997e480ca3b`: one native invocation, one substantive line (the proposed area/selection mechanism), mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. No additional agents or models. This document contains a manual derivation, not a verifier-accepted fact. The original minimax target remains OPEN.

## 1. Target, result, and scope

The original target is the uniform all-word lower bound

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)\ge(1-o(1))/B_n,
\qquad B_n=2S_\bullet(n)+1,
\]

with the original all-split Zhang--Jiang profile, finite-dimensional convex 1-smooth functions, an attained minimum, the original selected minimizer and radius, and the actual final iterate. Here \(\mathcal W=2\mathcal R\).

This attempt does **not** obtain that lower bound, or even its required disk-witness viability test on the complete inherited-parameter family \(H_m\). It obtains an explicit quantitative obstruction to the proposed measure-selection shortcut. For every enclosing \(m\ge1536\), there is an annular sector of actual initial points with all of the following properties:

1. Its invariant area is bounded below by one positive constant independent of \(m\).
2. Its original radius \(|v_0|\) is bounded above and below by positive absolute constants.
3. Every trajectory is noncollapsed and undergoes genuine reflection at every bridge of level at least 1.
4. Every point in the sector has actual original-radius terminal payoff at most
   \[
   4^{-2^m}.
   \]

In particular, the whole sector has payoff \(o(1/D_m)\), where \(D_m=1+2\sum H_m\). The supplied accepted comparison \(B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50\) also makes its payoff \(o(1/B_{2^m-1})\). The proof is uniform over the whole inherited-parameter family; it is not a finite-horizon example.

This is an obstruction to drawing a terminal lower bound from preserved positive area and the occurrence of reflections. It does not upper-bound the whole disk family, the remaining reflecting region, or the unrestricted function class. Another initial point may still have the required payoff.

## 2. One static admissible function and the original radius

Let \(K=B(e_1,1)\subset\mathbb R^2\) and define the single global function

\[
F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\]

It is convex as a supremum of affine functions. Completing the square gives the unique maximizer \(P_Kx\). Projection is 1-Lipschitz: the two projection variational inequalities imply

\[
\|P_Kx-P_Ky\|^2\le\langle P_Kx-P_Ky,x-y\rangle.
\]

The upper and lower envelope inequalities, together with continuity of projection, give differentiability and \(\nabla F_K=P_K\). Thus \(F_K\) is globally convex and 1-smooth.

Since \(0\in K\), \(F_K\ge0\). Its minimum set is precisely

\[
M=\{(\xi,0):\xi\le0\}.
\]

Indeed, for such an \(x\), every \(g\in K\) has \(g_1\ge0\), so the defining maximum is zero at \(g=0\). If \(x_1>0\), a sufficiently small positive horizontal \(g\) gives a positive value. If \(x_2\ne0\), choose a sufficiently small boundary point of \(K\) whose second coordinate has the sign of \(x_2\); its first coordinate is quadratic in its second coordinate, and the defining value is positive. This also covers \(x_1\le0,x_2\ne0\).

Write

\[
x=(1-u,v),\qquad u\ge1,\qquad r=\sqrt{u^2+v^2}.
\]

Then \(r\ge1\), and projection onto the disk gives

\[
\nabla F_K(x)=(1-u/r,v/r),\qquad F_K(x)=r-u.
\]

For every \(h\ge0\), the actual GD map is therefore

\[
u'=u+h(1-u/r),\qquad v'=(1-h/r)v,
\tag{2.1}
\]

or, with \(p=(u,v)\),

\[
T_h(p)=h e_1+(1-h/r)p.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Since \(u'\ge u\), the region \(u\ge1\) is invariant for every nonnegative word. On its axis \(v=0\), the point is a minimizer and is fixed by every step. The closest original minimizer to \(x_0=(1-u_0,v_0)\) is exactly \((1-u_0,0)\), so the original squared radius is \(v_0^2\), not \(u_0^2+v_0^2\). Consequently the actual normalized payoff is

\[
\Pi_h(u_0,v_0)
=\frac{2(r_n-u_n)}{v_0^2}
=\frac{2}{r_n+u_n}\frac{v_n^2}{v_0^2}
=\frac{2}{r_n+u_n}\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-h_t/r_t)^2.
\tag{2.3}
\]

Every state in this expression belongs to the one actual trajectory. No intermediate radius is independently selected.

## 3. Jacobian, inverse, sign charts, and actual image domain

For \(r>0\), differentiation of (2.2) gives

\[
DT_h=(1-h/r)I+(h/r^3)pp^{\mathsf T}.
\]

Its radial eigenvalue is 1 and its tangential eigenvalue is \(1-h/r\). Hence

\[
\det DT_h=1-h/r=v'/v\quad(v\ne0).
\tag{3.1}
\]

On any chart \(v\ne0,v'\ne0\), put \(q=\log|v|\). The determinant in \((u,q)\) coordinates is

\[
\frac{v}{v'}\det DT_h=1.
\tag{3.2}
\]

Thus the positive measure \(du\,d\log|v|=du\,dv/|v|\) is preserved, with the input and output sign charts retained. Reflection changes the sign chart; it creates no extra multiplicity.

For completeness, suppose \(p'\) has a nonaxis preimage with \(u>0\), and set \(R_b=\|p'-h e_1\|\). The identity

\[
u'-h=(1-h/r)u
\]

determines the branch:

\[
\begin{array}{ll}
u'>h:&r=h+R_b,\quad p=(1+h/R_b)(p'-h e_1),\\
u'<h:&r=h-R_b>0,\quad p=(1-h/R_b)(p'-h e_1).
\end{array}
\tag{3.3}
\]

The second branch requires \(R_b<h\). In either branch the additional condition \(u\ge1\) must still hold. A nonaxis point with \(u'=h\) has no preimage with \(u>0\). The circle \(r=h\) collapses to \((h,0)\). In the actual invariant patch it is nonempty only for \(h\ge1\). Once collapsed, the trajectory remains at this minimizer.

An explicit image description records the domain restriction. Let \(U\ge1\), use the strict nonaxis domain \(u>U\), and assume \(h>0\). For terminal \(u'\ge U+h\), every \(|v'|>0\) has a preimage in this domain. For

\[
U<u'<U+h,\qquad u'\ne h,
\]

the image consists exactly of

\[
0<|v'|<
\frac{|u'-h|\sqrt{h^2-(U+h-u')^2}}{U+h-u'}.
\tag{3.4}
\]

There is no image with \(u'\le U\), nor a nonaxis image with \(u'=h\). Formula (3.4) follows by substituting (3.3) into \(u>U\); in the reflected branch the same inequality also enforces \(R_b<h\). For \(h=0\) the map is the identity. The boundary \(u=U\) is obtained by replacing the appropriate strict inequality with equality.

For a finite word, exclude points whose trajectory first reaches one of its collapsed circles. Each first-hit set is a smooth circle arc pulled back through finitely many local diffeomorphisms, and has zero invariant area on compact subsets away from \(v=0\). There are finitely many such sets. Off their union, the composed map is injective and preserves invariant area. The sector constructed below avoids every collapsed circle, so no exceptional-set removal is needed there.

## 4. Why the immediate area comparison has no finite target volume

Take an initial rectangle \(1<u_0<U\), \(A<q_0<B\), of finite positive invariant area. Let \(S=\sum h_t\). Equation (2.1) gives \(1<u_n<U+S\). If every original-radius payoff in this rectangle were below \(\kappa\), then its image would lie inside

\[
1<u<U+S,\qquad
q<\frac12\log[d_\kappa(2u+d_\kappa)],
\quad d_\kappa=\frac{\kappa e^{2B}}2.
\tag{4.1}
\]

This follows from \(r-u<d_\kappa\) and \(v^2=(r-u)(2u+r-u)\). The set in (4.1) has **infinite** invariant area, because it extends to \(q=-\infty\) at every allowed \(u\). Preserving a finite positive initial area therefore gives no contradiction for any \(\kappa>0\).

A finite-volume argument must instead control the actual image near \(q=-\infty\), including caustic distortion and the original value of \(q_0\). Removing the collapsed curves themselves only removes null sets and does not provide this estimate. The next section exhibits the failure quantitatively in the precise inherited family requested in the task, with a bounded original radius and no exact collapse.

## 5. A positive-area reflecting sector with negligible payoff on every inherited H_m

Fix an enclosing \(m\ge1536\), and keep

\[
\varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad\beta=1-\varepsilon,
\quad c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c
\]

fixed throughout all children. Let \(H_0\) be empty and

\[
H_{j+1}=(H_j,\ell_j,H_j),\qquad \ell_j=1+a c^j.
\]

Here \(\ell_0\) is the leaf step; it is not a time index. Elementary rational bounds give

\[
12/5<c<3,\qquad 1/3<a<1/2,\qquad 4/3<\ell_0<2.
\tag{5.1}
\]

For example, \(\varepsilon<1/16\), \(\sqrt2<3/2\), and \(\sqrt2>7/5\) give \(c<85/32\), \(a>6/17>1/3\), and the stated bounds.

Set the absolute constants

\[
\zeta=1/64,\qquad \eta_0=\zeta^2/8.
\]

Define the initial annular sector

\[
E_m=\left\{
\begin{aligned}
u_0&=(\ell_0+\delta)\cos\theta,\\
v_0&=(\ell_0+\delta)\sin\theta,\\
\eta_0\ell_0/2&\le\delta\le\eta_0\ell_0,\\
3/4&\le\cos\theta\le4/5,\quad 0<\theta<\pi/2
\end{aligned}
\right\}.
\tag{5.2}
\]

All its points have \(u_0>1\), so the global function and radius calculation in Section 2 apply. Moreover

\[
4/5<|v_0|<3.
\tag{5.3}
\]

In polar coordinates \(du\,dv/v=dr\,d\theta/\sin\theta\). Therefore

\[
\mu(E_m)=\frac{\eta_0\ell_0}{2}
\int_{\arccos(4/5)}^{\arccos(3/4)}\frac{d\theta}{\sin\theta}
>
\frac{2\eta_0}{3}
\int_{\arccos(4/5)}^{\arccos(3/4)}\frac{d\theta}{\sin\theta}>0.
\tag{5.4}
\]

The final lower bound is independent of \(m\).

### 5.1 Whole-word product and weighted-prefix bounds

Assign the formal multiplier bound \(b_0=\zeta\) to each leaf and \(b_j=c^j\) to a level-\(j\) bridge for \(j\ge1\). These are analytic bounding quantities; no array or mathematical program was generated.

For a complete \(H_j\), let \(Q_j\) be the product of these bounds. For \(j\ge1\), direct counting gives

\[
Q_j=\zeta^{2^{j-1}}c^{2^j-j-1}
=c^{-j-1}d^{2^{j-1}},\qquad d=\zeta c^2<1/4.
\tag{5.5}
\]

The identity uses \(2^{j-k-1}\) occurrences of level \(k\). In particular, \(Q_1=\zeta\), and \(Q_{j+1}=Q_j^2c^j\).

Every formal prefix product in every \(H_j\) is at most 1. This follows by induction: the first child has this property; the product through its following bridge is

\[
Q_j c^j=c^{-1}d^{2^{j-1}}<1;
\]

and every prefix in the right child is this product times a child prefix.

Let \(P_t\) denote the formal product before step \(t\), and define the weighted prefix sum for the complete \(H_j\) by

\[
J_j=\sum_{t\in H_j}h_t P_t^2.
\]

The full physical prefix is used in this definition. With \(A_j=c^{2j}Q_j^2\), concatenation gives

\[
J_1=\ell_0<2,\qquad
J_{j+1}=(1+A_j)J_j+\ell_jQ_j^2.
\tag{5.6}
\]

Since \(\ell_j\le2c^j\), we have \(\ell_jQ_j^2\le2A_j\), and

\[
A_j=c^{-2}d^{2^j}<16^{-2^{j-1}},\qquad
\sum_{j\ge1}A_j<1/15.
\]

Thus \(J_{j+1}+2\le(1+A_j)(J_j+2)\). For nonnegative \(A_j\) with sum below 1, the finite product \(\prod(1+A_j)\) is at most \(1/(1-\sum A_j)\); this follows from \(1+A_j\le(1-A_j)^{-1}\) and \(\prod(1-A_j)\ge1-\sum A_j\). Consequently

\[
J_j+2<4\cdot\frac{15}{14}=\frac{30}{7},
\qquad J_j<\frac{16}{7}<3
\quad\text{for every }j\ge1.
\tag{5.7}
\]

Both (5.5) and (5.7) use the same enclosing \(m\) parameters in all children.

### 5.2 Bootstrap for the actual disk trajectory

The first leaf step is exact:

\[
p_1=(\ell_0+\delta\cos\theta,\delta\sin\theta).
\tag{5.8}
\]

In particular \(u_t\ge u_1>\ell_0\) for all \(t\ge1\). For any nonleaf level \(j\ge1\), regardless of reflection,

\[
|1-\ell_j/r_t|
\le\max\{1,\ell_j/\ell_0-1\}
\le c^j,
\tag{5.9}
\]

because \(r_t\ge u_t\ge\ell_0\), and
\(\ell_j/\ell_0-1=a(c^j-1)/\ell_0<c^j\).

We now close the leaf bound \(|1-\ell_0/r_t|\le\zeta\). Assume it has held at all previous leaf occurrences. The actual transverse coordinates then obey

\[
|v_t|\le\frac{|v_1|}{\zeta}P_t\le\frac\delta\zeta
\quad(t\ge1),
\]

and (5.7) bounds the entire accumulated drift by

\[
\begin{aligned}
u_t-u_1
&=\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}
\frac{h_s v_s^2}{r_s(r_s+u_s)}\\
&\le\frac{3\delta^2}{2\ell_0^2\zeta^2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{5.10}
\]

The terms are actual earlier iterates; the bound by the complete positive sum \(J_m\) is valid for every prefix. Also

\[
r_t-u_t\le\frac{v_t^2}{2\ell_0}
\le\frac{\delta^2}{2\ell_0\zeta^2}.
\]

Using \(\ell_0\ge1\) and \(\eta=\delta/\ell_0\le\eta_0\), we get

\[
\frac{r_t}{\ell_0}
\le1+\eta+\frac{2\eta^2}{\zeta^2}
\le1+\frac{5\zeta^2}{32}
<1+\frac\zeta2.
\tag{5.11}
\]

Since \(r_t\ge\ell_0\), (5.11) implies

\[
0\le1-\ell_0/r_t<\zeta.
\]

This establishes the next leaf bound and closes the bootstrap for every step of the entire \(H_m\), with constants independent of \(m\).

Every nonleaf bridge actually reflects. Indeed,

\[
\frac{\ell_1}{\ell_0}-1
=\frac{a(c-1)}{\ell_0}>1/6>\zeta/2,
\]

so (5.11) gives \(r_t<\ell_1\le\ell_j\) at every level \(j\ge1\). The first leaf has \(r_0>\ell_0\), and all later leaves have \(r_t\ge u_t>\ell_0\). Thus no step collapses a point in \(E_m\). The composed map is injective and preserves \(\mu(E_m)\) exactly. This pays all sign and caustic boundaries for this particular sector.

### 5.3 Original-radius terminal payoff

At the end of the complete word, the actual transverse coordinate satisfies

\[
|v_n|\le\frac{|v_1|}{\zeta}Q_m,
\qquad
\frac{|v_1|}{|v_0|}=\frac\delta{\ell_0+\delta}.
\]

Since \(r_n+u_n\ge2\ell_0\), equation (2.3) yields

\[
\begin{aligned}
\Pi_{H_m}(u_0,v_0)
&\le\frac1{\ell_0}
\frac{\delta^2}{(\ell_0+\delta)^2}
\frac{Q_m^2}{\zeta^2}\\
&\le\frac{\zeta^2}{64\ell_0}
c^{-2m-2}d^{2^m}
<4^{-2^m}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{5.12}
\]

This is an upper bound for **every actual original start in the positive-area sector**, including its original selected minimizer; there is no terminal renormalization.

Finally,

\[
S_m=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{m-j-1}\ell_j
<\frac{2c^m}{c-2}<5c^m,
\quad D_m<11c^m<11\cdot3^m.
\]

Therefore

\[
D_m\sup_{E_m}\Pi_{H_m}
<11\cdot3^m\,4^{-2^m}\longrightarrow0.
\tag{5.13}
\]

The accepted comparison supplied in the task would also give
\(B_{2^m-1}\sup_{E_m}\Pi_{H_m}\to0\). The proof of (5.12)--(5.13) itself does not depend on that accepted comparison or on the R6 quadratic draft.

## 6. What is and is not closed

The parent Jacobian and inverse formulas are correct on their stated noncollapsed charts, once the actual \(u\ge1\) image restrictions are retained. The invariant measure is real, but the most immediate bad-payoff region has infinite invariant area. More decisively, the exact inherited \(H_m\) maps a uniformly positive-area, finite-original-radius, genuinely reflecting sector to payoffs smaller than the target scale by the explicit factor in (5.13). No point in this sector supplies the missing gain.

An area argument would need an additional estimate selecting a different initial subset and controlling its actual image relative to the threshold

\[
\frac{2(r_n-u_n)}{v_0^2}\ge\frac{1-o(1)}{B_{2^m-1}}.
\]

Such an estimate must keep the dependence on the original \(v_0\), prevent the caustic-induced transverse contraction quantified above, and retain constants through every inherited level. Neither area preservation, injectivity, nullity of the collapsed circles, bounded horizontal drift, nor the occurrence of reflections supplies it. This attempt has no such selection estimate. It therefore does not pass the lower-bound viability test on \(H_m\), and it makes no all-word exact-profile claim.

The result should not be recorded as minimax progress or as a disproof of the disk family's possible usefulness. It is a quantitative reason not to renew the invariant-measure argument without a new terminal selection mechanism.

## 7. Evidence and closeout

Read in full: this allocation's `TASK.md`, current `OPERATOR.md`, the original `PROBLEM.md`, and `research/R7_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md`. The mathematical-writing skill and its integrity/genre references were used for scope and notation checks. A lightweight memory-registry check was used only for the historical scope warning; current authorization and target came from the live project files and the parent dispatch. The other disk call's proof was not read or duplicated.

All new mathematics here is UNVERIFIED. The derivation is text/manual only. No numerical, symbolic, solver, formal, worker, service, truth-store, Goal, publication, or additional-model action was performed. This one substantive area-selection line is finished; no allowance is carried forward.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001607 -->
# R7 returning-history handoff

2026-10-09. **All new mathematics UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.**

Allocation `grant7ef850dddbc80dd7` is exhausted by this one invocation and two substantive analytical lines. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, further models/agents, and formal submissions: **zero**. Parent owns ledger closeout; no renewal is requested or implied.

The complete proof is `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`, SHA-256 `9db0f5cdd4eb2e15b17a8fdda8501c3e9fa996ea1fe4b024c3528bae58d4ed1b`. The assigned task was read fully and its SHA-256 matches `483c75c26df7013ab16462b532433c8b96ff93d00d05f4999c63a45d148551d6`.

## Unchanged original problem

For every finite-dimensional convex L-smooth function with attained minimum, every original selected minimizer/start and actual last iterate, use

\[
 W_n(h)=\sup 2(f(x_n)-f_\star)/(L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2),\qquad W=2R.
\]

The target remains the uniform lower bound: for every epsilon>0 there is a word-independent N_epsilon such that every n>=N_epsilon and every predetermined nonnegative h obey W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. Here C0=B0=1, C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l), B_n=max G(C_k,B_l), over all integer k+l=n-1, with

\[
 \Omega(x,y)=(x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2})/2,
 \quad G(x,y)=2x+y/2+\sqrt{y^2+8xy}/2.
\]

A negative answer needs a full-class asymptotic upper certificate. Neither type of answer is provided here.

The full current OPERATOR, original PROBLEM, assigned TASK, R6 handoff, both R6 native proofs, and R6 quadratic baseline proof were read. Initialization was confirmed. The Zhang--Jiang HTML fetch failed with `UnexpectedStatusCode`; the primary PDF was subsequently accessible and its relevant retained inequalities were read. No sufficient upper certificate was treated as a converse.

## Actual family used for the decisive test

For each enclosing m>=4, keep eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c fixed throughout H0=empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). Let S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, D_m=1+2S_m. The existing auxiliary draft proves

\[
 D_m\sup_{\lambda\in[0,1]}\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2\le9/16,
 \quad p_m(\lambda)=\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}
 [1-\lambda(1+a c^j)]^{2^{m-j-1}}.
\]

It remains UNVERIFIED. The parent reports accepted comparison B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 for all m>=1536 from fact 931b9653ce0f7201; this invocation did not independently query that fact. No full-class upper bound on W(H_m) is assumed.

## Line 1: repeated returns in a static two-curvature function do not improve the quadratic pool

Use f_mu(x)=x^2/2 for x>=0 and mu*x^2/2 for x<=0, with 0<mu<=1. This one fixed convex 1-smooth function has unique minimum 0. It realizes all old, cross, future and terminal rows automatically. The result includes every nonzero original start.

Let mu0=(1-a)/(1+a). For mu<=mu0, a negative-to-positive switch can only occur at a nonzero-level bridge. The next step is level 0 and returns to the negative side. Every positive-side multiplier a is at most 1-mu*(1+a), while all other multipliers are those of the curvature-mu quadratic. The last point is nonpositive, so

\[
 E_\mu(H_m)\le\mu p_m(\mu)^2.
\]

For mu>=mu0, every multiplier has magnitude at most d_j=a*c^j at level j. Thus

\[
 E_\mu(H_m)\le P_m^2=p_m(1)^2,
 \qquad P_m=\beta^{2^m-1}c^{-m}.
\]

Consequently the ENTIRE specified two-curvature family is dominated by the actual quadratic pool on the same word and original radius. Conditional on the read auxiliary bound, its value is at most9/(16D_m), hence below423/(800B_(n_m)) under the accepted comparison.

This really tests repeated returns: mu=1/2 has exactly2^(m-2)-1 negative-to-positive switches, each followed by a positive-to-negative return, on every m>=4. No finite-horizon toy or formal Gram is used. The result does not cover all pairs of curvatures below1, all one-dimensional functions, or the full function class.

## Line 2: all affine-terminal returns of a one-sided Huber function are bounded by the standard witness

Use the single function

\[
 F(x)=\max_{g\ge-1}(xg-g^2/2)
 =\begin{cases}x^2/2,&x\ge-1,\\-x-1/2,&x\le-1,\end{cases}
 \quad \nabla F(x)=\max(x,-1).
\]

It is convex, 1-smooth and uniquely minimized at0. Every finite orbit is also realized by a single compact asymmetric interval hull [-1,M] with M chosen above all visited gradients. No cap changes during an orbit.

The parent supplied, and the proof verifies, the exact interval update. The complete image of [-R,R] is [-A_t,B_t], and for h_t>1, delta_t=h_t-1,

\[
 A_{t+1}=\max(A_t-h_t,\delta_tB_t),\quad
 B_{t+1}=\delta_t\min(A_t,1),\quad A_0=B_0=R.
\]

Continuity and the fixed point0 guarantee interval surjectivity. Optimizing endpoint objective divided by R^2 is exactly the supremum over actual original-radius starts, not a relaxed normalization.

The quantitative new bound is

\[
 A_{n_m}(R)\le\max\{R-S_m,
 aR-[S_m-(1+a)],a\beta\},\qquad B_{n_m}(R)\le a.
\]

Its proof unrolls the first interval recurrence, uses B_t<=delta_(t-1), and pays every reflected contribution a*d_j against its entire remaining physical suffix. A level-j bridge is followed by H_j; its following level-0 step still has suffix mass at least S_j-(1+a). The exact induction S_j-(1+a)>=a*d_j for j>=2 pays every such high-level return. The sole terminal low-level allowance is a*beta. This covers occurrences in every subtree, with no spine restriction and no bound on the number of returns.

It follows exactly that

\[
 A_{n_m}(R)\ge1\iff R\ge S_m+1,\qquad
 A_{n_m}(R)=R-S_m\quad(R\ge S_m+1).
\]

Thus every actual trajectory ending on the negative affine piece, however often it previously returned, has

\[
 \frac{2F(x_{n_m})}{|x_0|^2}
 \le\frac{2R-D_m}{R^2}\le1/D_m,
 \quad R=|x_0|.
\]

Equality is attained by x0=-D_m, the standard noncrossing witness. This closes the full affine-terminal sector, rather than merely a selected one-crossing itinerary. Every R>=sqrt(D_m) also gives at most1/D_m. For R<=1, all physical prefixes remain quadratic and the exact value is P_m^2<1/D_m.

## Exact remaining gap and stopping decision

The unresolved sector of this one-sided family has original radius1<R<sqrt(D_m) and terminal point in the quadratic part. Define, using the COMPLETE physical interval recurrence above,

\[
 K_m=\sup_{1<R<\sqrt{D_m}}
 \frac{\max(A_{n_m}(R)^2,B_{n_m}(R)^2)}{R^2}.
\]

The line proves the exact family decomposition E_F(H_m)=max{1/D_m,K_m}. It does NOT establish a target-scale lower bound for K_m or an upper bound excluding it. A viable witness in this family would first need liminf_m B_(n_m)K_m>=1; even that would only pass the inherited-family test, after which the full all-word selection and uniform loss would still need proof.

No target-scale nonlinear payoff was obtained, and no arbitrary-word cut/history rule was proved. The interval recurrence itself is not reported as progress toward a closed minimax theorem. The quantified affine-terminal result and the precise unresolved radius sector are the handoff; the whole one-sided family has NOT been ruled out.

Do not renew the two-curvature comparison as a route to the target, and do not reopen common-normal replenishment or the rightmost-spine path product. A future distinct mechanism would need an actual target-scale payoff in a still-unexcluded nonlinear family before claiming recursive viability. No new grant or mathematical execution is authorized by this handoff.

The proof was re-read after writing; a missing math-operator backslash and an unnecessarily weak maximum in the comparison statement were corrected before the final proof hash. No approval-review rejection occurred. No private worker memory, old numerical array, truth store, worker/service, paid consult, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal state was changed. This invocation now stops.

## DOC-001608 — handoff_20261010/materials/frontier/recursive_return_adversary_20261009_r7/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md

# R7: actual returning functions and the original-radius payoff

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Original target OPEN.**

Allocation `grant7ef850dddbc80dd7`: one native invocation, two substantive proof lines, zero mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, further model/agent calls, and formal submissions. The parent owns the allocation ledger. This report does not claim a new accepted fact or a resolution of the original problem.

The first line realizes repeated returns on every sufficiently deep inherited-parameter word, but its entire stated function family is dominated by the quadratic baseline. The second line studies the full trajectory of a one-sided Huber function. It gives a quantitative original-radius ceiling for every trajectory ending on its affine part, including trajectories with arbitrarily many returns. The remaining terminal-quadratic region is unresolved. No target-scale nonlinear witness was obtained, so no arbitrary-word selection theorem is claimed.

## 1. Target, evidence, and parameters

Set the smoothness constant to 1. The original quantity is

\[
 W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
 \qquad x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t\nabla f(x_t),\qquad W=2R.
\]

The supremum ranges over every finite-dimensional convex function with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, with the original selected minimizer and actual last iterate. The exact benchmark is \(U_n^{\rm ZJ}=1/B_n\), where \(C_0=B_0=1\) and

\[
 C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),\qquad
 B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l),
\]
\[
 \Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,
 \qquad G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2.
\]

Every integer split is included. The missing lower bound is

\[
 \forall\epsilon>0\ \exists N_\epsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\epsilon\
 \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:\quad W_n(h)\ge(1-\epsilon)/B_n,
 \tag{1}
\]

with \(N_\epsilon\) independent of the word. A negative resolution would require an upper certificate for the full function class, not the family bounds below.

The complete current `OPERATOR.md`, original `PROBLEM.md`, assigned `TASK.md`, R6 direct handoff, both R6 native proofs, and the R6 quadratic auxiliary proof were read. The deployment initialization marker exists. Relevant memory was used only for the original-scope boundary; the live operator profile supersedes its historical pause information.

The requested Zhang--Jiang HTML URL returned `Failed to fetch ... UnexpectedStatusCode`. The [primary-source PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.12395), identifying itself as v2, was then read at the relevant definitions and retained inequalities. Sections 2.2, 3.1, and 3.2 supply sufficient upper certificates and retain prefix, suffix, and cross-gradient information. No converse or completeness assertion about the present scalar functions is imported from those certificates.

Fix an enclosing integer \(m\ge4\), and keep its parameters unchanged throughout every child word:

\[
 \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad\beta=1-\varepsilon,
 \quad c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
 H_0=(),\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,b_j,H_j),\qquad
 b_j=1+d_j,\quad d_j=a c^j.
\]

Write \(S_j=\sum H_j\), \(V_j=S_j+1\), \(D_m=2S_m+1\), and \(n_m=2^m-1\). Useful elementary bounds are

\[
 0<a<\tfrac12,\quad \tfrac{15}{16}\le\beta<1,
 \quad \tfrac{12}{5}<c<\tfrac{21}{8},\qquad
 V_j\le c^j,\quad D_m<2c^m.
 \tag{2}
\]

For the mass bound, \(V_{j+1}=2V_j+a c^j\), and \(2+a\le c\) follows from \(a\le1/c\) and \(c\ge1+\sqrt2\). The assertion then follows by induction from \(V_0=1\). In the physical word, a level-\(j\) bridge has multiplicity \(2^{m-j-1}\). Every nonzero-level bridge is immediately preceded and followed by a level-0 step.

The fully read R6 auxiliary draft states

\[
 D_m\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2
 \le\frac9{16},\qquad
 p_m(\lambda)=\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}
 (1-\lambda b_j)^{2^{m-j-1}}.
 \tag{3}
\]

It is an **UNVERIFIED auxiliary draft**, not a fact newly verified here. Its all-frequency proof splits at the first negative factor and retains the displayed multiplicities. The new comparison in Section 2 is independent of the correctness of (3); the numerical ceiling in (9) uses (3) explicitly.

The parent reports accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201`, for the same enclosing parameters:

\[
 B_{n_m}/D_m<47/50\qquad(m\ge1536).
 \tag{4}
\]

That fact was not independently retrieved from the fact service in this invocation. The accepted single-cut-portfolio obstruction `b98bb807d514e80d` was also communicated by the parent. Its existence is a reason not to renew the single-crossing calculation; no new claim here depends on its proof.

## 2. Line 1: a static two-curvature function with genuine repeated returns

### 2.1 Explicit function and complete trajectory

For \(0<\mu\le1\), take the single fixed function on \(\mathbb R\)

\[
 f_\mu(x)=
 \begin{cases}
 x^2/2,&x\ge0,\\
 \mu x^2/2,&x\le0.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{5}
\]

Its derivative is continuous and nondecreasing, with slopes \(1\) and \(\mu\). Thus the function is convex and 1-smooth, with unique minimizer 0. At a positive query the next point is \((1-h)x\); at a negative query it is \((1-\mu h)x\). Every cross, old, future, and terminal interpolation condition is therefore realized by the displayed function. No function, curvature, or minimizer is changed at a checkpoint.

Define the original-radius family value on the entire physical word by

\[
 E_\mu(H_m)=\sup_{x_0\ne0}\frac{2f_\mu(x_{n_m})}{x_0^2}.
\]

The supremum over starts is included. Positive and negative starts need not have the same itinerary.

### 2.2 Small negative-side curvature: comparison with an actual quadratic

Set

\[
 \mu_0=\frac{1-a}{1+a}.
\]

Suppose \(0<\mu\le\mu_0\). Then \(\mu b_0\le1-a<1\). A negative-side query at a level-0 step cannot switch to the positive side. Any switch from negative to positive must occur at a nonzero-level bridge; its next physical step is a level-0 step. A positive start also encounters a level-0 step first. Consequently every positive-side query occurs at level 0, and it returns immediately to the negative side with magnitude multiplier \(a\).

At such a query,

\[
 a\le1-\mu b_0.
\]

At every negative-side query the magnitude multiplier is exactly \(|1-\mu h_t|\). Comparing the multipliers at every physical time gives

\[
 |x_{n_m}|\le |x_0|\,|p_m(\mu)|.
\]

The last step has level 0, so the terminal point is nonpositive; a zero reached earlier stays zero. Hence

\[
 \boxed{E_\mu(H_m)\le\mu p_m(\mu)^2
 \quad(0<\mu\le\mu_0).}
 \tag{6}
\]

This compares the returning nonlinear trajectory with the actual quadratic \(x\mapsto\mu x^2/2\) on the same word and original radius. It is not a comparison of unconstrained Gram data.

### 2.3 Larger negative-side curvature: all multipliers are paid

Suppose \(\mu_0\le\mu\le1\). Since \(d_j\ge a\), the decreasing function \((1-d)/(1+d)\) gives

\[
 1-\mu b_j\le d_j,
 \qquad \mu b_j-1\le b_j-1=d_j.
\]

Thus \(|1-\mu b_j|\le d_j\) on the negative side. On the positive side the magnitude multiplier is exactly \(d_j\). Every trajectory, irrespective of its sign switches, satisfies

\[
 |x_{n_m}|\le |x_0| P_m,
 \qquad
 P_m=\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}d_j^{2^{m-j-1}}
     =\beta^{2^m-1}c^{-m}.
\]

The last identity also follows from \(P_0=1\), \(P_{j+1}=d_jP_j^2\). Because \(2f_\mu(x)\le x^2\),

\[
 \boxed{E_\mu(H_m)\le P_m^2\le c^{-2m}
 \quad(\mu_0\le\mu\le1).}
 \tag{7}
\]

Here \(P_m^2=p_m(1)^2\) is the actual curvature-1 quadratic payoff. Combining (6) and (7) therefore gives the comparison proved in this line:

\[
 \sup_{0<\mu\le1}E_\mu(H_m)
 \le\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2.
 \tag{8}
\]

Using the auxiliary draft (3) yields

\[
 \boxed{\sup_{0<\mu\le1}E_\mu(H_m)\le\frac9{16D_m}.}
 \tag{9}
\]

Conditional on the reported accepted comparison (4), this is less than \((423/800)/B_{n_m}\). The standard noncrossing Huber witness remains larger, at \(1/D_m\).

### 2.4 The returning geometry is actually exercised

For a concrete member valid at every \(m\ge4\), take \(\mu=1/2\) and any \(x_0>0\). Level-0 and level-1 negative-side multipliers are positive because
\(b_0<2\), \(b_1=1+\beta<2\). Every level-\(j\) bridge with \(j\ge2\) has \(b_j>2\), so it switches the negative state to the positive side. The following level-0 step switches it back.

There are exactly \(2^{m-2}-1\) such negative-to-positive switches, each followed by a positive-to-negative return, in addition to the initial positive-to-negative step. None of their factors is zero. Thus this is an actual nonlinear, repeatedly returning history on the complete infinite-depth family. Its failure to provide the required scale follows from the original-radius bound (7), rather than from a failure of a proposed interpolation certificate.

This result covers the explicit family (5), including reflection of the coordinate axis, and arbitrary starts. It does not cover all one-dimensional smooth convex functions, all pairs of curvatures strictly below 1, or the full smooth-convex class.

## 3. Line 2: multiple Huber returns and the complete affine-terminal sector

### 3.1 One static function and an exact reachable interval

Use

\[
 F(x)=\max_{g\ge-1}(xg-g^2/2)
 =\begin{cases}
 x^2/2,&x\ge-1,\\
 -x-1/2,&x\le-1.
 \end{cases}
 \qquad \nabla F(x)=\max(x,-1).
 \tag{10}
\]

This is convex and 1-smooth, with unique minimizer 0. Its actual GD map is

\[
 T_h(x)=
 \begin{cases}(1-h)x,&x\ge-1,\\x+h,&x\le-1.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{11}
\]

Writing \(u=x+1\) gives \(u'=u+h-h\max(u,0)\). It allows repeated passages between the affine and quadratic pieces. The envelope in (10) is unbounded in gradient space, but this is not an admissibility issue for the original function class. Moreover, any finite trajectory is exactly reproduced by a compact interval hull \([-1,M]\) once \(M\) exceeds all visited gradients. The cap is chosen once before running that trajectory. The same statement holds simultaneously over a compact initial interval and a fixed finite word, because all its finite-time images are compact.

The parent supplied the following exact interval observation during this line. Its verification is included here. Start with every \(x_0\in[-R,R]\), \(R>0\). Every map is continuous and fixes 0, so the image at each time is a compact interval \([-A_t,B_t]\) containing 0. For \(h_t>1\), \(\delta_t=h_t-1\), its exact endpoints obey

\[
 A_{t+1}=\max\{A_t-h_t,\delta_tB_t\},\qquad
 B_{t+1}=\delta_t\min\{A_t,1\},
 \qquad A_0=B_0=R.
 \tag{12}
\]

Indeed the minimum of (11) on the interval is the smaller of the negative outer endpoint and \(-\delta_tB_t\). Its maximum is at \(-1\) if that point is present, and otherwise at \(-A_t\). Connectedness gives every point between those endpoints, so no independence or reachability correlation has been discarded.

Put

\[
 J(A)=\begin{cases}A^2,&0\le A\le1,\\2A-1,&A\ge1.\end{cases}
\]

Convexity of \(F\) implies that its maximum on the final interval is at an endpoint. The exact worst value for this fixed function is

\[
 E_F(H_m):=\sup_{x_0\ne0}\frac{2F(x_{n_m})}{x_0^2}
 =\sup_{R>0}\frac{\max\{J(A_{n_m}(R)),B_{n_m}(R)^2\}}{R^2}.
 \tag{13}
\]

For one direction, choose \(R=|x_0|\). For the other, an endpoint is attained by an actual start with \(|x_0|\le R\), and using its original radius only increases the ratio. If its endpoint value is positive, that start is nonzero. Formula (13) therefore does not replace the initial radius by a favorable new one.

Equations (12)--(13) alone are not a closure result. The quantitative part of this line is the full-word bound below, which handles every number and placement of returns in its stated sector.

### 3.2 Every returned excursion pays its physical suffix

Abbreviate \(n=n_m\), \(S=S_m\), \(b_0=1+a\). Unrolling the first equation in (12) gives the exact identity

\[
 A_n=\max\left\{R-S,
 \max_{0\le t<n}\left[
 \delta_tB_t-\sum_{u=t+1}^{n-1}h_u\right]\right\}.
 \tag{14}
\]

For \(t\ge1\), the second equation in (12) gives \(B_t\le\delta_{t-1}\). The adjacent-level structure of the complete word now pays every possible reflected contribution in (14).

First, for every \(j\ge2\),

\[
 S_j-b_0\ge a d_j.
 \tag{15}
\]

At \(j=2\), the left side is \(b_0+b_1=2+a+\beta\), while \(a d_2=\beta^2<1\). For the induction, let \(Q_j=S_j-b_0-a d_j\). Using \(a c=\beta\) gives

\[
 Q_{j+1}=2Q_j+b_0+1+(2a+1-\beta)d_j>0
 \quad\hbox{if }Q_j\ge0.
\]

If the current step has level \(j\ge1\), its preceding step has level 0. Thus \(\delta_tB_t\le a d_j\), and the physical suffix after this bridge contains a whole \(H_j\), of mass \(S_j\). The associated contribution in (14) is negative: for \(j=1\), \(a d_1=a\beta<b_0=S_1\); for \(j\ge2\), use (15).

If the current step has level 0 and \(t\ge1\), its preceding step is a level-\(j\) bridge for some \(j\ge1\). Again \(\delta_tB_t\le a d_j\). The remaining physical suffix contains that bridge's right child \(H_j\), except its first level-0 step, so its mass is at least \(S_j-b_0\). Equation (15) makes the contribution nonpositive when \(j\ge2\). When \(j=1\), it is at most \(a\beta\). These statements include occurrences inside every subtree; no restriction to a selected spine is made.

Finally, at \(t=0\), the term in (14) is \(aR-(S-b_0)\). Thus

\[
 \boxed{A_n(R)\le
 \max\{R-S,\ aR-(S-b_0),\ a\beta\},
 \qquad B_n(R)\le a.}
 \tag{16}
\]

This is a whole-history bound on actual interval images. It includes interior quadratic queries and arbitrary repetitions of earlier directions, without resetting the point, gradient hull, or radius.

### 3.3 Exact original-radius payoff for every affine-terminal trajectory

Let \(V=S+1\). For \(m\ge2\), \(V>4\), \(a<1/2\), and \(b_0<3/2\), so

\[
 (1-a)V>b_0.
\]

When \(R<V\), all three terms in the maximum in (16) are less than 1: the second one is less than
\(aV-S+b_0=1-[(1-a)V-b_0]\). When \(R\ge V\), the first term dominates the second and is at least 1, hence also dominates \(a\beta\). Starting at \(x_0=-R\) in this range stays on the affine ray throughout the whole word and ends at \(-R+S\). This realizes the bound. Consequently

\[
 \boxed{A_n(R)\ge1\ \Longleftrightarrow\ R\ge S+1,
 \qquad A_n(R)=R-S\quad(R\ge S+1).}
 \tag{17}
\]

Every actual trajectory with terminal point \(x_n\le-1\), regardless of prior returns, therefore satisfies, with its original \(R=|x_0|\),

\[
 \frac{2F(x_n)}{R^2}
 \le\frac{2(R-S)-1}{R^2}
 =\frac{2R-D_m}{R^2}
 \le\frac1{D_m}.
 \tag{18}
\]

The last inequality is exactly \((R-D_m)^2\ge0\). Equality is attained by the standard noncrossing start \(x_0=-D_m\). Thus the entire affine-terminal sector, including all possible returning itineraries, has exact best value \(1/D_m\). This is a bound within the fixed one-sided family, not an upper bound for \(W(H_m)\).

More generally, every radius \(R\ge\sqrt{D_m}\) in (13) gives at most \(1/D_m\). Between \(\sqrt{D_m}\) and \(V\), (17) and \(B_n\le a\) make the terminal numerator at most 1. Above \(V\), (17)--(18) apply, and the positive endpoint has still smaller value.

### 3.4 The small-radius quadratic region and the decisive unresolved inequality

Every prefix of \(H_j\) has scalar-quadratic multiplier of absolute value at most 1 at curvature 1. To prove this, use induction on \(j\). A prefix in the first child has the induction bound. The complete first child has magnitude \(P_j\), and multiplying by its bridge gives

\[
 P_jd_j=a\beta^{2^j-1}\le a<1.
\]

Multiplication by any prefix of the second child preserves this upper bound. The empty prefix is 1. Therefore, when \(0<R\le1\), every trajectory starting in \([-R,R]\) stays in the quadratic part of (10). In that region

\[
 A_n(R)=B_n(R)=P_mR,
 \qquad E_R=P_m^2<1/D_m\quad(m\ge4).
 \tag{19}
\]

The strict inequality uses only (2): \(D_mP_m^2\le D_mc^{-2m}<2c^{-m}\le2(5/12)^4<1\).

The remaining sector is precisely

\[
 1<R<\sqrt{D_m},\qquad -1<x_n<1.
\]

In particular, with (12) used for the complete inherited-parameter word, define

\[
 K_m=\sup_{1<R<\sqrt{D_m}}
 \frac{\max\{A_{n_m}(R)^2,B_{n_m}(R)^2\}}{R^2}.
\]

The preceding bounds give the exact decomposition for this fixed function:

\[
 E_F(H_m)=\max\{1/D_m,K_m\}\qquad(m\ge4).
 \tag{20}
\]

No estimate reaching the required scale for \(K_m\) was proved. A successful witness in this particular family would first have to establish

\[
 \liminf_{m\to\infty}B_{n_m}K_m\ge1,
 \tag{21}
\]

or a comparably strong bound after combining with another actual nonlinear family. In view of (4), reaching the original scale through this family requires an improvement over \(1/D_m\) in the unresolved quadratic-terminal sector; affine-terminal returns cannot supply it. Even (21) would only pass the inherited-family viability test. It would not prove the uniform arbitrary-word assertion (1).

The recurrence (12) and the restatement (21) are not claimed as progress toward a solved minimax theorem. The new quantitative information is (16)--(19), which closes the large-radius and affine-terminal parts of this actual returning family. The central intermediate-radius inequality remains open. No scalar cap, restart, or purported routine selection lemma is substituted for it.

## 4. Closeout

Both lines use fixed one-dimensional convex 1-smooth functions with attained, unique minimum 0, every physical step, the actual last iterate, and the original radius. Their definitions automatically retain all interpolation rows. Multiplying either function by any prescribed \(L>0\) restores the original \(L\)-smooth setting without changing the normalized GD trajectory or \(W=2R\).

Line 1 rules out its full specified returning two-curvature family as the missing nonlinear gain, conditional only on the explicitly labeled R6 bound for its numerical ceiling. Line 2 proves that arbitrarily many affine-terminal Huber returns also fail to add the missing gain on the actual inherited family. Its quadratic-terminal intermediate-radius sector is not settled. The full finite-cap asymmetric-Huber family, weighted centered simplexes with varying normal components, and higher-dimensional returning histories are not upper-bounded by these results.

There is no target-scale nonlinear witness, no uniform all-word lower bound, no full-class separation, and no complete candidate for formal verification. The original mathematical target remains OPEN. These family restrictions do not suggest that its answer is negative.

This one-invocation/two-line allocation ends here. There was no mathematical execution, old-array reanalysis, further model or agent, formal submission, private worker-memory access, truth-store edit, worker/service action, paid consult, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or Goal mutation. No allowance is renewed automatically.

# R13 handoff: relaxed PD history input OPEN

**UNVERIFIED manual mathematics. No all-depth input proof, input counterexample, or original-target resolution.**

Fresh grant `1a5403f680a965c1` ends with this handoff. Resource counts: **one native invocation, two substantive analytical approaches, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers zero, formal submissions zero, additional agents/models zero.** No per-call token receipt is available or invented. The parent owns ledger closure and all shared administration.

Full proof: `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`, SHA-256 `1bd04c98681c45cbfad72ade79e3c49a1fa9ed1031e9f72e3fc048941fdf3688`.

Input TASK SHA: `9ef075dfc458d2526b56399ec10c611bdf2932cd8c1a0e35efbf1f41b2bde08b`.
Input relaxed PD note SHA: `f7a80cbc05ee2da4b168f61fbc307817550865fa94978fcbfe067f1dae125c09`.

## Exact target retained

For one enclosing `m >= 1536`, use `epsilon=1/(4m)`, `beta=1-epsilon`, `c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon)`, `a=beta/c` in every child of `H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j)`. Put `S_j=sum H_j`, `C_j=1+S_j`.

The unresolved input is, for all sufficiently large enclosing m, every `1 <= j < m`, and every original convex 1-smooth actual history,

    E_j = 2 sum h_i(f_i-f_end)
          - sum h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2
          - [(7/10) C_j^2-C_j] ||g_end||^2 >= 0.

The original minimax question retains `W=2R`, all-integer `B_n`, every finite dimension, every original selected minimizer/radius and the actual last iterate. The conditional `17/16` upper interface and accepted benchmark comparison do not solve the problem without this input.

## Conditional PD join checked

The parent retained-row identity, the three scalar coefficients, the reduction to `D0=4(32-15/C)` and `N=9+128k/C`, the uniform positive determinant `>851 C^2/196000`, and the leaf two-row identity pass manual algebra checks. No high-level primitive premise was imported. The joining calculation remains conditional precisely on the unproved history input above.

## Actual obstruction to a broad history-composition rule

PROOF Sections 3.1--3.3 derive an exact full non-diagonal remainder in `(p,q,G_R)` with all bridge/right original rows and both signed child residuals retained. Its standard specialization is

    E_P^std = E_L^std + B E_R^std
              + ell(A_v+A_y+Q_vy+k Q_yv)
              + Delta (||q||^2-||p||^2),
    ell=(C+k)/(C-k), B=1+2ell,
    Delta=B k^2-C^2
          = C^2(1+r)(r^2+2r-1)/(1-r), r=k/C.

This matches the parent's independently supplied signed-defect identity. The important new exclusion is actual, not a matrix-direction argument.

For any actual `j >= 3`, use the static asymmetric Huber function with gradient `clamp(x,-r,1)`, `r=k_j/C_j`, unique minimizer zero, and original start `x0=C_j`. Every left-child gradient is one; its endpoint is one. The physical bridge lands at `-k_j`; every right-child gradient is `-r`; the final point and gradient are both `-r`. All physical values and all original minimizer rows are explicit in the full proof; the minimizer rows vanish.

For the requested coefficient, with `vartheta=7/10`, the exact residuals are

    E_L = (3/10) C^2,
    E_R = (3/10) C^2 r^2,
    E_P = C^2 [1-vartheta {r(2+r)}^2].

The actual same-enclosing-m ratio satisfies `r>sqrt(2)-1` at every `j>=3`. Thus `E_P<E_L`. This rejects every rule

    E_P >= alpha E_L + beta_R E_R + sum lambda_il Q_il + PSD,
    alpha>=1, beta_R>=0, all lambda_il>=0,

even with arbitrary original interpolation rows and an unrestricted PSD remainder on the original gradient variables. The entire non-diagonal family tried here is included. The exclusion holds on unbounded inherited depth by taking `j=m-2`.

It is NOT a counterexample to the history input: since `r<5/12`, the actual parent residual exceeds `(29/100)C^2`. The fixed three-tenths margin survives this trajectory. A direct whole-word certificate or another joint invariant is not excluded. Reusing the weakened left residual at unit coefficient, then attempting to pay everything else with nonnegative terms, is excluded.

## Fixed-depth robustness: counterexample depth must grow

PROOF Sections 4.1--4.2 give a full manual proof of the following quantified statement:

    For each fixed j>=1, there are eta_j>0 and M_j<infinity such that
    E_j >= (eta_j/2) sum_(i=0..N_j)||g_i||^2
    for every m>=M_j and every original actual H_j(m) history.

The proof does not assume positive-epsilon primitive positivity. At epsilon zero, the leaf and exact standard join prove the standard history inequality. Equality together with terminal gradient zero forces every gradient to vanish: induct on the right child, use the bridge/terminal row sum `2k||p||^2`, then induct on the left child. The weakened residual adds `(3/10)C_j^2||g_end||^2`, making it strictly positive on every nonzero interpolation-feasible gradient dataset.

Normalize the sum of squared gradient norms to one. The finite Gram matrix is PSD of trace one, and the two terminal interpolation rows bound all function differences by the fixed word mass. This produces a compact feasible set, uniform over ambient dimension. Its weak residual minimum is positive. A convergent-subsequence argument transfers that strict bound to the actual same-enclosing-m parameters for sufficiently large m.

The quantifiers are not interchangeable. There is no estimate on `M_j` as j grows. This is NOT the required all-depth assertion. It does prove that a violating depth along enclosing parameters tending to infinity cannot stay bounded. The proposed fixed-depth negative-history prefix followed by absorption at a minimizer is therefore unavailable eventually; a growing-depth version is still open.

## Cone absorption excluded at all depths

PROOF Section 4.3 separately proves that for every closed convex cone K, `f(x)=||P_K x||^2/2`, every originally selected `z_star in K^polar`, and every physical prefix T of every actual inherited word,

    ||x_T-z_star|| <= ||x0-z_star||.

This compares each prefix with the original start, not consecutive iterates. Cone gradient norms obey `||g_next|| <= (h-1)||g||`; every inherited physical prefix has product `prod(h-1)<=1`. Summation by parts converts these facts into nonnegative cumulative distance decrease, despite individual long steps. The conclusion also covers a compact cone truncation if its cap never binds.

This closes the farther-original-minimizer stall mechanism for that whole subclass. It does not cover compact origin-vertex bodies with active caps, nonhomogeneous projection bodies, or arbitrary smooth-convex functions. The earlier R11 objective-product bound alone did not establish this original-selected-minimizer distance statement.

## Boundary and stop receipt

No complete target-level object exists for formal review. The exact all-depth weakened history input and the original minimax target remain OPEN. The new scoped results constrain a future continuation to growing internal depth and to information beyond the weakened scalar child residuals; bounded-depth or nonbinding-cone absorption cannot provide the requested actual obstruction.

Only the two authorized files in this directory were written. No Goal, truth store, ledger, OPERATOR, CURRENT_RUN, other packet, worker/service state, finalization, revocation, publication, push, consult, or shutdown was changed. No worker-private memory was read. The two approach allowance is exhausted; no automatic renewal or unused credit remains.
