<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001617 -->
# R9: discounted child reserves and the complete parent reserve

**UNVERIFIED research draft. The all-depth certificate is not closed.**

Allocation `b942008eeb852c04`: one native analytical invocation, two substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. The parent owns the ledger. The calculations below are manual exact derivations. None has been submitted to the Danus verifier.

The supplied discounted-mass identity is correct. Its use in a parent introduces a negative left-endpoint square at the same time as the positive prefix square. The full parent maximization remainder also contains a right endpoint-to-initial-auxiliary difference and an associated projection normal. A bound retaining only a fraction of the canonical child maximization remainder has not been propagated. No coefficient sequence with final kappa at least 16/17 has been proved.

## 1. Target and accepted premises

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, retain

    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i),
    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    W_n^*=inf_(h>=0) W_n(h),  W=2R.

The supremum is over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with attained minimum, every selected minimizer, and every distinct start. The target is whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to one, where U_n^ZJ=1/B_n is the original benchmark with every integer split retained.

Fix one enclosing integer m>=1536 and, throughout all children, set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, C_j=1+S_j, D_m=1+2S_m.

Each H_j has length 2^j-1. Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` gives B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. Therefore a proved full-class bound W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) for all sufficiently large m would give a strict 799/800 subsequence separation. That full-class bound remains unproved.

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4` supplies finite-word equality of the full original supremum with the supremum over origin-vertex projection-simplex envelopes. It also supplies the actual same-m H_3 U law for every compact convex body containing zero, every start, and every d>=1. Part B proves primitive positivity for H_1 and H_2 and gives exact identities; it does not assume primitive positivity for H_3 or higher levels. Its Part C retains the original selected-minimum radius.

Throughout the new derivations fix one nonempty compact convex K containing zero, in a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space, and write

    P=P_K,
    F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
    U_t(x)=max_(g in K)(2<x,g>-t||g||^2), t>0.

Every physical and auxiliary projection uses this same K. The gradient is P and the full minimum set is K^polar. For any selected x_star in K^polar,

    U_t(x)<=||x-x_star||^2/t.

Thus an all-body final U law at d=1 would transfer, by the accepted supremum equality, to the full W class. A result for one body or one trajectory cannot make that transfer.

## 2. First proof line: derive the discounted identity and its actual parent payment

### 2.1 The discounted identity, including all signs

Let a complete actual finite nonnegative word have mass S, input x and endpoint y. For d>=1 and 1/2<=kappa<=1 put

    delta=1-kappa, M=d+2kappa S,
    q=P(y/d), p0=P(x/M),
    z=[x-y+(M-S)q]/M.

The physical displacement is x-y=sum_i h_i g_i. Since M-S=d+(2kappa-1)S>=0, z is a convex mixture in K. Define

    phi_t(v,g)=2<v,g>-t||g||^2,
    Rem(v,t,g)=U_t(v)-phi_t(v,g).

For g in K and p=P(v/t), expansion at the true maximizer gives

    Rem(v,t,g)=t||p-g||^2+2<v-tp,p-g> >=0.           (2.1)

The normal term is nonnegative because <v-tp,g-p><=0. In particular no arbitrary nonnegative value is assigned to a remainder.

Here x-Mz=y-(M-S)q, whence

    M phi_M(x,z)=||x||^2-||y-(M-S)q||^2.

Subtracting M U_d(y)=M(2<y,q>-d||q||^2) yields

    M[phi_M(x,z)-U_d(y)]
      =||x||^2-||y+S q||^2+M(d-M+2S)||q||^2.

Since d-M+2S=2delta S,

    U_M(x)-U_d(y)
      =[||x||^2-||y+S q||^2]/M
        +2delta S||q||^2+Rem(x,M,z).                (2.2)

Let g=P(y), V=||x||^2-||y+Sg||^2, and

    Lrad=2S<y,g-q>+S^2(||g||^2-||q||^2).

The two maximizer comparisons at trailing parameters 1 and d give

    ||g||^2-||q||^2 <= 2<y,g-q>
       <= d(||g||^2-||q||^2).

For d>1 these inequalities imply both differences are nonnegative; at d=1 they vanish. Thus Lrad>=0, and the parent-supplied identity is exactly

    U_M(x)-U_d(y)
      =(V+Lrad)/M+2delta S||q||^2+Rem(x,M,z).       (2.3)

At kappa=1 it reduces to the accepted identity. For S=0, z=q=p0 and every term vanishes.

The accepted primitive H_1 and H_2 certificates, together with (2.3), give the genuine retained base

    U_(d+2kappa S_j)(x)-U_d(T_Hj x)
       >= Rem(x,d+2kappa S_j,z),  j=1,2,            (2.4)

for every allowed kappa, every d>=1, every K and every start. Thus eta=1 is justified at these two bases. For H_3 the accepted unrelaxed U law also implies the discounted gap is nonnegative, because U_t decreases as t increases. That monotonicity does not establish (2.4) for the new canonical remainder at H_3.

### 2.2 The true discounted parent

Take the actual parent H_(j+1). Write s=S_j, C=1+s, k=a c^j, b=1+k, h=s+b=C+k, and S=2s+b. Its left child runs from x to v, its physical bridge uses p=P(v), and its right child runs from w=v-bp to y. Both children use the exact H_j and its enclosing parameters.

For one fixed kappa in [1/2,1] and d>=1 define

    delta=1-kappa, sigma=2kappa-1,
    A=1+2kappa s, alpha=A-s=1+sigma s,
    D=d+2kappa s, M=D+2kappa h=d+2kappa S,
    r=P(w/D), q=P(y/d), ell=P(x/A),
    z_L=[x-v+alpha p]/A,
    z_R=[w-y+(D-s)q]/D,
    z_P=[x-w+(M-h)r]/M.

All three comparison points are in K. Their physical-gradient weights and the displayed auxiliary weights are nonnegative and sum to their denominators. Put

    u=ell-z_L, n_L=x-A ell,
    RL=Rem(x,A,z_L)=A||u||^2+2<n_L,u>,
    RR=Rem(w,D,z_R), RP=Rem(x,M,z_P),
    E_L=U_A(x)-U_1(v), E_R=U_D(w)-U_d(y),
    Psi=U_M(x)-U_d(y),
    V_L=||x||^2-||v+s p||^2.

The left identity (2.3), at trailing one, is

    V_L=A E_L-A RL-2delta A s||p||^2.               (2.5)

This is the negative left square that must accompany any use of the positive discounted prefix square.

The two actual bridge normals are

    Np=<v-p,p-r> >=0,
    Nr=<w-D r,r-p> >=0.

Set

    Theta=h[2CD/(D+k)-h]
         =h[CD-k(C+D+k)]/(D+k),
    J=Theta||p-r||^2
      +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2hC/(D+k)Nr.               (2.6)

These coefficients use the new D, not the old undiscounted value. To check the identity without importing the old M, put e=p-r and X=v+s p. The actual bridge gives w+h r=X-h e, and

    Np+Nr=(D+k)<p,e>-D||e||^2,
    <X,e>=C D||e||^2/(D+k)
           +(1+C/(D+k))Np+C Nr/(D+k).

Consequently ||x||^2-||w+h r||^2=V_L+J. Applying (2.2) to the actual prefix of mass h, with trailing D, gives

    M Psi=V_L+J+2delta M h||r||^2+M RP+M E_R.       (2.7)

Together (2.5) and (2.7) give both signed discount terms exactly. Neither child endpoint has been reinitialized.

### 2.3 Feasible auxiliary mixture and the relative-reserve debt

For a scalar theta consider

    z_theta=z_P+(theta A/M)u.

Writing G=x-v=sum_i h_i g_i for the complete actual left displacement gives

    M z_theta=(1-theta)G+(b-theta alpha)p
               +(M-h)r+theta A ell.                (2.8)

Since M-h=D+sigma h>0, the explicit sufficient feasibility range is

    0<=theta<=min(1,b/alpha).                        (2.9)

Let R_theta=Rem(x,M,z_theta). A direct square expansion gives

    M[phi_M(x,z_theta)-phi_M(x,z_P)]
       =2theta A<w-(M-h)r,u>-theta^2 A^2||u||^2.

Also x-Az_L=v-alpha p, so

    RL=2<v-alpha p,u>-A||u||^2.

Combining these equalities with (2.5)-(2.7) yields

    M Psi=A E_L+M E_R+J+M R_theta+T_theta,           (2.10)

where

    T_theta=-(1-theta)A RL
       +theta(1-theta)A^2||u||^2
       +2theta A<(alpha-b)p-(M-h)r,u>
       +2delta[Mh||r||^2-As||p||^2].                (2.11)

Equivalently, the first two terms are

    -(1-theta)^2 A^2||u||^2
       -2(1-theta)A<n_L,u>.

The left normal is nonnegative and has a negative coefficient unless theta=1. The mixed vector is exactly

    (alpha-b)(p-r)-(M-A)r.                          (2.12)

Thus the positive discounted r square cannot simply be appended to R8's payment. The coefficient of the p square, the left mixture capacity, the trailing root parameter, and the mixed r component all change.

For kappa=16/17, the root coefficient itself remains positive at every j>=2. Here are elementary uniform bounds. The exact mass solution is

    C_j=v0 c^j+(1-v0)2^j, v0=a/(c-2), 0<v0<1,

which follows by substitution in C_(j+1)=2C_j+a c^j and C_0=1. Also v0=beta/[c(c-2)] is between zero and one because c>lambda and c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. Hence k/C<=c-2. The inequality sqrt(2)<99/70 and epsilon<=1/6144 give

    c<(169/70)(6145/6144)<29/12,

where the last comparison is 12462060<12472320. Hence k/C<5/12. Since a>1/3 and beta>9/10, C_2=4+2a+beta>5, and C>5 for j>=2. Therefore

    D/C >= [1+(32/17)(C-1)]/C >29/17,
    Theta/(hC)>7/12-170/433=991/5196>0.             (2.13)

The two normal coefficients in J are positive as well. However,

    alpha-b=(15C+2)/17-1-k
             >(95C-180)/204>0.

Thus even with the allowed discount, the guaranteed mixture range has theta<=b/alpha<1. The shortcut theta=1 still has a negative bridge-gradient coefficient in (2.8). No claim that it lies in K is made.

One can state a precise sufficient payment for this first attempt. Suppose, as an additional proved child hypothesis, E_L>=eta RL with 0<=eta<=1, and E_R>=0. If theta obeys (2.9) and theta>=1-eta, the nonnegative part of RL can be used to obtain

    M Psi >= M R_theta
       +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2hC/(D+k)Nr+Q_eta,

where, with e=p-r,

    Q_eta=A^2[eta-(1-theta)^2]||u||^2
       +(Theta-2delta As)||e||^2
       +2delta(Mh-As)||r||^2-4delta As<e,r>
       +2theta A(alpha-b)<e,u>
       -2theta A(M-A)<r,u>.                         (2.14)

The inequality follows from RL>=A||u||^2 and A(eta-1+theta)>=0. No coefficients have been asserted positive without that hypothesis. A positive semidefinite scalar coefficient matrix for (2.14) would be a sufficient one-parent U payment. Even such a payment would not retain a fraction of the *complete parent's* canonical remainder, which is required for another level. That is the second line below.

I did not prove an all-body eta for H_3 and beyond that both pays (2.11) and propagates. Taking eta=1 from the H_2 base and silently repeating it would assume the missing theorem. Taking eta=0 from the accepted U law leaves the relative-reserve debt unpaid. Equations (2.10)-(2.14) are exact or explicitly conditional statements, not a closed recursion.

## 3. Second proof line: transport the complete parent remainder, retaining the new vector and normal

The first line could at best pay Psi. A retained-reserve induction needs to compare its parent reserve to the canonical reserve in the proposed induction statement. These are different actual comparison points.

Define

    z_all=[x-y+(M-S)q]/M,
    R_all=Rem(x,M,z_all),
    u_R=r-z_R,
    n_R=w-D r,
    v_R=q-r.

The notation v_R in this section is a vector, not a child endpoint or a primitive energy. Every quantity is from the already fixed physical parent and its actual auxiliary projections.

Using the complete physical right displacement D z_R=w-y+(D-s)q, coefficient collection gives

    t:=M(z_all-z_theta)
      =-D u_R+sigma h v_R-theta A u.                (3.1)

Indeed the coefficient of q after eliminating the right displacement is M-D-h=sigma h. The coefficient of r is then the opposite one. The same calculation at the prefix point gives

    n_theta:=x-M z_theta
       =n_R-sigma h r-theta A u.                   (3.2)

Neither z_theta nor its residual n_theta is presumed to be an optimizer/normal pair. Both z_theta and z_all are in K, so their maximization remainders exist and are nonnegative, but that does not sign their difference.

Expanding phi_M at z_theta gives the exact remainder transport

    M(R_all-R_theta)=-2<n_theta,t>+||t||^2.          (3.3)

For any proposed parent fraction eta' in [0,1], (2.10) becomes

    M(Psi-eta' R_all)
       =A E_L+M E_R+J+(1-eta')M R_theta+T_theta
          +eta'[2<n_theta,t>-||t||^2].              (3.4)

This is the complete same-body composition identity for the attempted fraction, not a projection in a difference space. It retains the actual parent canonical remainder rather than replacing it by the prefix remainder R_theta.

For clarity about the new normal term put t0=-D u_R+sigma h v_R, so t=t0-theta A u. Then the correction in (3.4) is

    2<n_theta,t>-||t||^2
      =2<n_R,t0>-2theta A<n_R,u>
         -2sigma h<r,t0>+2sigma h theta A<r,u>
         +theta^2 A^2||u||^2-||t0||^2.              (3.5)

In particular

    2<n_R,t0>=-2D<n_R,u_R>+2sigma h<n_R,q-r>.

The last inner product is nonpositive by the actual normal at r, so it is a *negative* multiple of the nonnegative reserve

    N_(r,q)=<n_R,r-q> >=0.

It cannot be discarded as favorable. The first term also uses the real right-child maximization normal, which is part of RR=D||u_R||^2+2<n_R,u_R>. The full E_R remains available in (3.4); retaining only E_R>=eta RR does not by itself sign the other terms in (3.5).

### 3.1 An actual parent trajectory with RR=0 and q-r nonzero

Here is an exact admissible test showing that the additional vector in (3.1) cannot be bounded by RR alone. It does not refute the U law or a retained-fraction inequality.

Take K=[0,1] on the real line. Its envelope is

    F(z)=0                    for z<=0,
         z^2/2                for 0<=z<=1,
         z-1/2                for z>=1.

It is globally convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient P(z)=max(0,min(z,1)) and minimum cone (-infinity,0]. Fix any j>=1 and let ell0=1+a be the first step of its actual H_j. Keep the actual parent bridge b and child mass s. Choose the original parent start

    x=s+b+ell0.

Every left-child iterate is at least v=b+ell0>1, so every left gradient is one and the complete left child ends at v. The physical bridge sends v to w=ell0. The first step of the right child uses gradient one and sends w exactly to zero. Every subsequent original right-child step stays at zero, because its gradient is zero. Thus y=0 and q=P(y/d)=0 for every d>=1.

Since s>=ell0 and kappa>=1/2,

    D=d+2kappa s>ell0,
    r=P(w/D)=ell0/D in (0,1),
    z_R=[w-y+(D-s)q]/D=ell0/D=r.

Therefore

    RR=0, u_R=0, n_R=0, q-r=-ell0/D !=0,
    E_R=U_D(w)-U_d(0)=ell0^2/D=D||q-r||^2>0.       (3.6)

All points are from one actual parent run; there is no independent resetting of the right child's start. This example contradicts any proposed uniform estimate ||q-r||^2<=c RR with a finite c, even at a fixed child level and fixed trailing parameter. It also shows why retaining the entire right gap matters: that gap is positive precisely when its canonical remainder vanishes in this example. It does not contradict an estimate involving E_R, and no impossibility claim for such an estimate is made.

### 3.2 What is still required

Equation (3.4) needs a quantitative payment of the actual vector v_R and the actual normal N_(r,q), together with the mixed u terms. One possible stronger child statement would retain these quantities jointly with RR. Neither its base constants nor an all-body composition were established here. They cannot be introduced as free nonnegative reserves with convenient independent values.

The identity (3.4) is also not proof that a retained-fraction statement is false. Its signed terms are correlated by all original projection rows. A negative direction of a coefficient matrix with independent u, u_R, r and q would not be a realizable function counterexample. The only claimed counterexample in this line is the explicitly scoped bound-by-RR-alone failure in (3.6).

No extrapolated point was silently projected back into K. Every comparison point used above has its displayed convex-mixture membership proof. No difference gradients were treated as a new smooth-convex gradient history, and no all-level primitive assumption or R7 terminal potential was used.

## 4. Final extraction and why it is not available

If an all-body induction actually established, at j=m and d=1,

    U_1(T_Hm x)<=U_(1+2kappa_m S_m)(x),
    kappa_m>=16/17,

then for every selected x_star in K^polar,

    2F(T_Hm x)<=||x-x_star||^2/[1+(32/17)S_m]
               <=17||x-x_star||^2/[16(1+2S_m)].

The last comparison is 16+32S_m<=17+32S_m after multiplying positive denominators. Accepted finite-word supremum universality would give the required full W bound, and accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` would yield the 799/800 subsequence gap. There is no change of original radius, last iterate, normalization, or hierarchy in this implication.

The missing premise remains the all-body all-depth induction, including a proved retained fraction or another sufficient vector reserve. This report establishes no such premise, no full-class upper bound at height m, and no original-target resolution.

## 5. Evidence and resource closeout

Read completely: current OPERATOR; original PROBLEM; this allocation TASK; R8_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009; the complete matched_child_u_closure_20261009_r8/PROOF_ATTEMPT; the statement and complete Parts B/C of accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4`; and the R7 relaxed-terminal PROOF_ATTEMPT for the boundary on endpoint-potential repairs. Both stop at their explicit unpaid inequalities. There is no complete candidate for selective formal submission.

# R6 centered/path-simplex handoff

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** Allocation `0d40dd1ca38f0e52` is finished: one native analytical invocation, two substantive lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero, no added agent/model. Parent owns budget and registration. Only `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md` and this file were written in this directory.

## Exact objective retained

Prove uniformly over every sufficiently large horizon and every prescribed nonnegative physical word that $W_n(h)\ge(1-o(1))/B_n$, where $W=2R$ and $B_n$ is the exact all-integer-split Zhang--Jiang recurrence, or give a full-class asymptotic separation. Both remain unresolved. No candidate is ready for formal submission.

## Line 1: actual R5 audit and a failed selection rule

The complete R5 centered simplex was rederived: static $F_K$, every old/future projection row, unique minimum 0, original variance radius, and exact terminal value. With $p_i=1/A_i$, $P_i=\sum_{j\ge i}C_j/p_j$, and $\delta_i=d_i-p_iP_{i+1}\ge0$,

\[
 \bar P=S+1-\sum\delta_i,\quad
 2F_n=2\sum\delta_i+(A_M-1),\quad
 R^2=\sum_{i<j}p_ip_j\left(\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}C_k/p_k\right)^2.
\]

All tight guards force $X_n=w_M$. For one checkpoint $1+d$, ordinary masses $C_1-1,C_2-1$, and $r=p_1/p_2\le d/C_2$, the exact payoff is

\[
 [2dr-(2C_2-1)r^2]/C_1^2.
\]

Its optimum is $d^2/[C_1^2(2C_2-1)]$, attained at $r=d/(2C_2-1)$. The tight rule yields $d^2/(C_1^2C_2^2)$; its relative value tends to zero as the tail mass grows. Thus equality-backward selection is quantitatively invalid. This is not a failure theorem for the full centered family or its baseline pool.

## Line 2: an actual neighboring simplex, and the decisive tested scope

For $M$ phases, take $w_1=a_1e_1$, $w_i=-b_ie_{i-1}+a_ie_i$, and $w_M=-b_Me_{M-1}$. Set $Q_i=C_i+d_i$ for $2\le i<M$, $Q_M=L\ge C_M$, and require

\[
 b_2Q_2=d_1a_1,\quad b_{i+1}Q_{i+1}=Q_i a_i,\quad
 C_i a_i(a_i+b_{i+1})\le b_i^2d_i.
\]

The simplex has positive null coefficients proportional to $(d_1,Q_2,\ldots,Q_M)$, spans dimension $M-1$, and has unique minimizer 0 for its static projection envelope. Start $X_0=C_1a_1e_1$. Every phase cancels the preceding coordinate exactly. The full report checks all old, neighboring, distant-future and terminal rows, including all ordinary steps.

The optimum amplitude ratios for fixed partition and $L$ give

\[
 E=\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2}\frac{2L-2C_M+1}{L^2}
      \prod_{i=2}^{M-1}\frac{d_iQ_{i+1}}{C_i(Q_i+Q_{i+1})}.
\]

This removes the dense full-tail guard and carries no omitted initial-radius or spectral cost. The product losses remain real.

On the exact inherited-$m$ hierarchy $H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j)$, with $\varepsilon=1/(4m)$, $\beta=1-\varepsilon$, $c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon)$, $a=\beta/c$, and $D_m=1+2\sum H_m$, a uniform analytical bound was obtained:

**For every $m\ge1536$, every nonempty subsequence of the rightmost root-to-leaf checkpoints, every number of selected levels, every skipped-level pattern, every $L\ge C_{\rm tail}$, and all admissible amplitudes in this exact-cancellation construction, $E<4/(5D_m)$.**

The proof retains skipped subtrees in the physical masses. Writing $V_j=1+\sum H_j=\alpha c^j+(1-\alpha)2^j$, $\alpha=a/(c-2)$, a level gap $g$ before level $r$ gives phase mass $C\ge c^{g-1}V_r$. Internal nonterminal factors lose at least $1/2$; the optimized last factor is bounded by $7/[10(2V_r-1)]$. The resulting depth factor is at most $[c(c-2)/2]^{k-2}$, and the explicit leading upper bound is $12252303/16000000<4/5$. Single-checkpoint selections are below $3/4$ of Huber.

The parent auxiliary draft was read completely: for this same enclosing-$m$ word, the complete scalar-quadratic pool is at most $9/(16D_m)$. It remains UNVERIFIED. Combined with the supplied accepted comparison $B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50$, the specified standard noncrossing Huber witness, all scalar quadratics, and all the above rightmost-spine path selections still give only $1/D_m$. This is the exact baseline-deficient scope: no optimization over every Huber scale, start or crossing itinerary was proved.

## Precise open boundary

No theorem controls all centered-simplex choices or all path-simplex partitions on this word. In particular, checkpoints elsewhere in the recursive tree were not reduced to a rightmost-spine set. No universal arbitrary-word selection is proved. The report must not be summarized as a failure of the full simplex family, a worst-case upper bound, or a negative answer to the original problem.

The bound does rule out automatic renewal of the natural rightmost-spine selection inside this exact-cancellation repair: its full depth-varying family has now been tested analytically and remains below an already insufficient Huber baseline.

This invocation is stopped. No automatic renewal or formal submission is requested; no truth store, service, worker or Goal state was changed.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001619 -->
# R6: centered-simplex selection, slack loss, and a path-simplex test

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Original target OPEN.**

Allocation `0d40dd1ca38f0e52`: one native analytical invocation, two substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. No additional agent or model was called. The two lines below do not establish the required all-word selection theorem. They also do not give a worst-case upper bound for the full smooth-convex class.

The complete current `OPERATOR.md`, original `PROBLEM.md`, R5 joint-history proof and handoff, and `research/R5_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261008.md` were read. An initial read at the project-root handoff path failed with `Cannot find path`; the parent supplied the correct research-subdirectory path, which was then read completely. The auxiliary parent draft `research/R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md` was read completely. No worker-local memory or raw numerical array was accessed.

## 1. Fixed target and actual baseline witnesses

Set the smoothness constant to 1. For a prescribed finite nonnegative physical word $h=(h_0,\ldots,h_{n-1})$, let $S=\sum h_t$ and

\[
 W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
 \qquad x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t\nabla f(x_t).
\]

The supremum is over all finite dimensions, convex differentiable functions with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and original initial/minimizer pairs. Thus $W=2R$. The exact benchmark is $U_n^{\rm ZJ}=1/B_n$, where $C_0=B_0=1$ and

\[
 C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),\qquad
 B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l),
\]
\[
 \Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,
 \qquad G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2.
\]

Every integer split is included. The missing affirmative statement is

\[
 \forall\epsilon>0\ \exists N_\epsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\epsilon\
 \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:\quad W_n(h)\ge(1-\epsilon)/B_n,
 \tag{1}
\]

with $N_\epsilon$ independent of $h$. No such threshold is produced here.

The standard noncrossing Huber witness and the full scalar-quadratic pool give

\[
 W_n(h)\ge\frac1{2S+1},\qquad
 W_n(h)\ge\lambda\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-\lambda h_t)^2
 \quad(0\le\lambda\le1).
 \tag{2}
\]

For the first, take derivative $\operatorname{clip}(x,-1,1)$, minimum 0, and start $2S+1$; all iterates stay on the positive affine ray, giving normalized value $1/(2S+1)$. For the second, take $f(x)=\lambda x^2/2$, start 1 and minimizer 0. These are lower witnesses, not an upper certificate for $W$. Throughout, “Huber baseline” means exactly this standard noncrossing witness; it does not mean every possible Huber scale, starting point or crossing itinerary.

## 2. Line 1: the actual R5 simplex and a quantitative failure of tight-guard selection

### 2.1 Full construction and projection audit

Partition the complete physical word into $M\ge2$ phases. For $i<M$, phase $i$ contains an ordinary block of mass $C_i-1\ge0$, followed by a checkpoint $1+d_i$, with $d_i>0$. The last block has mass $C_M-1\ge0$. Then

\[
 S=\sum_{i=1}^M C_i+\sum_{i=1}^{M-1}d_i-1.
\]

Choose $A_i>0$ with

\[
 p_i:=A_i^{-1},\quad \sum_i p_i=1,\quad
 P_i=\sum_{j=i}^M A_jC_j,\quad A_i d_i\ge P_{i+1}\quad(i<M).
 \tag{3}
\]

Let $u=(\sqrt{p_i})_i$, $V=u^\perp$, and

\[
 w_i=\sqrt{A_i}e_i-u,\qquad K=\operatorname{conv}\{w_i\}_{i=1}^M.
\]

The inner products are $\langle w_i,w_j\rangle=-1$ for $i\ne j$ and $\|w_i\|^2=A_i-1$. Also $\sum p_iw_i=0$, and the vertices span $V$. Thus 0 is in the relative interior of this simplex. Use the single static function on $V$

\[
 F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
 \tag{4}
\]

Completing the square gives the unique maximizing gradient $P_Kx$; projection is nonexpansive, so $F_K$ is convex and 1-smooth. Since $K$ contains a relative neighborhood of 0, every nonzero $x\in V$ has positive value in (4) by testing a sufficiently small positive multiple of $x$. Consequently its unique minimizer in $V$ is 0. At a proposed query $x$, a proposed vertex $w_i$ is its gradient exactly when

\[
 \langle x-w_i,w_j-w_i\rangle\le0\quad\hbox{for every }j.
 \tag{5}
\]

Put

\[
 \bar P=\sum_i p_iP_i,\qquad
 X_0=\sum_i\sqrt{p_i}P_i e_i-\bar P u.
\]

Its original squared radius is

\[
 R^2=\sum_i p_iP_i^2-\bar P^2
     =\sum_{i<j}p_ip_j(P_i-P_j)^2>0.
 \tag{6}
\]

The last inequality follows because every $C_i>0$, so the $P_i$ strictly decrease. Prescribe gradient $w_i$ in phase $i$. If $q_j$ is the physical step mass already spent with gradient $w_j$, and $s=\sum q_j$, then exact GD gives

\[
 \langle X,w_j\rangle=P_j-\bar P+s-A_jq_j.
\]

Writing $z_j=P_j-A_jq_j$, the row (5) for $j\ne i$ is $z_j-z_i+A_i\le0$; the self-row is identically zero. Throughout an ordinary block, including the query at its checkpoint, $q_i\le C_i-1$, so $z_i-A_i\ge P_{i+1}\ge0$. Future phases have $z_j=P_j\le P_{i+1}$. Every earlier phase has permanently

\[
 z_j=P_j-A_j(C_j+d_j)=P_{j+1}-A_jd_j\le0.
\]

In the last phase the terminal inequality is $z_M-A_M\ge0$. These statements check all old, future and adjacent rows, including the terminal query. The row against 0 follows from its positive convex representation. No step is deleted, and zero steps inside blocks cause no change to the argument.

The exact terminal numerator and resulting witness are

\[
 N:=2F_K(X_n)=2S+1+A_M-2\bar P,
 \qquad E_{\rm cen}=N/R^2.
 \tag{7}
\]

The R5 construction itself passes this source-bound algebraic audit. It remains an unverified research construction under the project's verification policy.

### 2.2 What the negative centering cancels, and what tight guards lose

Define the nonnegative actual guard slacks

\[
 \delta_i=d_i-p_iP_{i+1}\quad(i<M).
\]

Interchanging finite sums gives

\[
 \bar P=\sum_i C_i+\sum_{i<M}p_iP_{i+1}
       =S+1-\sum_{i<M}\delta_i,
\]
\[
 N=2\sum_{i<M}\delta_i+(A_M-1).
 \tag{8}
\]

Also $P_i-P_j=\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}C_k/p_k$. Hence the variance (6), for fixed $p$, cancels the terminal block mass $C_M$ entirely. This is a genuine cancellation, but $C_M$ still affects feasibility and (8); it cannot be discarded from selection.

If every guard is tight, then $\bar P=S+1$. The terminal supports are $-1$ on every earlier vertex and $A_M-1$ on the last vertex. Since the vertices span $V$, these are exactly the supports of $w_M$, so

\[
 X_n=w_M\qquad\hbox{when all guards are tight}.
 \tag{9}
\]

This restriction has an explicit unbounded quantitative cost already on arbitrary two-block physical words. For $M=2$, let $r=p_1/p_2>0$ and $T=2C_2-1\ge1$. Equation (3) becomes $r\le d_1/C_2$; (6)--(7) become

\[
 R^2=C_1^2/r,\qquad N=2d_1-Tr,
 \qquad E_{\rm cen}(r)=\frac{2d_1r-Tr^2}{C_1^2}.
 \tag{10}
\]

The unconstrained maximizer $r=d_1/T$ is feasible because $T\ge C_2$. Therefore

\[
 \sup E_{\rm cen}=\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2(2C_2-1)}.
 \tag{11}
\]

The tight choice $r=d_1/C_2$ instead gives

\[
 E_{\rm tight}=\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2C_2^2},\qquad
 \frac{E_{\rm tight}}{\sup E_{\rm cen}}
       =\frac{2C_2-1}{C_2^2}\longrightarrow0
       \quad(C_2\longrightarrow\infty).
 \tag{12}
\]

These are full physical words: either ordinary block may have any finite length and any nonnegative step sizes with the displayed mass. This rules out treating tight backward selection as a lossless quantitative theorem. It does not rule out the complete centered-simplex family, and it does not say that these particular words defeat either baseline in (2).

I did not obtain a selection of nonzero slacks and a partition for arbitrary words that compares (7) with $1/B_n$. The exact recurrence roots do not follow merely from (8). No unknown child-attainability condition or all-word inequality is assumed here.

## 3. Line 2: a neighboring-simplex repair with an all-depth quantitative test

The dense R5 history guard requires an early checkpoint to pay $\sum_{j>i}A_jC_j$. The following actual simplex replaces that sum by local transfer constraints. It has only neighboring negative inner products, an explicit unique minimum, and a one-coordinate original start. This change does not carry a claimed dominance over R5.

### 3.1 Static geometry and parameter selection for any fixed partition

Use the same $M\ge2$ phases. Let

\[
 Q_i=C_i+d_i\quad(2\le i<M),\qquad Q_M=L\ge C_M.
\]

Here $L$ is a dimensionless construction parameter; the smoothness constant remains 1. Choose $a_1>0$. In $\mathbb R^{M-1}$, use

\[
 w_1=a_1e_1,\quad
 w_i=-b_i e_{i-1}+a_i e_i\ (2\le i<M),\quad
 w_M=-b_Me_{M-1}.
 \tag{13}
\]

Require positive coefficients and

\[
 b_2Q_2=d_1a_1,\qquad
 b_{i+1}Q_{i+1}=Q_i a_i\quad(2\le i<M),
 \tag{14}
\]
\[
 C_i a_i(a_i+b_{i+1})\le b_i^2d_i\quad(2\le i<M).
 \tag{15}
\]

For $M=2$, (14) reads $b_2L=d_1a_1$, and (15) is empty. For larger $M$, an explicit positive solution is

\[
 b_2=d_1a_1/Q_2,\qquad
 a_i=b_i\sqrt{\frac{d_iQ_{i+1}}{C_i(Q_i+Q_{i+1})}},\qquad
 b_{i+1}=Q_i a_i/Q_{i+1}.
 \tag{16}
\]

These choices make (15) equalities. The direction scale $a_1$ cancels from normalized performance.

The positive coefficients proportional to $(d_1,Q_2,\ldots,Q_M)$ annihilate $\sum\theta_iw_i$, by (14). The vectors span $\mathbb R^{M-1}$, because the first $M-1$ vectors form a triangular full-rank system. Their only linear dependence therefore has positive coefficients. They are affinely independent and 0 is an interior point of their simplex $K=\operatorname{conv}\{w_i\}$. Use exactly (4). Its unique minimizer is 0, and its gradient is $P_Kx$, as proved above.

### 3.2 Every physical query and every cross-history row

Start at

\[
 X_0=C_1a_1e_1,\qquad \|X_0\|^2=C_1^2a_1^2.
 \tag{17}
\]

During the first ordinary block, at spent mass $t\in[0,C_1-1]$, the point is $(C_1-t)a_1e_1$. Its residual against $w_1$ is a nonnegative multiple of $e_1$, with nonnegative own support, nonpositive support on $w_2$, and zero support on every other vertex. Thus all rows (5) hold. The checkpoint lands at $-d_1a_1e_1=-b_2Q_2e_1$.

Inductively, phase $i$, $2\le i<M$, starts at $-b_iQ_ie_{i-1}$. At ordinary spent mass $t\in[0,C_i-1]$, its point and own residual support are

\[
 X=-b_i(Q_i-t)e_{i-1}-t a_i e_i,
\]
\[
 H_i(t):=\langle X-w_i,w_i\rangle
   =b_i^2(Q_i-t-1)-(t+1)a_i^2.
\]

The residual support on the next vertex is $(t+1)a_i b_{i+1}$. Condition (15), together with $t+1\le C_i$, gives

\[
 H_i(t)\ge(t+1)a_i b_{i+1}\ge0.
 \tag{18}
\]

The residual support on the immediately preceding vertex is

\[
 -a_{i-1}b_i(Q_i-t-1)\le0,
\]

because $Q_i-t-1\ge d_i>0$. The residual support on every nonneighboring vertex is 0. Subtracting the nonnegative own support $H_i(t)$ checks (5) against every old and future vertex, and against 0. At the checkpoint query the same inequalities apply. The total mass $Q_i=C_i+d_i$ of this phase cancels its old coordinate exactly, leaving

\[
 -Q_i a_i e_i=-b_{i+1}Q_{i+1}e_i.
\]

This completes the induction without an unexamined old coordinate.

In the last phase, $X=-b_M(L-t)e_{M-1}$, $0\le t\le C_M-1$. Its residual against $w_M$ is a nonpositive multiple of $e_{M-1}$. Its own support is $b_M^2(L-t-1)\ge0$, its previous-vertex support is nonpositive, and every other support is 0. Thus all final rows also hold. All physical steps, including zero ordinary steps and arbitrarily large finite checkpoints, are preserved in this one static function.

### 3.3 Exact original-radius payoff

At the terminal query,

\[
 2F_K(X_n)=(2L-2C_M+1)b_M^2.
\]

Using (14)--(16) and (17), the equality choice gives the actual witness

\[
 E_{\rm path}
 =\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2}\,
   \frac{2L-2C_M+1}{L^2}
   \prod_{i=2}^{M-1}
      \frac{d_iQ_{i+1}}{C_i(Q_i+Q_{i+1})}.
 \tag{19}
\]

An empty product is 1. Choices strictly satisfying (15) only reduce the product for this exact-cancellation construction; (19) is its optimum over the positive local amplitude ratios with the partition and $L$ fixed. This follows directly by telescoping $b_M/a_1=(d_1/L)\prod_{i=2}^{M-1}(a_i/b_i)$, while each ratio has the independent upper bound in (16).

There is no auxiliary initial-radius charge and no separate global curvature inequality: 1-smoothness follows from the static projection representation. The factors in (19) are nevertheless real transfer losses. They cannot be removed when comparing with the original target. For a single checkpoint, maximizing over $L\ge C_2$ gives $L=2C_2-1$ and exactly (11).

### 3.4 A genuine all-depth test on the inherited-parameter hierarchy

The next bound tests a whole family of selections on the actual baseline-deficient word. It does not test every possible partition of that word.

Fix an enclosing integer $m\ge1536$, and keep its parameters fixed at every recursive depth:

\[
 \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad\beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c.
\]

Let $H_0$ be empty and $H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j)$. Write

\[
 V_j=1+\sum H_j,\quad D_m=2V_m-1,\quad
 \eta=c-2,\quad \alpha=\frac a{c-2}.
\]

Solving the finite mass recurrence gives exactly

\[
 V_j=\alpha c^j+(1-\alpha)2^j.
 \tag{20}
\]

Elementary bounds valid here are

\[
 \frac{12}{5}<c<\frac{49}{20},\quad
 0<\eta<\frac9{20},\quad \frac9{10}<\alpha<1.
 \tag{21}
\]

For the upper bound on $c$, use $1+\sqrt2<29/12$ and $\varepsilon\le1/6144$. For the lower bound on $\alpha=\beta/[c(c-2)]$, use $\beta>999/1000$ and $c(c-2)<441/400$; this ratio exceeds $9/10$. Its upper bound follows from $c(c-2)>1$ and $\beta<1$.

Select any nonempty subsequence of checkpoints on the rightmost recursive root-to-leaf path. Denote their levels by

\[
 m>r_1>r_2>\cdots>r_k\ge0,
 \qquad r_0=m,\quad g_i=r_{i-1}-r_i\ge1.
\]

The checkpoint at level $r_i$ is $1+d_i$, $d_i=a c^{r_i}$. The exact physical phase masses, including every skipped checkpoint and every skipped subtree, are

\[
 C_i=V_{r_{i-1}}-V_{r_i}-d_i\quad(1\le i\le k),
 \qquad C_{k+1}=V_{r_k}.
 \tag{22}
\]

Indeed after the preceding selected checkpoint the remaining right subtree is $H_{r_{i-1}}$; its suffix after the next selected checkpoint is $H_{r_i}$. Formula (22) subtracts precisely that suffix and the selected checkpoint from the physical mass.

We first show

\[
 C_i\ge c^{g_i-1}V_{r_i},\qquad
 \frac{d_i}{C_i}\le\eta c^{-(g_i-1)}.
 \tag{23}
\]

For $g_i=1$, the recurrence $V_{r+1}=2V_r+a c^r$ gives $C_i=V_r$. For $g_i\ge2$, (20) gives $V_{r+g}\ge\alpha c^gV_r$, since $V_r\le c^r$, and $a c^r\le\eta V_r$, since $V_r\ge\alpha c^r$. Therefore

\[
 C_i\ge(\alpha c^{g_i}-1-\eta)V_{r_i}
       \ge c^{g_i-1}V_{r_i}.
\]

The last inequality follows from
$c^{g_i-1}(\alpha c-1)> (12/5)(29/25)>29/20>1+\eta$.
The second inequality in (23) follows immediately.

For a nonterminal transfer, $2\le i<k$, the exact cancellation masses satisfy

\[
 Q_i=C_i+d_i\ge V_{r_i},\qquad
 Q_{i+1}=V_{r_i}-V_{r_{i+1}}<V_{r_i}.
\]

Thus

\[
 \frac{Q_{i+1}}{Q_i+Q_{i+1}}<\frac12.
 \tag{24}
\]

For the final transfer put $V=V_{r_k}$, $T=2V-1$, $Q=Q_k\ge V$. For every $L\ge V$,

\[
 \frac{2L-T}{L(Q+L)}\le\frac7{10T}.
 \tag{25}
\]

To see this, write $x=L/T$, $q=Q/T\ge1/2$. The maximum of $(2x-1)/[x(q+x)]$ on its positive range is
$2/[1+q+\sqrt{1+2q}]$, found by differentiating the displayed rational function. This is at most $2/(3/2+\sqrt2)<7/10$. The restriction $L\ge V$ can only lower the maximum.

Finally, $D_m<2c^m$. If $r\ge1$, then

\[
 2V_r-1>(9/5-5/12)c^r=(83/60)c^r>(4/3)c^r;
\]

for $r=0$, $2V_0-1=1=c^0$. In particular $D_m/T<2c^{m-r_k}$.

For $k\ge2$, substitute (23)--(25) into the full payoff (19), with $M=k+1$. Since $\sum g_i=m-r_k$,

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 D_m E_{\rm path}
 &<\frac75\,
     \frac{\eta^{k+1}c^{k+1-g_1}}{2^{k-2}}\\
 &\le\frac75c^2\eta^3
           \left(\frac{c\eta}{2}\right)^{k-2}
 <\frac45.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{26}
\]

The last bound uses $c\eta/2<441/800<1$ and

\[
 \frac75\left(\frac{49}{20}\right)^2
             \left(\frac9{20}\right)^3
 =\frac{12252303}{16000000}<\frac45.
\]

For $k=1$, the exact one-checkpoint optimum is (11). If $r_1\ge1$, then

\[
 D_m E_{\rm path}
 <\frac32\eta^2c^{2-g_1}
 \le\frac32c\eta^2
 <\frac{11907}{16000}<\frac34.
 \tag{27}
\]

If $r_1=0$, then $g_1=m\ge4$, and instead

\[
 D_m E_{\rm path}<2\eta^2c^{2-m}
 \le2(9/20)^2/(12/5)^2=9/128<3/4.
\]

Consequently every nonempty subsequence of this rightmost spine, at every depth, every terminal parameter, and every admissible amplitude choice within this exact-cancellation family satisfies

\[
 \boxed{E_{\rm path}<\frac4{5D_m}.}
 \tag{28}
\]

This is a uniform analytical bound on an infinite, depth-varying selection family; the number of selected checkpoints may grow with the enclosing horizon, and skipped levels are included through their exact physical mass. It is not a ceiling for all path-simplex checkpoint partitions.

### 3.5 Why this test is on a word that also defeats both baselines

For exactly the enclosing-$m$ word above, the supplied accepted result `931b9653ce0f7201` states

\[
 \frac{B_{2^m-1}}{D_m}<\frac{47}{50}\qquad(m\ge1536).
 \tag{29}
\]

The parent R6 draft, read completely during this call, gives for this same word

\[
 D_m\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}\lambda
 \prod_{j=0}^{m-1}[1-\lambda(1+a c^j)]^{2^{m-j}}
 \le\frac9{16}\qquad(m\ge4).
 \tag{30}
\]

Its evidence status is **UNVERIFIED auxiliary draft**, not a newly accepted fact. The exponent $2^{m-j}$ is the squared GD multiplicity, not a single copy of each level. The source proof splits at the first negative factor and controls all frequencies; it is not a sampling assertion. Its key intermediate bound, for first negative level $j\ge1$, is $T=a c^{j+1}\lambda p_j(\lambda)\le2/e<3/4$, followed by $|p_m|\le T^{2^{m-j}}/(z c^{m-j+1})$, $z=a c^j\lambda>a/(1+a)$. These retain the actual enclosing parameters and multiplicities. This call checked that algebra against the source, without opening another research line or submitting it for verification.

Conditionally on the stated auxiliary bound, the specified standard noncrossing Huber witness equals $1/D_m$, the complete scalar-quadratic pool is at most $9/(16D_m)$, and every path witness covered by (28) is smaller than $4/(5D_m)$. Their combined value is therefore exactly $1/D_m< (47/50)/B_{2^m-1}$. This excludes that entire natural recursive selection family as a route to (1) on a hierarchy deficient for this precisely stated baseline pool. No optimization over all Huber scales, starts or crossing itineraries is asserted.

It does not exclude other checkpoint partitions of the path simplex, does not exclude the original centered simplex, and does not upper-bound $W(H_m)$. In particular it provides no negative resolution of the original minimax problem.

## 4. What remains and final status

Line 1 establishes an exact loss of the all-tight backward rule and retains the full centered construction with all its slacks. Line 2 replaces its dense future-history guard by a real locally coupled simplex, with every old/future row and the original radius checked; its exact transfer losses then defeat every selection on the rightmost recursive spine of the genuine inherited-parameter hierarchy.

The unproved issue is still a quantitative selection over **all** physical words and **all** relevant partitions that attains the exact $B_n$ profile, or a different construction that does so. In particular neither a greedy slack rule nor a reduction of arbitrary checkpoint sets to rightmost-spine sets was proved. Such a reduction must not be inferred from (28), and no unknown minimax inequality has been promoted to a lemma.

There is no complete affirmative proof, no uniform $o(1)$, and no full-class asymptotic separation. All mathematical derivations in this report remain UNVERIFIED. Scaling the constructed functions by any prescribed smoothness constant restores the original $L$-smooth class without changing normalized values. The finite dimension is always $M-1$, the minimum is attained and unique for both simplexes, and the actual last iterate and initial radius were used throughout.

This bounded invocation ends with the two requested files. No formal submission, truth-store edit, worker/service action, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal mutation occurred. Parent owns the allocation closeout.

# R8 tangent-disk handoff

2026-10-09. **All new mathematics UNVERIFIED. Original exact-profile target OPEN.**

Allocation `grantf27a906e67edabf8`: one invocation completed; one substantive disk-construction line used; optional second line unused and not carried forward. Mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. No additional agent/model, private worker memory, worker/service/truth-store action, Goal mutation, publication, finalization, revocation, or shutdown.

Read PROOF_ATTEMPT.md in this folder for the full self-contained derivation. Its principal usable result is an actual original-radius ceiling, not a whole-disk ceiling:

For K=B(e1,1), F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), the complete minimum set is the negative horizontal ray. For x0=(1-u0,v0), u0>=1 and v0 nonzero, the closest original minimizer is (1-u0,0), so R0^2=v0^2. With r=sqrt(u^2+v^2), delta=r-u, q=r+u, the same global function has

    F=delta, g=(delta/r,v/r),
    u_next=u+h*delta/r, v_next=(1-h/r)*v,
    r_next^2=r^2+2*h*delta*(h/r-1), q_next>=q,
    E=2F_end/v0^2=(2/q_end)*product_t(1-h_t/r_t)^2.

All these equations, convexity, global 1-smoothness, full mincone and normal constraints are derived in the proof. The original radius debt is

    ||x_t-x_star||^2=(r_t-u0)^2+2*u0*delta_t.

For ANY complete finite nonnegative word with S=sum h>0 and b=max h, if q0>=2b then every r_t>=b and r_t<=r0. Consequently

    E <= (2/q0)*exp(-2*S/r0) <= 1/(e*S).

On the literal inherited H_m, S_m>3, hence D_m E<7/8. Using the accepted contextual comparison B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50, this gives B E<329/400 for every m>=1536 in that entire initial-point sector. The bound retains all physical steps, the true final iterate, the fixed enclosing-m parameters, and the original minimizer/start.

A concrete curved full-word orbit is u0=v0=3S_m. Its tangential curvature 1/r_t strictly increases at every physical step, and its actual payoff satisfies

    E >= 4/(27*sqrt(2)*S_m), D_m*E > 8/(27*sqrt(2)).

This is only a subtarget constant and belongs to the deficient sector above. It confirms actual changing curvature without pretending that drift supplies the target gain.

For each fixed word, the limit v0->0 at fixed u0>=1 is exactly

    E -> (1/u0)*product_t(1-h_t/u0)^2.

Thus infinitesimal transverse starts recover the scalar-quadratic pool. Conditional on the separate UNVERIFIED R6 bound 9/(16D_m), a fixed-m continuity argument excludes a small neighborhood |v0|<=eta_m uniformly over 1<=u0<=b_m, with E<=3/(4D_m). No quantitative or uniform-in-m control of eta_m was obtained.

The SAME disk already supplies the standard Huber payoff without an added vertex: on the positive axis, F(z,0)=z^2/2 for 0<=z<=2 and 2z-2 for z>=2. The original start x0=2D_m e1, closest min0, remains affine throughout and gives E=1/D_m. Since every H_m step is >1, any earlier quadratic-axis visit reaches the minimum ray at the next step. Checking the possible final quadratic visit proves the exact supremum over ALL axis starts is 1/D_m. No radius v0^2 is used for these positive-axis starts.

Unresolved sectors:

- Left starts with q0<2b_m, including finite-amplitude reflecting histories. Necessarily 1<=u0<b_m and |v0|<2 sqrt(b_m(b_m-1)); the fixed-m infinitesimal neighborhood is conditionally excluded as above. The coupled product has no target-scale lower bound and no whole-sector upper bound.
- Starts with positive horizontal coordinate and nonzero vertical coordinate, with their original radius ||x0|| and genuine interior/exterior transitions.
- Every arbitrary-word exact-profile selection requirement of the original problem.

The optional vertex-plus-disk repair was not used: a constant-gradient plateau is already present in the original disk, and no quantified mixed plateau/curved witness exceeding it was established. Do not renew the route merely to rederive projection geometry, the exact recurrence, a continuous flow, or free curvature parameters. No whole-disk failure is claimed, and no original-target proof or disproof was obtained. There is no complete candidate for a formal call.

The bounded invocation is finished. Parent owns ledger closeout. No automatic renewal or unused proof-line credit is authorized.

# R8 tangent-disk adversary: original-radius sector bounds

**UNVERIFIED PROOF ATTEMPT — 2026-10-09.** Every new mathematical statement below is a manual draft, not a verifier-accepted Danus fact. The original exact-profile problem remains OPEN. This attempt does not establish the required nonlinear witness on the entire inherited family, and does not establish an upper bound for the whole tangent-disk family or the full smooth-convex class.

Allocation: `grantf27a906e67edabf8`; one analytical invocation, one substantive construction line used, optional second line unused. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents/models, services, workers, and truth-store changes: zero. The calculations below are handwritten derivations. File reads and artifact writes are administrative actions.

## 1. Target and outcome

For a prescribed nonnegative word h of length n, use the original normalization

\[
 W_n(h)=\sup_{f,x_0,x_\star}
 \frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},\qquad L=1.
\]

The supremum includes all finite dimensions, globally convex differentiable functions with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and actual selected minimizers and starts. The physical update is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Write B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1, where S_bullet is the exact all-integer-split sequence in the original PROBLEM.md. The original positive target is a word-independent threshold for W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n for every prescribed word, not merely a result on the family below.

For every enclosing m>=1536, keep its parameters fixed throughout

\[
 \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\]
\[
 H_0=\varnothing,\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),\quad
 S_j=\sum H_j,\quad D_m=1+2S_m,\quad n_m=2^m-1.
\]

TASK.md and the current R7 handoff quote accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201` as B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50. That comparison is an external accepted input here; no new fact-store lookup or formal submission was made.

This attempt obtains the following original-radius results for the single fixed disk envelope:

1. For every complete nonnegative word with mass S>0 and maximum step b, every left-side start satisfying q_0=r_0+u_0>=2b has payoff at most 1/(e S). On the literal H_m this is less than 7/(8D_m), and hence less than (329/400)/B_(n_m).
2. A concrete genuinely curved trajectory exists on every complete H_m: u_0=v_0=3S_m. Its original-radius payoff is at least 4/(27 sqrt(2) S_m), but it lies in the preceding deficient sector. Its actual tangential curvature changes at every physical step.
3. The same disk already supplies exactly the standard 1/D_m Huber payoff on its positive horizontal ray. In fact, every horizontal-axis start is bounded by 1/D_m for H_m.
4. Infinitesimal transverse starts limit to the scalar-quadratic pool. Conditional on the separate unverified R6 quadratic bound, these starts are also deficient. This limit is not uniform in m without additional work.

The remaining finite-amplitude reflecting sector is not bounded or made into a target-scale witness. Starts with a positive horizontal coordinate and nonzero vertical coordinate also remain open. Consequently there is no whole-family viability result, and no arbitrary-word closure.

## 2. One global function and its full minimum set

Let e=(1,0), K={g: ||g-e||<=1}, and

\[
 F(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\]

Completing the square gives

\[
 F(x)=\tfrac12\|x\|^2-\tfrac12\operatorname{dist}(x,K)^2,
 \qquad g(x)=P_Kx.
\]

The maximum is attained uniquely because K is compact and the objective is strictly concave in g. A maximum of affine functions of x is convex. To check differentiability directly, let p=P_Kx and p_z=P_K(x+z). Comparing each maximizer against the other gives

\[
 \langle p,z\rangle\le F(x+z)-F(x)
 \le\langle p_z,z\rangle.
\]

Projection optimality gives <x-p,q-p><=0 for q in K. Using this for two projections and adding yields

\[
 \|P_Kx-P_Ky\|^2\le
 \langle P_Kx-P_Ky,x-y\rangle.
\]

Thus P_K is 1-Lipschitz. The preceding difference inequalities show F is differentiable with gradient P_K and that its gradient is globally 1-Lipschitz. In particular, the piecewise formula used below defines one admissible function on all of R^2.

Since 0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0. Moreover P_Kx=0 exactly when <x,g><=0 for every g in K. The support function of K is x_1+||x||. Therefore

\[
 \arg\min F=\{(z,0):z\le0\},\qquad F_\star=0.
\]

This is the complete minimum set. The closest minimizer to (x_1,x_2) is (x_1,0) when x_1<=0, and is 0 when x_1>=0. A different minimizer only increases the denominator for this fixed start, so it cannot improve this particular function's normalized payoff.

For r=||x-e|| the exact global formula is

\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 r\le1:& g=x,\quad F(x)=\|x\|^2/2,\\[2mm]
 r\ge1:& g=e+(x-e)/r,\quad F(x)=r+x_1-1.
 \end{array}
\]

The formulas agree on the boundary. They follow either from the projection formula for a ball or directly from the completed square above.

## 3. Exact left-side dynamics and the original radius

Write

\[
 x=(1-u,v),\quad u\ge1,\quad
 r=\sqrt{u^2+v^2},\quad \delta=r-u,\quad q=r+u.
\]

This point is outside the open disk; it is strictly outside when v is nonzero. The preceding global formula gives

\[
 F(x)=\delta,\qquad
 g(x)=(\delta/r,v/r),\qquad v^2=\delta q.
\]

Every gradient of F has a nonnegative first component, because K is contained in {g_1>=0}. Thus every nonnegative physical step preserves u>=u_0>=1. Substituting the gradient, without changing h, gives

\[
 u'=u+h\delta/r,\qquad v'=(1-h/r)v.
 \tag{3.1}
\]

Expanding the two squares gives

\[
 r'^2-r^2
 =2h\left(\frac{u\delta-v^2}{r}\right)
   +h^2\frac{\delta^2+v^2}{r^2}
 =2h\delta(h/r-1),
 \tag{3.2}
\]

where u delta-v^2=-r delta and delta^2+v^2=2r delta.

The claimed monotonicity of q needs a separate check. Set A=r-h delta/r. Then

\[
 r'^2-A^2=\frac{h^2\delta q}{r^2}
             =\frac{h^2v^2}{r^2}\ge0.
\]

If A>=0, this implies r'>=A; if A<0, that inequality follows from r'>=0. Hence

\[
 q'-q=r'-r+h\delta/r\ge0.
 \tag{3.3}
\]

It is strict if h>0 and v is nonzero. In contrast, r need not be monotone: (3.2) gives its direction of change exactly. The varying curvature is therefore coupled to this same trajectory.

For v_0 nonzero the closest original minimizer is

\[
 x_\star=(1-u_0,0),\qquad R_0^2=\|x_0-x_\star\|^2=v_0^2.
\]

Using (3.1) through the true final time N gives the exact normalized payoff

\[
 E(h;u_0,v_0):=\frac{2F(x_N)}{v_0^2}
 =\frac{2}{q_N}\prod_{t=0}^{N-1}(1-h_t/r_t)^2.
 \tag{3.4}
\]

If a factor vanishes, the trajectory reaches the minimum ray and remains there, and (3.4) still gives zero. No post-step radius is used as the denominator.

Two further identities make the original-radius accounting explicit:

\[
 \|x_t-x_\star\|^2
 =(u_t-u_0)^2+v_t^2
 =(r_t-u_0)^2+2u_0\delta_t,
 \tag{3.5}
\]
\[
 \langle g_t,x_t-x_\star\rangle
 =\delta_t+\frac{u_0}{2}\|g_t\|^2,
 \qquad \|g_t\|^2=2\delta_t/r_t.
 \tag{3.6}
\]

For (3.6), its numerator is (u_0-u_t)delta_t+v_t^2=delta_t(u_0+r_t). Thus the horizontal drift remains a real distance cost relative to the originally selected minimizer.

For completeness, the global normal geometry also follows directly. Put s_t=(-u_t,v_t)/r_t. Then g_t=e+s_t, x_t-g_t=(r_t-1)s_t, and ||s_t||=1. For two trajectory samples,

\[
 \langle x_i-g_i,g_i-g_j\rangle
 =(r_i-1)(1-\langle s_i,s_j\rangle)
 =\tfrac12(r_i-1)\|g_i-g_j\|^2\ge0.
 \tag{3.7}
\]

This checks all disk supporting normals at once; no interpolation point is freely activated. The same formula includes the original minimizer as g_star=0, r_star=u_0, s_star=-e. On the exterior, differentiation gives the Hessian (I-s s^T)/r, whose nonzero tangential eigenvalue is 1/r.

## 4. A complete-word ceiling when q_0>=2 max h

**Draft sector theorem.** Let h be any finite nonnegative word with S=sum h>0 and b=max h. For the fixed disk and a left-side start with u_0>=1, v_0 nonzero, and q_0>=2b,

\[
 E(h;u_0,v_0)\le\frac1{eS}.
 \tag{4.1}
\]

Indeed r_t=(q_t+delta_t)/2>=q_t/2>=q_0/2>=b. Therefore every multiplier 1-h_t/r_t lies in [0,1]. Equation (3.2) also yields r_(t+1)<=r_t, so r_t<=r_0 throughout the complete word. By 1-z<=exp(-z) for z>=0,

\[
 \prod_t(1-h_t/r_t)^2
 \le\exp(-2\sum_t h_t/r_t)
 \le\exp(-2S/r_0).
\]

Equations (3.3)--(3.4) and q_0>=r_0 now give

\[
 E\le\frac2{q_0}e^{-2S/r_0}
 \le\frac2{r_0}e^{-2S/r_0}
 =\frac1S z e^{-z}\le\frac1{eS},
 \qquad z=2S/r_0.
\]

The last scalar maximum follows by differentiating z exp(-z), whose derivative is (1-z)exp(-z). A zero multiplier is already covered. Zero entries of h cause no difficulty.

For H_m, S_1=1+a and S_2=3+2a+beta>3, so S_m>3 for m>=2. Consequently

\[
 D_m E\le\frac{2+1/S_m}{e}
 <\frac7{3e}<\frac78.
 \tag{4.2}
\]

Here e>8/3 follows from the positive exponential series, whose terms through degree three sum to 8/3. Combining (4.2) with the accepted input B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 gives

\[
 B_{n_m}E<\frac{329}{400}<1.
 \tag{4.3}
\]

This is an original-scale failure on every complete inherited word, uniformly over this whole start sector. It is not an upper bound for starts outside the sector.

The sector is substantial relative to the largest physical step. The exact mass, obtained by unrolling S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j and summing the geometric progression, is

\[
 S_m=2^m-1+a\frac{c^m-2^m}{c-2},\qquad
 b_m=1+a c^{m-1}.
\]

As the enclosing m tends to infinity with its prescribed parameters, c tends to 1+sqrt(2), a tends to its reciprocal, and (2/c)^m tends to zero. Thus

\[
 b_m/S_m\longrightarrow(c_0-2)/c_0=3-2\sqrt2.
\]

This asymptotic observation is not used in (4.2)--(4.3); those bounds hold for the literal finite words and parameters.

## 5. An explicit full-word curved trajectory, with its deficient payoff

The ceiling is not based on forcing a constant curvature. For every H_m with S=S_m, choose the explicit original start and minimizer

\[
 u_0=v_0=3S,\qquad
 x_0=(1-3S,3S),\qquad x_\star=(1-3S,0).
\]

The original radius is R_0=3S. Every physical h_t is at most S, while r_t>=u_t>=u_0=3S. Hence every multiplier lies in (0,1), v_t stays nonzero, and, since each H_m entry is positive, (3.2) gives r_(t+1)<r_t at every step. The tangential curvature 1/r_t strictly increases at each of the n_m physical steps. Equation (3.3) also gives a strictly increasing q_t.

At the same time,

\[
 \frac{v_N}{v_0}
 =\prod_t(1-h_t/r_t)
 \ge\prod_t(1-h_t/(3S))\ge1-\sum_t h_t/(3S)=2/3.
\]

The product inequality follows by induction from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b for nonnegative a,b with sum at most one. Also q_N<=2r_N<=2r_0=6sqrt(2)S. Substitution in (3.4) yields the actual terminal bound

\[
 E(H_m;3S_m,3S_m)\ge\frac4{27\sqrt2\,S_m},
 \qquad
 D_m E>\frac8{27\sqrt2}.
 \tag{5.1}
\]

This construction is finite-dimensional, uses one global function and the full word, and has genuine curvature drift. Its payoff is only a fixed subtarget constant. The sector ceiling (4.3) excludes this entire choice of large initial scales from reaching 1/B_(n_m); (5.1) is not claimed as the requested target-scale closure.

## 6. The small transverse limit and its precise scope

For any fixed complete word and fixed u_0>=1, the exterior formulas are smooth in (u,v) near the invariant trajectory (u_0,0). Induction in the finite number of physical steps gives, as v_0 tends to zero,

\[
 u_t=u_0+O(v_0^2),\quad r_t=u_0+O(v_0^2),\quad
 v_t=v_0\prod_{s<t}(1-h_s/u_0)+O(v_0^3).
\]

The constants can depend on the entire word. Using (3.4),

\[
 \lim_{v_0\to0} E(h;u_0,v_0)
 =\frac1{u_0}\prod_t(1-h_t/u_0)^2.
 \tag{6.1}
\]

For u_0>=1, lambda=1/u_0 lies in (0,1]. Thus this limit is exactly the admissible scalar-quadratic payoff, with no rescaled physical steps. Taking u_0 large covers the endpoint limit lambda=0 as well.

The separate file R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md is an **unverified** manual proof that the entire scalar-quadratic pool on this same inherited H_m is at most 9/(16D_m). Conditional on that draft, (6.1) is at most this value for every u_0.

There is a limited uniform statement at each fixed m. On the compact interval 1<=u_0<=b_m, the finite compositions in (3.1) and the expression (3.4) have a continuous extension to v_0=0. Their convergence in (6.1) is therefore uniform on this compact interval. Conditional on the R6 draft, for each fixed m there exists eta_m>0 such that

\[
 1\le u_0\le b_m,\quad 0<|v_0|\le\eta_m
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad E(H_m;u_0,v_0)\le\frac3{4D_m}.
 \tag{6.2}
\]

For u_0>=b_m, the sector theorem already applies since q_0>=2u_0. No estimate on eta_m as m grows has been obtained. In particular, the fixed-m continuity argument cannot exclude m-dependent transverse amplitudes outside this neighborhood or be treated as a uniform asymptotic theorem.

## 7. The same disk's exact horizontal-axis baseline

Other starts require their actual minimum distance. On the horizontal axis the global function in section 2 reduces to

\[
 F(z,0)=
 \begin{cases}
 0,&z\le0,\\
 z^2/2,&0\le z\le2,\\
 2z-2,&z\ge2.
 \end{cases}
\]

For any nonnegative word of mass S, put D=1+2S and x_0=2D e. The closest minimizer is 0, so R_0^2=4D^2. Every physical iterate is x_t=2(D-prefix_mass_t)e and lies in the affine region. The true terminal point is 2(S+1)e and F(x_N)=2D. Hence

\[
 \frac{2F(x_N)}{R_0^2}=\frac1D.
 \tag{7.1}
\]

For H_m, every physical step is greater than one. This permits an exact upper bound on the entire horizontal-axis family, including crossings. A nonpositive iterate is already a minimum and remains there. An iterate z in (0,2] is mapped by the next step h>1 to (1-h)z<0. Thus a nonzero terminal objective requires all earlier iterates to remain in the affine region, except that the final point itself may be in [0,2]. In that event z_0=z_N+2S_m and the original minimizer is 0.

If z_N>=2, the payoff is

\[
 \frac{4z_N-4}{(z_N+2S_m)^2}\le\frac1{1+2S_m},
\]

as differentiation gives the maximum at z_N=2(S_m+1). If 0<=z_N<=2, it is

\[
 \frac{z_N^2}{(z_N+2S_m)^2}
 \le\frac1{(S_m+1)^2}\le\frac1{1+2S_m}.
\]

An earlier quadratic visit gives zero, as already explained. Therefore, for every complete H_m,

\[
 \sup_{\text{horizontal-axis starts, minimizers}}
 \frac{2F(x_N)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}=\frac1{D_m}.
 \tag{7.2}
\]

The supremum can restrict to closest minimizers because F>=0. The accepted comparison gives B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50, so this exact baseline still misses the original target.

## 8. What remains, and why no vertex repair was claimed

For a left-side start to escape the unconditional sector ceiling, it must satisfy

\[
 u_0\ge1,\qquad 0<q_0<2b_m.
\]

Since q_0>=2u_0 and v_0^2=q_0(q_0-2u_0), this entails

\[
 1\le u_0<b_m,\qquad
 0<|v_0|<2\sqrt{b_m(b_m-1)}.
\]

Conditional on the separate R6 draft, the fixed-m infinitesimal neighborhood in (6.2) can also be removed. The residual sector consists of finite transverse amplitudes at smaller initial q_0, where long physical steps may reflect v and change r. No bound on the full product in (3.4), relative to the original v_0, was obtained there. It must not be replaced by an optimized free sequence of r_t.

The theorem in section 4 does not cover every left-side trajectory that happens to avoid a reflection, nor does failure of its initial condition prove that a reflection occurs. It is a sufficient condition with a precise quantitative conclusion. No inference about the complement is made.

Starts with x_(0,1)>0 and x_(0,2) nonzero are a further unexcluded sector. Their closest minimizer is 0 and their original radius is ||x_0||. They may have disk-interior transitions before reaching the left region. A left-region formula cannot be attached to such a trajectory by resetting its radius.

The optional exposed-vertex repair was not used. The disk itself already has an exact constant-gradient Huber plateau, by section 7. Adding a vertex merely to supply such a plateau would not address a missing mechanism. A useful second construction would have to supply a quantified mixed plateau/curved trajectory exceeding the baseline on complete H_m words, while proving its connecting projection geometry and paying the prefix radius. No such quantitative candidate was established in this invocation. A geometric parametrization alone would not meet the task's decisive test.

Even a future target-scale witness for all H_m would only pass the specified family test. The original problem would still require an all-word selection theorem with the exact B_n and a schedule-independent asymptotic threshold. Conversely, the restricted upper bounds here say nothing about a full-class asymptotic separation.
