# R11: complete two-input transport and an actual obstruction to discarding child surplus

**All new mathematics in this packet is UNVERIFIED under Danus policy. The original minimax problem remains OPEN. This is not an all-depth upper certificate.**

Grant `233b55a4bc4f693c`: one native analytical invocation, at most two substantive approaches. Exactly two approaches were used. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents and additional model invocations: zero. The parent owns the ledger and all shared administration.

The two true input projections remove the R10 expanding-normal-fan argument. Completing that transport, including the parent endpoint remainder, does not close the induction. There is an actual, arbitrarily deep inherited family on the fixed body `[-1,1]` where the complete lower expression obtained by replacing both child gaps by the proposed common two-remainder bound is strictly negative. All normals retained in that expression vanish on this family. This is a counterexample to that particular payment rule, not to the two-remainder invariant or to the desired U inequality. The positive child surplus discarded by that rule is explicitly computed below.

The second approach retains the entire signed child energy and the actual cross-subtree normal rows. Its exact recurrence is derived for every trailing parameter. It supplies no quantitative uniform lower bound on the signed right-child energy, so it does not yield a final coefficient `kappa_m >= 16/17`. No complete target-level candidate exists for a finished-object independent adversarial audit.

## 1. Fixed target and notation

The unchanged objective is to determine whether

`lim_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1`,

where `W_n(h)` is the supremum of `2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)` over every finite dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every original selected minimizer and distinct original start, under the literal predetermined nonnegative word `h`. The infimum defining `W_n^*` is over all such words, including words with zero entries. `W=2R`; `U_n^ZJ=1/B_n` uses the original all-integer-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark.

Fix one enclosing integer `m >= 1536`, with

\[
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\quad H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

Every child uses these same parameters. Write `S_j=sum H_j` and `D_m=1+2S_m`. Accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201` gives `B_(2^m-1)/D_m < 47/50`. Its conditional separation follows if the missing full-class upper bound `W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m)` holds for every sufficiently large enclosing m. Neither that premise nor a substitute is proved here.

Fix one arbitrary nonempty compact convex `K` containing zero. All samples use `P=P_K`. Set

\[
U_d(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{2\langle x,g\rangle-d\|g\|^2\}.
\]

If `r=P(x/D)`, `n=x-Dr`, and `z in K`, then

\[
\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,z)
=D\|r-z\|^2+2\langle n,r-z\rangle.
\tag{1.1}
\]

The normal convention throughout is `<n,r-z> >= 0`. For an actual word of mass `s`, input x, output y, and trailing `d>=1`, define

\[
D=d+2\kappa s,\quad q=P(y/d),\quad r=P(x/D),
\quad z=q+\frac{x-y-sq}{D},
\]
\[
E=U_D(x)-U_d(y),\quad R=\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,z),
\quad B=\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,q).
\tag{1.2}
\]

Here `16/17<=kappa<=1`, `delta=1-kappa`, `sigma=2kappa-1`. The mixture z is feasible because all physical steps are nonnegative and `D-s>0`.

The proposed sufficient induction is

\[
E_j\ge\eta R_j+\gamma B_j\qquad(d\ge1),
\tag{1.3}
\]

with common `eta=3/4`, `gamma=1/1200`, and `kappa=16/17`. R10 proves this leaf base in draft. No higher instance of (1.3) is assumed as a fact. Below, child instances are explicit conditional premises used to test preservation. Denote the actual surplus by

\[
F_j=E_j-\eta R_j-\gamma B_j.
\tag{1.4}
\]

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4` supplies the finite-word full-supremum projection transfer and the actual same-parameter H3 law, not higher primitive positivity. An all-body law `U_1(T_Hm x)<=U_(1+(32/17)S_m)(x)` would retain every original minimizer and radius, since for any polar-cone minimizer `x_star`,

\[
U_D(x)\le\frac{\|x-x_\star\|^2}{D},\qquad
\frac1{1+(32/17)S_m}\le\frac{17}{16D_m}.
\]

This remains a sufficient implication with an unproved premise.

## 2. Approach 1: finish the common-fraction transport

### 2.1 Actual parent data and retained radial relations

At a parent with two H_j children, let

\[
s=S_j,\quad k=ac^j,\quad b=1+k,\quad h=s+b,
\quad C=1+s,\quad S=s+h,
\]
\[
A=1+2\kappa s,\quad\alpha=A-s,\quad
D=d+2\kappa s,\quad M=D+2\kappa h,\quad\Delta=\alpha-b.
\]

The actual path is `x -> v`, `p=P(v)`, `w=v-bp`, then `w -> y`. Set

\[
G=x-v,\quad \ell=P(x/A),\quad n_L=x-A\ell,
\quad u=\ell-\frac{G+\alpha p}{A},
\]
\[
p_0=P(x/M),\quad n_0=x-Mp_0,\quad w_0=Mp_0-A\ell,
\]
\[
r=P(w/D),\quad n_R=w-Dr,\quad q=P(y/d),\quad
u_R=r-\frac{w-y+(D-s)q}{D}.
\]

The left child uses trailing 1 and the right child trailing d. Define `R_L,B_L,E_L` and `R_R,B_R,E_R` by (1.2). The parent uses `R_all,B_all,Psi` at mass S and trailing d. Let

\[
e=p-r,\quad z=q-r,\quad
e_N=n_R-n_0=w_0+Au+\Delta p-Dr,
\]
\[
R_0=M(p_0-r)-A(\ell-r),\qquad \chi=1-\eta.
\tag{2.1}
\]

Every projection or mixture point just used is actual; the displayed vector differences retain their defining relations. In particular, with `N_0(t)=<n_0,p_0-t>`, the two input projections obey

\[
N_0(\ell)+\langle n_L,\ell-p_0\rangle
=\langle w_0,\ell-p_0\rangle\ge0.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Writing `zeta=ell-p0`, this includes the radial constraint

\[
A\|\zeta\|^2\le(M-A)\langle p_0,\zeta\rangle.
\]

No vector below is freed from these relations.

### 2.2 The fan cancellation and complete disappearance of theta

For `0<=theta<=min(1,b/alpha)`, use the parent note's feasible mixture

\[
z_\theta=
\frac{(1-\theta)G+(b-\theta\alpha)p+(M-h)r+\theta A\ell}{M}.
\]

The coefficient of the residual left-normal term after subtracting the same parent canonical fraction is `-2chi(1-theta)A<n_L,u>`. The parent's true normal rows satisfy

\[
A\langle n_L,u\rangle
=\sum_{i\in L}h_iN_0(g_i)+\alpha N_0(p)-A N_0(\ell)
 +A\langle w_0,u\rangle.
\tag{2.3}
\]

Thus the complete normal fan over the original left queries cancels. Using

\[
N_0(p)-N_0(r)=N_r-\langle e_N,r-p\rangle,
\quad N_r=\langle n_R,r-p\rangle,
\]

leaves the nonnegative input rows

`2chi[A N_0(ell)+(M-A)N_0(r)]`,

a root normal contribution `-2chi Delta N_r`, and finite-vector terms.

Here is the full square cancellation. Put `V=w0+Delta p-Dr`. Then

\[
M(p_0-z_\theta)=V-\sigma h r+(1-\theta)Au.
\]

Combining the parent square, the left child square, all u cross terms, and the finite term from (2.3) gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\chi\|V-\sigma hr+(1-\theta)Au\|^2
-\chi(1-\theta)^2A^2\|u\|^2\\
&\quad+2\chi\theta A\langle\Delta p-(D+\sigma h)r,u\rangle
-2\chi(1-\theta)A\langle w_0,u\rangle
 +2\chi\Delta\langle V+Au,r-p\rangle\\
&=\chi\left[
\|R_0\|^2-\Delta^2\|e\|^2
-2\Delta\sigma h\langle r,e\rangle
-2A(M-A)\langle r,u\rangle\right].
\tag{2.4}
\end{aligned}
\]

For the last step use `D+sigma h-Delta=M-A`. All theta dependence disappears. In particular, optimizing theta cannot repair the remaining expression after the same child fraction is inserted in this way.

### 2.3 Independent transport of the parent's endpoint remainder

The endpoint comparison needs its own accounting:

\[
B_{\rm all}=M\|p_0-q\|^2+2N_0(q),
\]
\[
N_0(q)=N_0(r)+N_{R,q}+\langle e_N,q-r\rangle,
\quad N_{R,q}=\langle n_R,r-q\rangle.
\tag{2.5}
\]

Subtracting `gamma M B_all` therefore costs

\[
\gamma M^2\|p_0-q\|^2+2\gamma M\langle e_N,z\rangle
 +2\gamma M N_0(r)+2\gamma M N_{R,q}.
\]

The last normal term exactly cancels the `2gamma M N_R,q` in the R10 right-child payment. The initial-normal coefficient stays nonnegative provided

\[
\gamma\le\chi(1-A/M).
\tag{2.6}
\]

This condition is uniform here. Since `h=s+b>=s+1=C` and `2kappa>=1`,

`2kappa h >= 2kappa C >= A` and `M=A+(d-1)+2kappa h>=2A`.

Consequently `gamma<=chi/2` suffices. The actual leaf constants obey it: `1/1200<1/8`. This establishes compatibility of the endpoint NORMAL coefficients only; it establishes no endpoint quadratic bound.

### 2.4 Exact full transport, with both child surpluses visible

Let

\[
N_p=\langle v-p,p-r\rangle,\quad
K_p=h\left(1+\frac C{D+k}\right),\quad
K_r=\frac{hC}{D+k}-\chi\Delta,
\]
\[
\Theta=\frac{h[CD-k(C+D+k)]}{D+k},\qquad
\mathrm{Fan}_R=\sum_{i\in R}h_i\langle n_R,r-g_i\rangle.
\]

Then the complete identity is

\[
M F_P=A F_L+M F_R+\mathcal Q+\mathcal N,
\tag{2.7}
\]

where

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal Q={}&\chi\bigl[
\|R_0\|^2-\Delta^2\|e\|^2
-2\Delta\sigma h\langle r,e\rangle
-2A(M-A)\langle r,u\rangle\bigr]
+\gamma A B_L+\Theta\|e\|^2\\
&+2\delta\bigl(Mh\|r\|^2-As\|p\|^2\bigr)\\
&+\eta\bigl[
2\kappa hD\|u_R\|^2+2D\sigma h\langle u_R,q\rangle
-\sigma^2h^2(\|q\|^2-\|r\|^2)\bigr]\\
&+\gamma MD\|z\|^2-\gamma M^2\|p_0-q\|^2
-2\gamma M\langle e_N,z\rangle,
\tag{2.8}
\end{aligned}
\]

and

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal N={}&2K_pN_p+2K_rN_r
+2\chi A N_0(\ell)
+2[\chi(M-A)-\gamma M]N_0(r)\\
&+\frac{4\eta\kappa h}{D}\mathrm{Fan}_R
+\frac{2\eta hd}{D}N_{R,q}.
\tag{2.9}
\end{aligned}
\]

The actual nonnegative quantity `gamma A B_L` is left intact in (2.8), including its normal part. The negative left discount square `-2delta As||p||^2` is present.

To check the right quadratic in (2.8), let `t0=-D u_R+sigma h(q-r)`. The R10 payment contributes `eta MD||u_R||^2+gamma MD||z||^2`; the remaining canonical transport contributes `-eta||t0||^2-2eta sigma h<r,t0>`. Expanding gives the displayed right bracket. The right normal coefficients in (2.9) use exactly

`(M-D)(D-s)/D-sigma h=hd/D`.

If `K_r<0`, the negative root coefficient is not discarded. The actual bridge identity is

\[
N_p+N_r=(D+k)\langle p,e\rangle-D\|e\|^2.
\]

Move `2K_r[(D+k)<p,e>-D||e||^2]` into `Q` and replace its root normal part by `2(h+chi Delta)N_p`. This coefficient is positive: for `Delta<0`, `h+chi Delta>=h+Delta=s+alpha>0`; the other case is immediate. Equations (2.7)--(2.9), with this optional exact rewrite, contain no uncontrolled independent negative normal.

The direct preservation rule would now use only `F_L,F_R>=0` and prove `Q+N>=0` on every actual datum. That last claim is false, as follows.

## 3. An arbitrarily deep actual obstruction to that payment rule

### 3.1 Exact limiting trajectories on one compact body

Use `K=[-1,1]`, so `P(t)=max(-1,min(1,t))`. Write

\[
c_0=1+\sqrt2,\quad a_0=1/c_0=\sqrt2-1.
\]

First consider the limiting parameters `epsilon=0`, only to construct a strict inequality that will subsequently be transferred to finite allowed enclosing m. Superscript zero indicates these limiting words. Their exact masses and bridges satisfy

\[
S_j^0=c_0^j-1,\quad C_j^0=c_0^j,\quad k_j^0=a_0 C_j^0.
\tag{3.1}
\]

The actual scalar map is odd, and

\[
T_{H_j^0}(k_j^0)=(-1)^j a_0.
\tag{3.2}
\]

For j=1 this is the step `sqrt(2)` from 1. For the induction, `k_j^0=C_(j-1)^0=S_(j-1)^0+1`. Every query of the first H_(j-1) child is at least 1, and that child ends at 1. Its bridge sends 1 to `-k_(j-1)^0`. Oddness and the induction hypothesis prove (3.2).

Take any even j, and the parent H_(j+1)^0 with start

`x=C=s+1`, `s=S_j^0`, and trailing `d=1`.

Its left child has every gradient equal to 1 and ends at `v=p=1`. Its bridge sends this to `w=-k=-a0 C`. Its right child ends at

`y=q=-a0`.

All relevant auxiliaries are the true interior projections

\[
\ell=C/A,\quad p_0=C/M,\quad r=-a_0C/A,
\quad D=A,
\quad S=c_0C-1.
\tag{3.3}
\]

Thus `n_L=n_0=n_R=v-p=0`. Every normal in (2.9) vanishes; so do the optional bridge rewrite and all the radial normal rows. These are actual data from the one fixed body, not independently chosen vectors or normal values.

### 3.2 Exact child surplus and exact parent deficit

For the left child,

\[
R_L=B_L=\frac{\sigma^2s^2}{A},\quad
E_L=\frac{s^2+2\delta s}{A},\quad
E_L-R_L=2\delta s.
\tag{3.4}
\]

The equality in the last expression uses `1-sigma^2=4kappa delta`. Hence

\[
F_L=\frac{[1-(\eta+\gamma)\sigma^2]s^2+2\delta s}{A}.
\tag{3.5}
\]

The actual right child has

\[
R_R=B_R=a_0^2 R_L,\quad E_R=a_0^2 E_L,
\quad F_R=a_0^2F_L.
\tag{3.6}
\]

For example, its comparison mixture equals q exactly, because `w=(s+1)q`; this checks `R_R=B_R` without a norm estimate.

The actual parent satisfies

\[
M F_P=C^2-Ma_0^2-\eta\sigma^2S^2a_0^2
-\gamma(C+Ma_0)^2.
\tag{3.7}
\]

Since all normal terms vanish, (2.7) gives the following exact value for the completed payment expression:

\[
\mathcal Q=
C^2-Ma_0^2-\eta\sigma^2S^2a_0^2-\gamma(C+Ma_0)^2
-(A+Ma_0^2)F_L.
\tag{3.8}
\]

As even j tends to infinity, `C/s->1`, `S/s->c0`, and `c0 a0=1`. Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\mathcal Q}{s^2}\longrightarrow
-a_0(1-\eta\sigma^2)
-\gamma\bigl[(1+2\kappa)^2-\sqrt2\,\sigma^2\bigr].}
\tag{3.9}
\]

For any fixed `kappa in [16/17,1)`, `0<=eta<=1`, and `gamma>=0`, this limit is strictly negative. Indeed `sigma^2<1`, and the second bracket is positive: `(1+2kappa)^2>=4` while `sqrt(2)sigma^2<=sqrt(2)<2`. Thus even choosing another common retained fraction cannot make this same discarded-surplus payment work at a discounted kappa.

For the selected constants the children satisfy their proposed invariant strictly on this family: (3.5) is positive. The parent invariant itself also holds for all sufficiently large j, because

\[
\frac{M F_P}{s^2}\longrightarrow
1-\eta\sigma^2-\gamma(1+2\kappa)^2
\ge\frac14-\frac9{1200}>0.
\tag{3.10}
\]

The negative quantity is solely the remainder after BOTH positive child surpluses have been discarded.

### 3.3 Transfer of the strict failure to literal inherited parameters

This construction is not confined to an inadmissible limiting word. Fix any sufficiently large even j for which (3.8) is strictly negative. For every finite allowed enclosing m use the actual parameters prescribed in Section 1 and the actual start

`x=S_j(m)+1`.

All left-child physical queries are at least 1, so the left child still ends exactly at `p=v=1`. The bridge starts the actual right child at `-k_j(m)`. Define, rather than assign,

`y_m=T_(H_j(m))(-k_j(m))`.

Projection onto the fixed interval is continuous, and the finite word's steps are continuous in epsilon. Consequently, as m tends to infinity with this j fixed,

`y_m -> -a0`, together with convergence of every physical and auxiliary sample. For all sufficiently large m, `|y_m|<1`, so `q=y_m=P(y_m)`, and all auxiliary projections in (3.3) remain interior. Every normal retained in (2.9) still vanishes. The complete expression (2.8) is continuous in these actual data, so it remains strictly negative.

Choose such an enclosing m also larger than `max(1536,j+1)`. This is a literal internal H_(j+1) of the requested hierarchy, with no child parameter reset. Since even j can be arbitrarily large, this gives arbitrarily deep actual inherited failures of the direct payment rule. This is an analytic continuity argument with a strict limiting sign, not a finite numerical screen or a solver-generated example.

What it rules out precisely is:

> Replace both actual child gaps by exactly `eta R+gamma B`, discard the entire nonnegative child surplus, and prove the remaining expression is nonnegative by the retained actual radial and bridge relations.

That assertion fails on actual data. It does not rule out retaining quantitative child surplus, changing the joint quantity, using additional original-query inequalities, proving the U law directly, or disproving the original minimax conjecture by another family.

## 4. Approach 2: keep the complete signed energies and original cross-subtree rows

The lost surplus in (3.4) is order s, and its contribution to (2.7) is order s squared. Treating it as an arbitrarily small numerical correction cannot repair the recurrence. The second approach keeps the full quantities rather than discarding them.

For a child or parent define the actual signed energy

\[
V_d(x,y;s)=\|x\|^2-\|y+sP(y/d)\|^2.
\tag{4.1}
\]

It satisfies the exact identity

\[
E=R+\frac{V_d}{d+2\kappa s}+2\delta s\|P(y/d)\|^2.
\tag{4.2}
\]

No positivity of `V_d` at higher children is assumed. In particular, (4.2) is not an all-level terminal-norm reserve assertion.

At the same actual parent put

\[
V_L=\|x\|^2-\|v+sp\|^2,\quad
V_R=\|w\|^2-\|y+sq\|^2,\quad
V_P=\|x\|^2-\|y+Sq\|^2.
\]

Use the physical bridge normal `n_p=v-p` and the actual terminal auxiliary normal `n_q=y-dq`. If the right-child gradients are `g_i`, define the original-query sums

\[
\mathcal A_p=\sum_{i\in R}h_i\langle n_p,p-g_i\rangle,\qquad
\mathcal A_q=\sum_{i\in R}h_i\langle n_q,q-g_i\rangle,
\]
\[
I_p=\langle n_p,p-q\rangle,\qquad
I_q=\langle n_q,q-p\rangle.
\tag{4.3}
\]

All four are nonnegative on the original common body. Every right query appears with its actual step weight.

Set `L0=s+d-k`. This is positive for the inherited hierarchy: its exact mass formula is

\[
C_j=\frac{a}{c-2}c^j+\left(1-\frac{a}{c-2}\right)2^j,
\]

where `0<a/(c-2)<1`; thus `k_j/C_j<c-2<1/2` in the stated parameter range. Hence `L0>=C-k>0`.

Write `G_R=w-y=n_p-n_q-kp-dq`. Expansion of the two squares defining V_R and the two weighted sums gives

\[
V_R+\mathcal A_p+\mathcal A_q
=L_0(\langle n_p,p\rangle-\langle n_q,q\rangle)
-d I_p-k I_q+k^2\|p\|^2-(d+s)^2\|q\|^2.
\tag{4.4}
\]

Since `U_1(v)-U_d(y)=||p||^2-d||q||^2+2<n_p,p>-2<n_q,q>`, (4.4) is equivalent to

\[
\begin{aligned}
L_0[U_1(v)-U_d(y)]={}&2V_R+2\mathcal A_p+2\mathcal A_q
+2d I_p+2k I_q\\
&+(L_0-2k^2)\|p\|^2
+[2(d+s)^2-dL_0]\|q\|^2.
\tag{4.5}
\end{aligned}
\]

This is a retained actual-normal version of the sufficient-decrease calculation: the signed V_R is still visible. The source check of Zhang--Jiang's Definitions 4--7 and Section 3.1 shows that their primitive sufficient-decrease use requires the right child to be primitive. That hypothesis is not supplied for higher H_j here. The equations above are derived directly, without importing it. [Primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2).

The elementary parent square expansion is

\[
V_P=V_L+V_R+h\{U_1(v)-U_d(y)
+(s-k)\|p\|^2-(d+2s+h)\|q\|^2\}.
\]

Inserting (4.5) yields the full recurrence

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
V_P={}&V_L+\left(1+\frac{2h}{L_0}\right)V_R
+\frac{h A_0}{L_0}(\|p\|^2-\|q\|^2)\\
&+\frac{2h}{L_0}(\mathcal A_p+\mathcal A_q+d I_p+k I_q),\\
A_0={}&(C-k)(s+d)-kC-k^2.
\end{aligned}}
\tag{4.6}
\]

For `d=1`, `A0=C^2-2Ck-k^2`. This is the full signed identity, not just the scalar joining capacity. Its actual retained cross-subtree rows vanish on the interval equality trajectory in Section 3 because both `n_p` and `n_q` vanish. Thus these specific added rows cannot, by themselves, supply a uniform positive surplus on that mode.

The right-child multiplier in (4.6) is greater than one. At limiting silver parameters and d=1 it equals `c0^2`. A useful lower bound on the signed V_R must therefore be propagated quantitatively; replacing it by zero would assume unproved higher primitive positivity.

The negative root sign also occurs within the actual inherited depth range, not only in a formal fixed-epsilon extension. Write

\[
\frac{k_j}{C_j}=\frac{c-2}{1+\tau(2/c)^j},\qquad
\tau=\frac{c(c-2)-\beta}{\beta}
=\frac{(5+2\sqrt2)\epsilon+c_0^2\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon}.
\]

For `epsilon<=1/6144`, `0<tau<9epsilon`. For every `j>=3`,

`a0 tau(2/c)^j < 72epsilon/c0^4 < c0 epsilon`,

where the last inequality uses `c0^5=41+29sqrt(2)>72`. Since `c-2=a0+c0 epsilon`, it follows that `k_j/C_j>a0`. Thus `A0=C^2-2Ck-k^2<0` at d=1 for every actual inherited `3<=j<m`. Its gradient-norm difference has no fixed sign on actual bodies. The signed right-child energy and this root term prevent concluding a nonnegative V or a sufficient discounted U law by dropping the remaining terms.

On the limiting interval trajectory both child energies are exactly zero, and (4.2) recovers precisely the discount surplus in (3.4)--(3.6). This checks the second transport against the actual obstruction. It does not justify imposing `V_L,V_R>=0` on other inherited data. No all-depth lower estimate for the signed-energy/normal combination in (4.6), and no replacement joint invariant that supplies one, was obtained within this grant.

## 5. Audit, scope, and stop condition

The principal draft result is the actual arbitrarily deep failure of the completed discarded-surplus payment, not a new unpaid matrix proposed as progress toward closure. Its proof checks the entire scalar physical path, both actual input projections, the parent endpoint transport, all relevant normal values, the negative left discount square, the signs at all trailing parameters in the general identity, and the transfer from limiting parameters to genuine same-enclosing-m children. The interval has an attained original minimizer zero; no radius change is used. The example is not invoked as a full-class worst-case upper or lower certificate.

The original U target is not refuted. The common two-remainder invariant is not refuted. In fact, Section 3 gives positive child and parent invariant surplus for the selected constants while the proposed payment expression is negative. Thus no conclusion about the truth of the target is drawn from failure of this warrant.

No final all-depth coefficient recurrence was established. There is no complete full-class asymptotic separation, no all-schedule lower proof, and no claim that the original minimax problem is solved. A finished-object independent adversarial audit was not triggered because there is no complete target-level candidate. The explicit identities and obstruction received manual text/source checks only; Danus verification has not accepted them.

Sources read: current OPERATOR; complete original PROBLEM; complete TASK; complete R10B mathematical elaboration; complete R10 PROOF_ATTEMPT; complete R11 parent input-normal note; relevant full statements of accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` and `1e0bf3f41457d8e4`; the cited Zhang--Jiang definitions and sufficient-decrease proof; mathematical-academic-writing and its mathematical-integrity/source-audit references. An initially combined read was truncated; required text was reread in bounded sections. The memory registry was used only for the evidence boundary; current authorization supersedes its historical pause.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001624 -->
# R10b handoff: genuine reservoir recursion, scoped obstruction, no target closure

2026-10-09. Grant `d18f0ac50208916a`. **All new mathematics UNVERIFIED. Original Goal ACTIVE / OPEN.** This is the closeout of one gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation with two approaches. No grant renewal is implied.

## Read first

Read this packet's complete PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. It contains the actual construction, its chronology, all original-radius terms, the complete-H_m estimates, and the limits of the conclusions. The exact original target and every integer split of C_n/B_n are retained in Section 1. The governing source hashes were checked and are recorded there.

There is no complete uniform all-word lower theorem, no full-class asymptotic upper certificate, and no complete target-level candidate for an adversarial audit. Do not promote either scoped ceiling below into an upper bound on W_n(H_m).

## Approach 1: an actual movable-wall reserve, then an all-depth obstruction

The construction genuinely consumes a terminal reserve. It uses the parent's fixed weighted Euclidean K_a envelope. A frozen set D has current values <=0; a nonempty free set F is unsaturated at every already-built future physical query. At a reverse step h=1+d>1:

1. Retire any subset J of F, leaving F' nonempty. On J enlarge a_i to max(a_i,(Y_i)_+/d); old frozen walls stay fixed.
2. Put D'=D union J and t=[d E(a 1_D')-E(Y 1_D')]/P(F'). This is nonnegative.
3. Enlarge walls of the remaining free atoms to max(a_i,(Y_i-t)/d).
4. Set X_i=Y_i-h a_i on D' and X_i=ht/d-Y_i/d on F'.

All modified atoms were unsaturated at every old physical query. The guard holds, the projection mean is zero, both branches give the same Y, new frozen values remain <=0, and remaining free atoms are also unsaturated at the new query. The final labels are fixed before forward execution. The operation works for leaves and caps, with no omitted d>=1 case.

A concrete terminal law has one atom of mass 1/2 at -q and M>=n+1 free atoms of masses 1/(2M) at q+eta_i, with mean eta zero and |eta_i|<=1/2. Initial walls are 1. Terminal twice-value is 2q-1+(mean eta_i^2)/2 and is preserved throughout. Retiring one reserve atom per reverse step is therefore a real whole-H_m construction with many terminal values.

The exact predecessor radius is

    E[1_D' (Y-h a)^2] + d^-2 E[1_F' (ht-Y)^2].

Thus large wall labels are charged, including their h^2 E_D' a^2 contribution. This construction uses direct radius accounting and no auxiliary-potential identity. Its required auxiliary list is empty; any optional auxiliary records further restrict movable atoms.

The resulting active sets only expand in forward time. The proof goes beyond this particular policy: **every** nested-active-set trajectory on K_a, with any finite weights, walls, and terminal shape, satisfies on the complete inherited H_m

    2 F_a(X_n) / ||X_0-0||^2 <= 1/D_m.

The proof refines every face expansion into weighted orthogonal face-normal increments. Simultaneously entering atoms are ordered by their old clipping slack; this supplies the initial-coefficient bounds needed to charge every released mode. Each released mode has payoff at most (c^2/u^2)c^(-2m), with u=a_h/(c-2)>1/2. Only the unreleased affine mode attains 1/D_m. Zero-increment directions have zero initial coefficient and are treated explicitly.

At terminal twice-value B=B_(n_m), this means Var X_0>=B D_m and B times the payoff <47/50, using the accepted comparison supplied in the task. The obstruction covers the entire monotone-reserve family, not just a two-point endpoint or one-step greedy choice.

**Minimizer scope is essential.** The denominator is the original radius to the selected point 0. Positive walls make this minimum unique. With zero walls, this does not exclude a different normalization at another original minimizer of the final fixed body. A zero-wall repair may change the minimum cone. No closest-minimum theorem is claimed.

## Approach 2: pay for opposite-face reflection

This approach changes the actual mechanism. For a selected physical step 1+d and complete-word prefix/suffix sums P,R, use the fixed asymmetric Huber function with gradient clip(x,[-A,1]). Take

    A=(1+2R)/d,
    x_0=-A(1+P),
    x_before_bridge=-A,
    x_after_bridge=1+2R,
    x_n=1+R.

The entire prefix is on the negative saturated face, the bridge changes faces, and the entire suffix is on the positive saturated face. Its original squared radius is A^2(1+P)^2 and actual terminal twice-value is 1+2R. Its payoff is exactly

    L_i(h)=d^2 / [(1+P)^2 (1+2R)].

This is optimal over terminal q>=1 within this one-reflection component, including its choice of A. Zeros elsewhere in the word cause no problem.

A recursive reserve bank adds one such component per eligible bridge greater than 1 in a binary parse, always using GLOBAL prefix/suffix sums; bridges at most 1 add no reflection component. Its function is an orthogonal sum of scaled interval envelopes, and every component executes the complete word. It has several terminal values and genuine face changes, and its total radius and objective are exact sums. The normalized payoff is their radius-weighted average. This product body is not represented as a coupled simplex.

For every occurrence in the complete H_m, if s=S_j is its child mass, then P,R>=s and d/(1+s)<=c-2<3/7. The proof obtains

    D_m L_i(H_m) <= (c-2)^2 [3+2(c-2)] <243/343<1.

Thus every paid-reflection reserve in this explicit bank is worse than the baseline Huber component, and no positive mixture improves it. The loss comes from the charged original preparation radius, not an invalid free wall change.

## What remains genuinely outside the two exclusions

A coupled K_a trajectory with repeated active-face exits and reentries is not covered. It must make correlations between reserve coordinates pay for the reflected histories; independent reserve sums cannot do that. A different original minimizer for a final body with zero walls is also outside the selected-0 ceiling. Neither mechanism has been constructed at benchmark scale in this invocation.

The result is therefore a realizable but quantitatively insufficient recursive construction, plus a separately paid recycling attempt that also fails. Do not state that the requested recursive terminal-reservoir theorem has been obtained. The original all-word problem remains unresolved.

## Resource and administration receipt

- Native invocation: 1, assigned gpt-6-astra / ultra.
- Substantive analytical approaches: 2.
- Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, verifier calls: 0.
- Extra agents/models, renewals, worker/service changes: 0.

- Goal, budget.json, OPERATOR, CURRENT_RUN, truth stores, other packets, publication, finalization and revocation: untouched.
- No private worker memory was read.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001625 -->
# R10b: a realizable reservoir recursion, its all-depth obstruction, and paid face changes

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft. Original minimax target ACTIVE / OPEN.**

Grant `d18f0ac50208916a`. One assigned native gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation; exactly two substantive approaches; mathematical programs, arrays, solvers, formal submissions, extra agents and extra model calls: zero. This packet does not contain a complete target-level candidate.

The main outcome is an actual backward construction using movable walls, followed by an all-depth obstruction to its entire monotone-face class. It handles an arbitrarily large finite terminal reservoir, not just the previously excluded two-point policies. The second approach pays for a real change between opposite saturated faces; it gives a recursive bank of full-word witnesses with exact radii, but its independent reserves also fail on the complete inherited hierarchy. Neither obstruction is an upper bound on the unrestricted worst-case value.

## 1. Target, sources, and notation

The required lower theorem remains

\[
 \forall\epsilon>0\ \exists N_\epsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\epsilon\quad
 \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:\qquad
 \mathcal W_n(h)\ge(1-\epsilon)/B_n,
 \qquad
 \mathcal W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_*)}{L\|x_0-x_*\|^2}.
\]

The alternative is a certified asymptotic separation for the full function class. We retain W = 2R, the original chosen minimizer, the original radius, the actual final iterate, all finite dimensions, and every predetermined nonnegative word. The exact benchmark includes **every** integer split k+l=n-1:

\[
 C_0=B_0=1,\quad C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),\quad
 B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l),\quad U_n^{\rm ZJ}=B_n^{-1},
\]
\[
 \Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,\qquad
 G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2.
\]

Read in full: this TASK, OPERATOR.md, PROBLEM.md, R9_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md, R10_PARENT_VARIABLE_WALL_NOTE_20261009.md, and inverse_pp_joint_attainability_20261009_r10/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. Checked source hashes:

- TASK: `428b28ce15e41780f95b0796f7be6e21a5c1a6b2f611a4994bb2042d983f2164`.
- Parent variable-wall note: `8a77974afa9995b0758ec77c96305de0d732dc1722680339c4823c9a8d725040`.
- Previous inverse-policy proof: `3b719f94f9f2c8afcdab61869fda6fefcebf6b4f1eee1af51788c09802a84f6b`.

The previous invocation's two policies are not renewed. The accepted-project comparison B_(n_m)/D_m < 47/50 for m >= 1536 is used only with the scope reported in the supplied handoff; it was not independently reverified here. All new results below remain UNVERIFIED.

For the diagnostic hierarchy, fix one enclosing m >= 1536 and use its parameters in every child:

\[
 \sigma=1+\sqrt2,\quad \varepsilon=1/(4m),\quad
 \beta_h=1-\varepsilon,\quad c=\sigma(1+\varepsilon),\quad
 a_h=\beta_h/c,
\]
\[
 H_0=\varnothing,\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\quad
 d_j=a_hc^j,\quad S_j=\sum H_j,\quad D_j=1+2S_j.
\]

The scalar a_h is unrelated to the coordinate wall vector a below. Put

\[
 u=\frac{a_h}{c-2}.
\]

The exact sum and product formulas are

\[
 S_j+1=uc^j+(1-u)2^j,\qquad
 P_j:=\prod_{h\in H_j}(h-1)=\beta_h^{2^j-1}c^{-j},
 \qquad d_jP_j=\beta_h^{2^j}/c<1/c.
 \tag{1.1}
\]

Here 1/2 < u < 1, 2 < c < 17/7 < 5/2, and D_j < 2c^j for j>=1. For example u<1 follows from c(c-2)>1>beta_h; u>1/2 follows already from beta_h>3/4, c<5/2, and c-2<1/2. These elementary bounds, rather than a numerical recurrence, will suffice.

## 2. Approach 1: build and spend a finite pool of movable coordinates

### 2.1 One fixed function and the records that must be preserved

On a finite weighted probability space, work in the Euclidean space E={X: E X=0}, with squared norm E X^2. For fixed a_i>=0 and E a>0, define

\[
 K_a=\{g\in E:g_i\ge-a_i\},\qquad
 F_a(X)=\max_{g\in K_a}\{\mathbb E[Xg]-\tfrac12\mathbb E g^2\}.
\]

Its gradient is the Euclidean projection onto K_a. It is convex and globally 1-smooth, and 0 is a valid minimizer with value 0. The weighted isometry X_i -> sqrt(p_i)X_i gives an ordinary finite-dimensional Euclidean instance. All radii below are relative to this original 0.

**Radius scope.** When every wall is positive this minimizer is unique. When some walls are zero, the minimum set may contain other points. Every exclusion in Section 3 concerns the selected original minimizer **0** and the denominator ||X_0-0||^2. It does not bound a supremum over other selected minima, nor a denominator measured to the closest point of a polar minimum cone. A different final-body minimizer has not been constructed or analyzed here. Increasing a zero wall can shrink that cone, so an earlier choice of another minimizer cannot simply be retained through a repair.

At a query X, its projection is g_i=max(X_i-t,-a_i), E g=0. An atom is called free in the current backward record when it is unsaturated at **every** recorded later physical query. If a proof also uses auxiliary queries, they must be included in that record. Increasing its wall then preserves those recorded projections and values exactly, as in the parent note.

The following construction uses direct original-variance accounting, and does not invoke any auxiliary primitive-potential equality. Its required auxiliary record is therefore empty. If optional auxiliary queries are retained, only atoms unsaturated at those queries can remain in the movable pool. The all-depth obstruction below applies even before imposing those extra restrictions.

### 2.2 An actual backward step, with a reserve that can be consumed

Suppose a finite mean-zero next law Y and its entire future trajectory have been built. Partition its atoms into a nonempty movable pool F and a frozen set D. Maintain:

1. every atom in F is free in the full later record;
2. every atom in D has Y_i<=0;
3. walls of atoms in D will never be altered.

At a reverse step h=1+d>1, choose any subset J of F to retire, leaving F'=F\J nonempty. Set D'=D union J. For each newly retired atom choose

\[
 a_i' =\max\{a_i,(Y_i)_+/d\},\qquad i\in J.
 \tag{2.1}
\]

These increases preserve the whole later record because J was free. Keep all frozen walls unchanged. With A_D=E[a' 1_(D')] and p_F=P(F'), put

\[
 t=\frac{dA_D-\mathbb E[Y1_{D'}]}{p_F}.
 \tag{2.2}
\]

Every summand d a_i'-Y_i on D' is nonnegative, so t>=0. Now increase the remaining free walls, if necessary, to

\[
 a_i'\leftarrow\max\{a_i',(Y_i-t)/d\},\qquad i\in F'.
 \tag{2.3}
\]

This again preserves every old recorded query. It does not change (2.2), which depends only on D'. Define the predecessor by

\[
 X_i=\begin{cases}
 Y_i-ha_i',&i\in D',\\
 ht/d-Y_i/d,&i\in F'.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{2.4}
\]

This is the parent's genuine inverse with omega=1_(F'). Indeed, with Z=t+da'-Y,

\[
 Z\ge0,\qquad
 \mathbb E[1_{F'}Z]=p_Ft+d\mathbb E[a'1_{F'}]-\mathbb E[Y1_{F'}]
 =d\mathbb E a'.
\]

Thus E X=0; its actual projection at threshold t is -a' on D' and X-t on F'; and X-h P_(K_a')X=Y atom by atom. Atoms in F' are also unsaturated at this newly added query. Atoms in D' satisfy X_i<=0: for old frozen atoms this is immediate, and for newly retired positive Y_i, (2.1) gives X_i<=-Y_i/d<0. Therefore the three invariants hold for the next reverse step.

There is no omitted cap exception: (2.1)--(2.4) hold for every d>0, both d<1 and d>=1. A reflected free atom can become positive or negative; it remains free and is repaired by (2.3) if needed. A frozen atom is never reflected again. No saturation event is erased.

After a finite word, all wall labels, weights and X_0 have been fixed. Forward execution uses this one final function. Later wall modifications have preserved every previously constructed physical iterate and function value; there is no changing objective during that forward run.

### 2.3 A concrete multivalue terminal reserve and exact radius accounting

For a word of length n with every step >1, take M>=n+1 free atoms. Give one frozen atom mass 1/2 and terminal value -q. Give the M free atoms masses 1/(2M) and terminal values q+eta_i, where

\[
 q>2,\quad \sum_i\eta_i=0,\quad |\eta_i|\le1/2.
\]

Initially use wall 1 everywhere. The terminal threshold is q-1. The frozen gradient is -1, and the free gradients are 1+eta_i. Thus all M reserve atoms are genuinely unsaturated at the terminal query. Retiring one atom per reverse step leaves at least one movable atom throughout. This gives a complete deterministic construction, with a genuinely multivalue terminal law if the eta_i are not all equal.

Writing overline(eta^2)=M^-1 sum eta_i^2, its preserved terminal value and variance are

\[
 2F_a(X_n)=2q-1+\tfrac12\overline{\eta^2},\qquad
 \|X_n\|^2=q^2+\tfrac12\overline{\eta^2}.
 \tag{2.5}
\]

At every reverse step, the exact original-metric cost of the new predecessor is

\[
 \|X\|^2=
 \mathbb E[1_{D'}(Y-ha')^2]
 +d^{-2}\mathbb E[1_{F'}(ht-Y)^2].
 \tag{2.6}
\]

In particular the term h^2 E[1_(D') (a')^2] is paid. Large walls on rare newly retired atoms are not free merely because their terminal gradients stay unchanged. Equations (2.1)--(2.6), applied to the complete given word, specify actual finite values and an exact radius; they are not an unproved radius bound.

If desired, choose q so that (2.5) equals B_(n_m). The construction then has terminal benchmark value exactly B_(n_m). The next result shows why its radius cannot meet the required benchmark estimate on H_m.

## 3. All-depth obstruction for the entire monotone-face class

The recursion above has active sets that only expand in forward time. This is a restriction on the actual physical trajectory, not a guess about its endpoint law. The following derivation covers every K_a trajectory with that property, including arbitrary finite weights, arbitrary nonnegative walls, arbitrary terminal laws and arbitrary numbers of atoms.

### 3.1 Orthogonal geometry of nested simplex faces

For a nonempty coordinate set A, let

\[
 p_A=P(A),\quad b_A=\frac{\mathbb E[a1_{A^c}]}{p_A},\qquad
 (e_A)_i=\begin{cases}b_A,&i\in A,\\-a_i,&i\notin A,\end{cases}
\]
\[
 L_A=\{v:\operatorname{supp}v\subset A,\ \mathbb E v=0\}.
\]

The minimum-norm point on the face with g=-a off A is e_A. Its affine hull is e_A+L_A, and e_A is orthogonal to L_A. If the projection of X lies on this face and is unsaturated on A, then

\[
 g=e_A+P_{L_A}X.
 \tag{3.1}
\]

For A contained in B, e_A-e_B lies in L_B and is orthogonal to L_A and e_B. Refining a nested chain one added atom at a time therefore gives mutually orthogonal nonzero increments

\[
 v=e_A-e_{A\cup\{i\}}.
\]

Each such v is a supporting normal of K_a on the smaller face: its largest coordinate is constant on A, and all other coordinates are smaller. Since g+a>=0 and E(g+a)=E a,

\[
 \langle g,v\rangle\le\|v\|^2\quad(g\in K_a),
 \tag{3.2}
\]

with equality on that smaller face. This uses the actual weighted Euclidean geometry; no non-isometric coordinate transform is inserted.

### 3.2 Simultaneous releases and their chronology

Suppose the actual active sets satisfy

\[
 A_0\subseteq A_1\subseteq\cdots\subseteq A_n.
 \tag{3.3}
\]

Boundary atoms may be assigned consistently to either side. At a step from A=A_(r-1) to B=A_r, define the old slack for each entering atom by

\[
 s_i=t_{r-1}-a_i-X_{r-1,i}\ge0.
\]

Refine this simultaneous release by adding the entering atoms in nondecreasing order of s_i. This refinement introduces no extra physical time or query; it only chooses an orthogonal basis.

For an intermediate set A'=A union J and the next entering atom i, direct averaging at the old query gives

\[
 \operatorname{mean}_{A'} X_{r-1}-X_{r-1,i}
 =b_{A'}+a_i+s_i-\frac{\mathbb E[1_Js]}{p_{A'}}.
 \tag{3.4}
\]

The last two terms have nonnegative difference by the chosen order. For the associated nonzero increment v, its normalized X coefficient is

\[
 \xi_{r-1}:=\frac{\langle X_{r-1},v\rangle}{\|v\|^2}
 =\frac{\operatorname{mean}_{A'}X_{r-1}-X_{r-1,i}}{b_{A'}+a_i}
 \ge1.
 \tag{3.5}
\]

At all earlier physical queries the actual face is contained in the smaller refined face, so the gradient coefficient on v is exactly 1. At and after time r it equals the X coefficient, by (3.1). At time r, (3.2) gives xi_r<=1. If S_<r denotes the sum of steps preceding the release step and that step is h=1+d, these facts give the exact chronology

\[
 \xi_{r-1}=\xi_0-S_{<r}\ge1,\qquad
 \xi_r=\xi_0-S_{<r}-h\le1,
\]
\[
 S_{<r}+1\le\xi_0\le S_{<r}+h+1.
 \tag{3.6}
\]

After the release, xi is multiplied by 1-h_t at every subsequent step. Thus the contribution of this entire mode to terminal twice-value, divided by its original squared radius contribution, is at most

\[
 \max\left\{
 \frac{d^2\prod_{\text{after }r}(h_t-1)^2}{(S_{<r}+1)^2},\quad
 \frac{\prod_{\text{after }r}(h_t-1)^2}{(S_{<r}+h+1)^2}
 \right\}.
 \tag{3.7}
\]

The maximum occurs at an endpoint of (3.6), because |1-(S_<r+h)/xi_0| has its maximum there. Importantly, an atom that enters late cannot bring an arbitrary uncharged quadratic deviation: (3.4)--(3.6) tie that deviation to its original radius.

For completeness, zero walls do not create a missing uncharged direction. A zero increment means b_(A')=a_i=0. All walls outside A' are then zero. Use the contrast direction positive and constant on A', negative on i, and zero elsewhere. Its old gradient coefficient is zero. By (3.4) and the same slack ordering its old X coefficient is nonnegative. Feasibility of every g in K_a makes its gradient coefficient nonpositive. After i is active the gradient coefficient equals the X coefficient; that X coefficient did not change in the release step because its old gradient coefficient was zero. It must therefore vanish. Its original coefficient also vanishes. Such directions can be discarded, rather than divided by a zero norm.

### 3.3 The remaining modes and exact original-radius comparison

Directions in L_(A_0) are quadratic from time 0 and have terminal-to-initial squared ratio product_t(h_t-1)^2. The last face has an unreleased normal e=e_(A_n). If e is nonzero and xi_t=<X_t,e>/||e||^2, its gradient coefficient is 1 throughout, so xi_n=xi_0-S_n. Projection optimality against 0 gives xi_n>=1. Its terminal twice-value is

\[
 (2\xi_n-1)\|e\|^2,
\]

and its original radius contribution is xi_0^2||e||^2. Their ratio satisfies

\[
 \frac{2(\xi_0-S_n)-1}{\xi_0^2}\le\frac1{1+2S_n}.
 \tag{3.8}
\]

Any residual original component orthogonal to L_(A_n) and e is orthogonal to every gradient in the chain; it stays constant and contributes only nonnegative additional radius. At the terminal point the envelope formula is exactly

\[
 2F_a(X_n)=\|P_{L_{A_n}}X_n\|^2
            +2\langle X_n,e\rangle-\|e\|^2.
 \tag{3.9}
\]

Equations (3.7)--(3.9) therefore bound the actual full ratio by the largest mode ratio. There is no replacement of the original radius by a radius at a release time.

### 3.4 Evaluate the bound on the complete H_m

Every suffix product of the positive excesses h-1 in H_j is at most 1. This follows by induction: a suffix either lies in the right child, or contains that child and the bridge, whose product is d_(j-1)P_(j-1)<1/c, times a suffix of the left child. Empty suffixes have product 1.

Let E_j be the largest of the two **unsquared** expressions in (3.7), over release steps anywhere in the complete H_j, with the real prefix sums in that word. For a release in its left child, the two quantities are multiplied by d_(j-1)P_(j-1)<1/c. At the bridge and throughout the right child, the denominators are at least 1+S_(j-1), while the required suffix products are at most 1. Therefore

\[
 E_j\le\max\{E_{j-1}/c,\ (1+S_{j-1})^{-1}\},\qquad E_0=0,
\]
\[
 E_j\le\frac1{u c^{j-1}}\quad(j\ge1).
 \tag{3.10}
\]

Consequently every released-mode payoff on H_m is at most

\[
 E_m^2\le(c^2/u^2)c^{-2m},
\]

and every initially quadratic mode is at most P_m^2<c^(-2m). For m>=1536 these are below 1/D_m: it suffices that c^m>2c^2/u^2, whose right side is <50, whereas c^m>2^m. The affine bound (3.8) is 1/D_m. Thus

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \frac{2F_a(X_n)}{\|X_0\|^2}\le\frac1{D_m}
 \quad\text{for every nested-active-set }K_a\text{ trajectory on }H_m.
 \quad}
 \tag{3.11}
\]

The standard constant-gradient Huber trajectory attains 1/D_m, so this upper bound within the stated trajectory class **with selected minimizer 0** is exact. In particular the concrete reserve recursion in Section 2 obeys

\[
 \|X_0\|^2\ge D_m\,[2F_a(X_n)].
\]

At terminal benchmark value B=B_(n_m), it requires original variance at least B D_m; its benchmark-normalized payoff is at most B/D_m<47/50. This rules out the whole monotone-reserve invariant, even with arbitrarily many terminal values and different walls. Adding old auxiliary-query preservation requirements only restricts this class further.

The conclusion does **not** cover a trajectory that leaves and later reenters an active face. It does not prove W_(n_m)(H_m)<=1/D_m for general functions or for all K_a trajectories.

## 4. Approach 2: pay for a real change between opposite saturated faces

The preceding obstruction forces actual recycling, so this approach abandons nested active sets. It uses a different fixed body when multiple reserves are combined: an orthogonal product of interval projection bodies. Each component executes the whole physical word and has a fully charged original radius.

### 4.1 An exact paid-reflection component for an arbitrary word

Take any predetermined nonnegative word and a selected step h_i=1+d>1. Write

\[
 P=\sum_{t<i}h_t,\qquad R=\sum_{t>i}h_t.
\]

For A>0 define the asymmetric Huber function

\[
 f_A(x)=\max_{g\in[-A,1]}\{xg-g^2/2\}.
\]

It is convex, 1-smooth, and minimized at the original 0. Its gradient is clip(x,[-A,1]). It is also a two-atom K_a instance in the weighted mean-zero metric: take X=(x,-x), equal weights, and a=(A,1).

Choose any q>=1, and fix

\[
 A=(q+R)/d,\qquad x_0=-A(1+P).
 \tag{4.1}
\]

Throughout the prefix the gradient is -A and the trajectory ends at x_i=-A. The chosen step gives

\[
 x_{i+1}=dA=q+R.
\]

Throughout the suffix the gradient is 1, and the actual terminal value is x_n=q. The two opposite gradient faces are attained; clipping is strict at the initial query when P>0, and at the post-bridge query when q+R>1. In particular both strict events occur at an internal bridge of H_m with nonempty children. Every step, including zeros elsewhere in the word, is accounted for. The wall A is chosen once before forward execution.

The exact original radius and terminal twice-value are

\[
 x_0^2=A^2(1+P)^2,\qquad 2f_A(x_n)=2q-1,
\]

so the paid payoff is

\[
 \frac{d^2(2q-1)}{(1+P)^2(q+R)^2}.
\]

Its maximum over q>=1 is attained at q=R+1. Thus a real full-word witness has

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 L_i(h)=\frac{(h_i-1)^2}{(1+P)^2(1+2R)},\quad
 A=\frac{1+2R}{h_i-1},\quad x_0=-A(1+P),\quad x_n=1+R.
 \quad}
 \tag{4.2}
\]

The factor A^2(1+P)^2 is exactly the cost of preparing the reflected reserve through the entire prefix. It cannot be replaced by the radius at x_i=-A. This is an actual face-change construction, not an increase of a formerly saturated wall while pretending its old query survives.

### 4.2 A recursive bank with several terminal values

One can build a finite reserve bank on any binary parse of the physical word: at each node whose bridge exceeds 1, add a component (4.2), using the bridge's **global** P and R in the complete word; recurse into the left and right node lists. Nodes with bridge at most 1 add no reflection component. Also retain a baseline Huber component with payoff 1/(1+2 sum h).

For arbitrary positive spatial scales lambda_i, set

\[
 F(x)=\sum_i\lambda_i^2 f_{A_i}(x_i/\lambda_i),
 \qquad (x_0)_i=\lambda_i[-A_i(1+P_i)].
 \tag{4.3}
\]

The gradient is 1-Lipschitz in the ordinary product Euclidean norm. The selected minimizer is 0. Every component runs the same entire physical word without a reset. Its terminal coordinate is lambda_i(1+R_i), so the bank can have many terminal values and many actual face changes at different times. Its original squared radius and terminal objective are exactly the sums of the component quantities.

Consequently its total normalized payoff is the radius-weighted average of its component payoffs. The bank cannot exceed their largest value. This statement is about the explicit product body (4.3); it is not a claim that a coupled simplex K_a decomposes into independent reservoirs.

### 4.3 Quantitative obstruction on every node of the complete H_m

Consider a step at level j in H_m. Its excess is d=a_h c^j. Put s=S_j. The actual global prefix and suffix sums satisfy P>=s and R>=s, because the occurrence has a full H_j child on each side. Also

\[
 \frac d{1+s}\le c-2=:r<3/7.
 \tag{4.4}
\]

Let T=P+R=S_m-(1+d). At fixed T, the denominator in (4.2) is

\[
 (1+P)^2[1+2(T-P)].
\]

Its logarithm is concave on s<=P<=T-s, so its minimum occurs at an endpoint. The smaller endpoint is P=s: the function (1+x)^2/(1+2x) is increasing for x>=0. Therefore

\[
 D_m L_i(H_m)
 \le\frac{d^2}{(1+s)^2}\,
 \frac{D_m}{D_m-2s-2(1+d)}.
\]

The last fraction decreases as D_m increases, and the actual condition R>=s gives D_m>=4s+2d+3. Hence

\[
 D_m L_i(H_m)
 \le\left(\frac d{1+s}\right)^2
       \frac{4s+2d+3}{2s+1}
 \le r^2(3+2r)
 <\frac{243}{343}<1.
 \tag{4.5}
\]

This covers every occurrence and every inherited level with the same enclosing parameters. Even after q and its wall A are optimized, every paid-reflection component in this bank is uniformly worse than the standard Huber component. Any finite positive combination has payoff at most 1/D_m, and hence benchmark-normalized payoff below 47/50.

At the root alone, (4.2) gives the particularly transparent complete-H_m witness

\[
 \frac{d_{m-1}^2}{(1+S_{m-1})^2D_{m-1}}.
\]

It has the correct order c^(-m), but a fixed loss. Formula (4.5), rather than this order statement, is the decisive reason this recursive independent-bank repair cannot close the target.

## 5. What is closed and what is still missing

Approach 1 supplies a genuine reserve recursion that can be executed at every reverse step on H_m, with all eventual wall labels fixed before forward execution and with exact original-variance accounting. Its tempting invariant is quantitatively insufficient: the entire nested-active-set class at the selected original minimum 0 has exact best payoff 1/D_m. Persistent physical unsaturation is therefore not by itself the missing target-level mechanism. Old auxiliary queries cannot repair this loss.

Approach 2 changes the physical mechanism: it crosses opposite saturated faces and pays the preparation radius. Its full-word witnesses and multivalue product bank are realizable, and (4.5) excludes the entire specified bank on H_m. The failure is the cost of the globally prepared reflected coordinates, rather than an unsupported claim that their wall labels were free.

Neither derivation excludes a **coupled** multivalue K_a trajectory with repeated exits and reentries of active faces. Such a trajectory would have to exploit correlations between reserve coordinates that are absent from (4.3), and it would not obey the orthogonal one-release decomposition used in (3.11). No construction or benchmark-scale original-radius upper bound for that coupled mechanism has been obtained in this invocation.

In particular there is no proved uniform all-word lower bound (1-o(1))/B_n, no full-class asymptotic upper certificate, and no complete candidate for formal audit. The unchanged low-mass Huber argument still covers sum h<=(B_n-1)/2. The exact all-integer-split DP has not been replaced by the diagnostic hierarchy.

## 6. Resource and closeout record

- Assigned native model invocation: 1, gpt-6-astra / ultra.
- Substantive approaches: 2, completed to the scoped outcomes above.
- Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers: 0.
- Formal submissions and verifier calls: 0.
- Additional agents/models, renewals, worker or service starts: 0.
- Access: designated public sources, operating instructions, writing skill, and a bounded memory-registry check; no private worker memory.
- Writes: this PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md in the assigned packet only.
- Budget, Goal, truth stores, OPERATOR, CURRENT_RUN, all other packets, publication, finalization and revocation: untouched.
- Exact token usage is not exposed to this child. The parent owns the authoritative backend/budget receipt; no token count is invented.

Stop after this bounded allocation. Original target ACTIVE / OPEN. All new mathematics UNVERIFIED.

# Handoff: full weighted-prefix finite PEP

**UNVERIFIED DISCOVERY. Goal ACTIVE, original target OPEN.** Allocation `6b000743076cec7f` is CLOSED. This is a fresh grant, separate from the completed analytical allocation `bfc190fee04e9d8d`.

## What ran

One native `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra` invocation, one batch, exactly five precharged whole-process slots of 20 seconds each. One CVXPY/CLARABEL solve per slot; no retry/fallback. Both calibrations passed, then `P_3,P_4,P_5` ran using the same enclosing `m_env=1536` parameters. All statuses were `optimal` / `Solved`, all export sets complete, all warning lists empty.

The full primal Gram PEP maximized

`V_pref=2 f_p-(1-a_last^2)||g_p||^2`

under original radius `||x0||^2<=1`, the full PSD Gram on `(x0,g0,...,g_p)`, and **every** ordered smooth-convex interpolation row on `star,0,...,p`. No rank, source-weight, common-column, tree, diagonal-residual, or support condition was imposed. Every incoming row at the final prefix query was kept.

For production, `eps=1/(4*1536)`, `beta=1-eps`, `c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps)`, `a=beta/c`, `H_0=()`, `H_(r+1)=(H_r,1+a*c^r,H_r)`. Each prefix deletes only the actual final step `1+a`; `D_j=1+2 sum H_j`. Full words, prefixes and matrices are saved.

## Exact calibration premises

The empty-prefix calibration has `a_last=1/2` and exact optimum `4/7`, with certificate

`(4/7)||x0||^2-2f0+(3/4)||g0||^2+2Q_(0,star)=(7/4)||g0-(4/7)x0||^2`.

A global Huber function with cap `4/7`, at `x0=1`, attains it. The second calibration has prefix `(1)`, `a_last=0`, exact optimum `1/4`, and multipliers `(0,star):1/2`, `(1,star):3/2`, `(0,1):1/2`, `(1,0):1`. Its remainder is

`||x0-g0-3g1||^2/4+||g0-g1||^2/4`.

A cap-`1/4` Huber function attains it with `g0=g1=1/4`, `x1=3/4`. `DERIVATION.md` proves global convexity, 1-smoothness, attained minimum, all signed rows and exact attainment before freeze. Charged `Fraction` checks returned zero exact differences; raw floating maximum was `2.7755575615628914e-17`, preserved under the `1e-7` gate.

Saved calibration objective errors: `9.500078501645248e-11`, `1.129529803023388e-11`. Both full gates PASS; exit zero alone was not used as the gate.

## Numerical outcome

| Case | `D_j V_pref` | `D_j q_dual` | Primal minus `17/16` |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `P_3` | 1.079536836925768 | 1.0795368390342717 | +0.017036836925768073 |
| `P_4` | 1.0307918140421866 | 1.0307918147264237 | -0.03170818595781344 |
| `P_5` | 1.0124176155927562 | 1.0124176174000286 | -0.050082384407243774 |

Unscaled primal values: `0.03977600183919927`, `0.015398842868376537`, `0.006209991968555536`. Raw dual-minus-primal gaps: `7.768872350988332e-11`, `1.0221715834868306e-11`, `1.108549016470839e-11`.

The production raw Gram minimum eigenvalues are `-2.2697398248745334e-11`, `-3.39535029749277e-12`, `-3.639481083660732e-12`. Every original dual polynomial also has a tiny negative eigenvalue; full stationarity and all raw spectra are retained.

# Handoff: weighted-prefix analytical grant bfc190fee04e9d8d

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** This fresh native invocation is complete. The parent owns budget closure. No renewal is authorized by this handoff.

## Read first

- `REPORT.md`, all sections, particularly the complete original-row derivation in Sections 2-3 and the uniform bound in Section 6.
- Original `PROBLEM.md` and current `OPERATOR.md`.
- Accepted facts `1252b8823bb5a290` and `931b9653ce0f7201`, at their stated scopes.

TASK hash checked: `83b6f143538f091bfa95725950425ffe8321c0ece8a59925974d81d7255501c3`.

## Exact object

For one enclosing `m>=1536`, keep

`eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, b=1+a`,

`H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum H_m, D=1+2S`.

The actual prefix removes only the final physical step `b`. The sought estimate is

`2 f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2 <= q||x_0||^2`, `q=17/(16D)`.

Smoothness on the actual last update `x_n=x_l-bg_l` gives

`2 f_n <=2 f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2`.

Thus the target would imply the required full-class upper bound, but this invocation supplies no proof of that target.

## Full matrix retained

Let `L_ii=1`, `L_ij=h_j` for `j<i`, and zero above its diagonal. Put

`v=L^T e_l=(h_0,...,h_(l-1),1)`, `T=sum v=S-a`.

For all original queried-prefix rows, use `mu_i=lambda_(i,star)`, `nu_i=lambda_(star,i)`, `N_ij=-lambda_ij` off the diagonal, `N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij`, and `B=sym(NL)`.

The exact coefficient conditions are

`N e=mu`, `N^T e=2e_l+nu`, `mu,nu>=0`, `N_ij<=0` for `i!=j`.

The complete polynomial is PSD exactly when

`K=B-a^2 e_l e_l^T-mu mu^T/(4q) >=0`.

No diagonal, rank, primitive-energy, fixed-common-column, or Huber-null restriction is placed on `K`.

## Completed two-law outcome

Law 1 was `mu=kappa v/T`, `kappa>=2`, permitting any nonnegative `nu` of mass `kappa-2`. It fails the source necessity `mu_0<=4q`.

The second and final law was

`d=b/2`, `w=v-d(e_0-e_l)`, `mu=kappa w/T`, `kappa>=2`.

At `kappa=2`, this repairs the source screen: `0<mu_0=b/T<4q`. Every query-to-query row, incoming minimizer profile of the necessary mass, and full non-diagonal residual remain free.

At the physical root set `k=2^(m-1)-1`, `H=sum_(j<k)h_j`, `R=h_k`, `z=1_[0,k)`, and `mu_L=sum_(j<k)mu_j`. The exact nonnegative original-row cut is

`C=H mu_L-z^T B z+a(mu_k-B_kk)`

`=sum_(j<k)(H-t_j)nu_j`

` +sum_(i>k,j<k)(H-t_j)lambda_ij`

` +sum_(j<k)(H-t_j-a)lambda_(k,j)`

` +a(R-1)sum_(j>k)lambda_(k,j) >=0`.

Full PSD gives `z^T B z>=mu_L^2/(4q)` and `B_kk>=mu_k^2/(4q)`. With `J_0=H-d`, law 2 has `mu_L=kappa J_0/T`, `mu_k=kappa R/T`. The report proves manually and uniformly

`C < -31 H/204000 < -H/7000 <0`

for every `m>=1536` and `kappa>=2`. Therefore this whole source-balanced law is excluded, including every completing skew/remote array, every incoming minimizer profile, and every full PSD residual.

## What is still missing

A different, genuinely free profile `mu`, together with `nu` and the original query array, must be constructed if this selected certificate route is to prove the target. The necessary conditions

`mu_0<=4q`,

`mu_L^2+a mu_k^2 <=4q(H mu_L+a mu_k)`,

`sum mu=2+sum nu`

are not sufficient and do not supply a recursive all-depth law. No such law is obtained here. Changing only the residual matrix cannot repair either frozen profile.

This is not a counterexample to the prefix inequality or to the actual final-iterate bound. Do not promote the obstruction to all source profiles or to the unrestricted target. The full old `8bcf300ff83e2288` report and the dense `A/D` report were read; their fixed columns and diagonal-residual assumptions were not inherited. The choice of the last prefix sample is not presented as novel or as success of the interrupted old worker.

## Resource receipt

# R4 handoff

Status: original exact GD minimax target OPEN. No complete candidate for formal review; all new mathematics is an unverified analytical draft.

Read PROOF_ATTEMPT.md for the self-contained statements and proofs. This was one native analytical invocation, with two substantive proof lines, mathematical PROGRAM0, numerical/array/solver0, formal0, and no additional agent/model. No worker, service, truth-store, Goal, publication or finalization action was taken.

## What was actually constructed

1. A fixed finite-dimensional function pasting two complete projection-type GD histories using one auxiliary coordinate. Equations (4)--(10) specify all points, gradients, values and cross-history rows; (11) pays the original initial radius. The full function is the quadratic-regularized support function of one finite gradient hull. It is chosen once before the run.
2. A fixed convex 1-smooth function realizing a terminal transverse-gradient perturbation of a strictly quadratic prefix. Its necessary and sufficient finite interpolation budget is (18), and (19) is an explicit conjugate construction, including an attained minimum. This is only a realized prefix, not a completed adverse trajectory for the original problem.
3. Within the same second proof line, an actual shared-history pasting with no auxiliary initial radius: a rotated quadratic prefix satisfying (22), followed by the metric cap (23)--(24). Equations (25)--(28) check every old and cross row, give the original-radius payoff, and give explicit scalar parameter conditions. This is a full-word hard function under those conditions, not a universal all-word construction.

## Decisive checks

The first construction cannot itself provide the sharp target uniformly. On two nonzero quadratic Silver children, its generated errors have an all-depth ceiling

\[
\limsup_m B_{2^{m+1}-1} E_m\le2(\sqrt2-1)<1.
\]

This uses the exact benchmark recurrence and the analytic coarse estimate \(B_n\le4(n+1)^\rho\), not numerical recurrence evaluation. The polar-cone check is included: for a nonzero negative clock gradient the minimizer is unique, and at zero negative clock gradient 0 remains the closest minimizer. The ceiling is therefore not an artifact of selecting a distant minimizer.

Scope is crucial: this ceiling concerns the displayed pasted witnesses. It is not a bound on \(W_n\), and it does not exclude a larger proof strategy that selects a global Huber or quadratic witness on these same words. Do not record it as an obstruction to the original conjecture or to every conceivable static gluing.

The second line directly attacked the auxiliary-radius loss by sharing coordinates. An already fixed unit-curvature prefix can force the next bridge and a unit step to land at the minimum, so no unrestricted extension theorem can use an arbitrary such child history. A transverse endpoint perturbation is realizable only within the terminal-scale slack (18); an initial-radius estimate cannot substitute for that slack. Changing the whole shared history with two quadratic modes avoids that endpoint-only restriction. The actual cap in (22)--(28) does complete a compatible whole trajectory without adding radius. Its available prefix test requires a unique spectral sign-changing earlier step larger than the selected bridge, and its moment interval excludes a unit bridge. Arbitrary-word availability, recursive compatibility and the exact benchmark payoff remain unproved.

## What remains missing

There is no construction selecting compatible shared histories for every nonnegative word, no proven arbitrary-word split, and no uniform loss sum producing \(1-o(1)\) with the exact \(C_n/B_n\) recurrence. The optional negative direction did not produce a full-function upper certificate or asymptotic separation. The gap has the strength of the actual target; it is not an unproved technical lemma.

No formal review or automatic renewal is requested by this handoff. The one-call/two-line allocation is finished. Parent owns shared registration and administrative closeout.
